# Hub (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-04)

## How to use this

1. Read this before posting, not after. The reader is a session or the savior about to `POST http://127.0.0.1:4180/pending` (`{text, context, notify:true}`; an update is `{kind:"update", text, context}`; an email draft card goes through `_system/drafts/send_card.py <draft_id>`, which builds `text`, `meta.body` and the `action`). The guard in `POST /pending` logs a hit and downgrades a confirm-what-he-ordered card to `kind:"update"`; it does not make a bad card good.
2. The hub is the only ping surface. Never `tg-bridge/send-to-phone.js` (a different bot, he does not see it); if 4180 is down, say so, do not fall back to another bot. Check `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:4180/unresolved-ids` before posting when a card for the same thing may already exist.
3. When he answers a card with feedback ("this sucks because ...", "I like this", "this again"), the same turn: `python <_system>/drafts/addrule.py --contract hub --rule "the rule" --quote "his exact words" [--soft] [--like] [--item <card id>]`. A change request ("change this on the go") is a build item on the hub workplan, reported in the next system check.
4. The ledger is `_system/HUB-CARD-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract hub --install`): never edit it by hand, the next compile overwrites it.
5. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked by the observer, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### New, not yet mapped

**R1.** Every new event (a message or email he sent, a reply that arrived, a meeting that happened) is checked against the open cards: a card whose proposal that event makes moot is closed or marked outdated the same run, never left open. *"the moment that new event went out (the moment I sent that message and that email, that's an event), it should have been read by the system. Then it should have noticed that all that Hub card is actually outdated now. That's part of the reason why I never go in the hub: there's a bunch of outdated shit."* [ledger H15]

**R2.** His edit of a card's text is the approval of that text: when he edits a draft in the hub or in the CRM review and approves, the edited version is what goes out, his diff is recorded as feedback even without a comment, and the comment box stays available for why. *"I just wanna edit the text directly. I want you to be able to see the changes I made on the text, and make sure that gets essentially approved. It's not just a change request ... It's me editing and then approving what I've edited."* [ledger H16]

**R3.** A card the system itself believes outdated is never shown to him with a 'probably outdated' label: the system closes it (judgement moot, drafts included, the Gmail draft untouched, the whole card kept in hub-outdated-log.jsonl); a mere doubt leaves the card as a normal card with no label. *"you suspected it being outdated... then why show me?"* [ledger H17]

**R4.** Il corpo di un messaggio a una persona sta SEMPRE nel blockquote richiudibile di Telegram, su qualunque canale: email, LinkedIn, Slack, WhatsApp. Si ottiene dando alla card un type fra email-draft, linkedin-draft, slack-draft, wa-draft; un type generico 'card' stampa un muro di testo che sul telefono non si legge. *"why did you not have like the toggle expand UI of telegram that you use for emails because I would like it. It's too much text like this ... let's always have it for messages LinkedIn or email or Slack or even WhatsApp"* [ledger H18]

**R5.** On any review page, a message's channel is shown as a solid badge with the real brand logo, in the colour of that channel's Telegram dot (Gmail red, LinkedIn blue, WhatsApp green, Slack purple), and each channel gets its own filter button. A pale text label is not enough. *"i wanted it more visual to see which is an email/a linkedin/a wa/a slack with the same colors we use on tg chat. why not also a nice logo like in the CRM"* [ledger H19]

**R6.** Every hub card arrives pre-chewed: a What / Who / Why / Recommendation block is the first thing on the card. A producer that knows them sends meta.brief {what, who, why, recommendation}; hub-review derives them otherwise and leaves out what it cannot find. And a card is always one screen: j/k shows the whole card, its head and its verdict buttons, at any length (the middle scrolls). *"The bottleneck usually isn't your yes. It's the time spent loading context to give it. So the first win is approvals that arrive pre-chewed: what, who, why, my recommendation. ... It happens very often that you are not able to see fully the card when I click j or k ... I need you to fix forever the hub cards"* [ledger H22]

**R7.** Every message card (email, LinkedIn, WhatsApp, Slack) is editable on the review page, and a yes sends HIS edited text: a WhatsApp reply card always carries its send action to the chat's real jid, questions or not; answering the questions is editing the text. *"dude, why can i not edit the text there before sending??? like id like to modify and dsend with a commit, but you wont let me modify it"* [ledger H23]

**R8.** He never chooses the rule ledger: whoever files his sentence classifies it by what it is about (RULE-LOOP.md section 2b), using the hub's '-> <ledger>' hint as a first guess, and never asks him which ledger. *"I don't want to have to think whether or not this is a proactive rule or a draft rule. I just want to tell you what's wrong with it, and you being able to understand which one it is."* [ledger H24]

**R9.** A card pings him once. It is never re-sent; it stays open on the hub until he resolves it or an event resolves it (the system recognises from an event that it is settled). *"resending me isnt good, but it shouldn't disappear either until resovled either by me or the system understands it as resolved from an event."* [item: aftw3nmfrwg] [ledger H27]

**R10.** When the system closes cards itself (outdated, duplicate, settled by an event), it does it, then keeps ONE pinned update card listing every card it dropped, each with its evidence and a one-click reverse; it is 'I did this, do you want me to reverse it?', never an approval request. *"can we make it act on them and then "pin" the update card with the drops (not really an approved more goes in updates., more of a "i did this, do you want me to reverse it?""* [ledger H28]

**R11.** Everything the cleaning agent did on its own (to-dos, CRM, hub) is shown in one section of the hub review page, 'Cleaning system update', separate from the other sections and opened with key 4, each action with its evidence and a reverse; it is where he judges how well it cleaned, and the cleaning itself runs automatically. *""cleaning system update" --> a new section, separate from the first 3 and activable with key 4. will have also the same cleaning agent we talked about before for the todo, for the crm, for the hub. the place where we cna judge how well it cleaned. (but it will do so automatically)."* [ledger H29]

**R12.** A research result (an artifact for him to read and iterate on) is never an approval card: it goes to Updates as a told-not-asked card with the link, where he can comment; no yes/no/change. If it came from a to-do, reading it is not a decision: the to-do ticks when he marks it seen or comments. *"what am i supposed to do w this? the artifact is created, i have to check it out no? ... ig its a card for me to give feedback on artifact? but then put it in update or something, not something for me to approve."* [item: ank12g6oqo] [ledger H30]

**R13.** Every card says, on the card itself, exactly what YES does and what NO does (and DONE/SKIP if they differ), in plain words: e.g. 'YES = I open the Google sign-in for alesoda2002 in your Chrome; NO = the contacts sync stays off and the card closes'. A card whose buttons do nothing must not offer them. *"you need to make it clear in the card itself that if I click yes, what will happen if I click yes, and what will happen if I click no? Because now I have no idea. Same goes for that artifact. All these ones that are a bit more ambiguous have no idea what happens if i click y/n"* [item: a9wbt0w4tm0] [ledger H31]

**R14.** A YES is final: it never comes back as a new card asking him for more (e.g. 'give the sentence'). If the producer lacks the text, the system writes it itself from the CRM row, the thread and the proposer's reason, and registers it through the lane. *"why do these come back? i said yes no? yest to all three, out of here."* [ledger H32]

**R15.** SKIP removes the card from his view after the commit (for the day) and from the tally and the commit count; skipped cards never stay on screen blocking the commit button. *"the ones that I skipped, once I click commit, didn't really go away, and they just remained ... I want them gone."* [ledger H33]

## Assumed (still checked by the observer, not in front)

- Anything outbound to another human (email, WhatsApp, Slack, LinkedIn) needs a card: the session writes the draft, the hub sends on his verdict, a session never sends to a third party itself. The one exception is "dsend", or the text quoted verbatim with "send it": that goes now, no card. A Telegram message to HIM is not outbound and needs no card. [ledger H1,H2; memory feedback_message_send_protocol, feedback_approvals_are_pings, feedback_no_headless_fuzzy_send, feedback_dsend_includes_attachment]
- A batch he already instructed IS the yes. Never a card asking him to confirm work he told you to do, or a board commit, or an "N yes". A card after the fact only when something NEW happened that he could not have known: a recipient unreachable, a send that failed, a reply from that person; then the card states the new fact and the choice, never "are you sure". [ledger H3; memory feedback_commit_batch_is_the_yes]
- An update is `kind:"update"`, not an approval: `POST /pending {kind:"update", text, context}` renders with no "Approvo?" line and no yes/no footer. Use it whenever you are informing rather than asking; a question mark on a card that has nothing to decide is a broken card. [ledger H4]
- One card per decision, and it pings ONCE: never re-ping a pending card, never a second card for the same thing, never "did you see the card?". An escalation (something broken you cannot fix cleanly) is the same: fix forward if safe, otherwise a single card or a single Telegram message, never a retry storm. An unanswered card waits in the queue. [ledger H5,H7; memory feedback_approval_ping_once]
- A finished long task (more than about five minutes) or a failure is a PushNotification, never a card: one line, what he would act on first, where to look. The hub is for decisions. [ledger H6; memory feedback_done_notifications]
- A card is a proposal, not state. Before posting a card that calls something "pending" or "not sent", check Sent and the calendar: 4 of 12 items were stale for exactly this reason on 2026-09-20. [ledger SOFT; memory feedback_state_check_sent_and_calendar]
- An auth only he can give is never a card: a laptop session opens the consent page in front of him, in the Chrome profile holding that account, with login_hint and authuser (one account, never a chooser), and catches the result itself, no paste-back. [ledger H58,H59; memory feedback_open_auth_directly, feedback_never_trigger_bare_account_chooser]
- The title is always `🟣 Ping hub`. Never "APPROVA?", never the old doubled `❓❓`. One surface, one name, on email and LinkedIn draft cards too. [ledger H8]
- Top down: the first content line says what he is deciding. `❓ Approvo?` followed by `#N · <the decision>`; `#N` is the daily-resetting handle the hub prepends to `text`, it is how he answers from the phone ("6 dsend", "6 no: ...") and how a later session resolves the answer. He must know from line one exactly what he is approving, without expanding anything. [ledger H9,H12]
- The card is FULL: the decision in `text`, the whole artifact in `context` (for drafts in `meta.body`, the expandable blockquote). A card he has to open the laptop to understand is a broken card; an artifact inlined into `text` gets clamped and looks broken on every surface. [ledger H10; memory feedback_ping_cards_must_be_full]
- Every email card carries its `action`: `{type:"gmail-send-draft", account, drafts:[id]}`, so a yes from the phone actually sends instead of waiting for a session. Draft IDs only, never composed text. Omit the action when the recipient address is unverified, and say so on the card. [ledger H11]
- Telegram only. The laptop popup and the MacroDroid phone popup are deactivated, not deleted (`LAPTOP_POPUP_ENABLED`, `PHONE_POPUP_ENABLED` in server.js). Never "fix" a missed approval by re-enabling them: if a card did not reach him, the fault is in the Telegram leg. [ledger H13; memory feedback_laptop_ping_card_deactivated]
- The emoji carry meaning and are the only ones on a card: `❓` for a decision, `📋` for an update, `✉️` for an email draft, `💬` for a LinkedIn draft. "emojis are useful." No decorative ones. [ledger H14]
- Card body at most about 58 lines; Telegram's hard limit is 4096 characters and the hub truncates `context` at 3500. A long artifact is cut at a paragraph, never mid-sentence, with a line saying where the rest is. [ledger SOFT]
- `silent:true` registers the card with its #N but pushes nothing to Telegram. Use it when the loop lives in another channel, e.g. a Quick Claude panel (`send_card.py --silent`, or a sidecar `origin: quick-claude`). [ledger SOFT]
- hub-review, his layout of 4 Oct 2026: the agent tab is 'System agent' (Needs you, Spot check of 3 random actions he marks right/wrong, Whitelist changes, Review, Health); every line expands with sources, step-by-step and links; card headers stick while scrolling; a left index on every tab (Updates one row per card, To decide grouped by type with type chips, chronological); the look borrows from the CRM review UI. [ledger H45,H46,H47,H48,H49,H50,H52]
- His verdict is executed as written and never re-asked. A yes on a card with an action is already sent by the hub (`/resolve` fires it): report, do not send again, and never test-resolve a card with a real action. "N dsend" = the hub sends draft N; "N no: <correction>" = revise and re-register the same draft (the card is edited in place, same #N, while it is open); a bare no is a rejection, superseding a card is a no WITH text. A `hub-review commit` or `crm-catchup commit` message from him is the same: execute each line, mark it done (`POST 127.0.0.1:4142/api/queue/done`), no card back. [memory reference_approval_hub, reference_hub_review_ui, feedback_commit_batch_is_the_yes]
- What he says on a card about the card is a rule or a change request, filed the same turn with the card id: "this sucks because ..." and "this again" go to the ledger (`addrule.py --contract hub --item <card id>`), "I like this" as a like, a system change into the hub workplan. He never edits a rule file; a sentence he gave and you did not file is a mistake you will make again. [memory feedback_rule_requests_are_binding]
- A number in his reply is resolved against what the hub actually holds (`GET /item/<id>`, today's `#N`), and against the notification log for a bare number on a SODANOtif card, never from memory; before acting on it, check that the resolved card fits the recipient and channel the reply implies, and stop on a mismatch. [memory feedback_telegram_ordinal_message_reference, feedback_read_source_thread_before_acting]
- A system-agent failure goes through the WHOLE fix workflow before anything reaches him: a timing failure (memory full, busy, a service briefly down) stays an open incident the always-on agent waits out and retries itself; a real fault gets the whitelist, then the dedicated Opus fix session within its cap; a card only when that session is genuinely stuck, saying what broke, what it means, what the system does next (who, by when), then the one thing he must do. Every incident report = cause + fix + prevention, each with proof, else it stays open. Never 'no safe fix / needs you', never the done glyph on a failure. [ledger H54,H55,H56,H57,H51]
- A comment on a card must STACK, not replace. He can add a second, separate comment to the same card, including after a commit has already gone out, and each one reaches the session as its own line. The review page offers one textarea per card and no way to add another, so a second thought has to be typed into the next commit's SYSTEM box, where it loses its card. *"I am not able to stack comments top of each other, if, for example, I just sent a comment on an email and realized that I wanted to have another one. I can't really do it, which is upsetting."* [item: aea70dneans] [ledger H20]
- The system-wide feedback box on the review page accepts images. He reviews on the phone, where a screenshot is the fastest way to show what is wrong, and today the input is text only. *"here in the system-wide feedback option input, I'm not able to upload images, which is something I'd like to do"* [ledger H21]
- The feedback processor checks his premise against the data before acting (here the card's '0' was a counting bug: 14 were paused), names the exact person he means from the threads (Luca Diamanti, San Raffaele, PoC call Tue 6 Oct 11:00) and surfaces collateral risks it finds on the way (a scheduled campaign mail to the same hospital). *"i like the thing that the simulation did actually"* [item: aftw3nmfrwg] [ledger H26]
- A yes is final. When the producer has no text for the thing he said yes to (instinct gave no sentence), the system writes the draft itself from the CRM row, the thread and the stated reason and registers it through the lane with the critic; it never answers a yes with a new card asking him for more. *"why do these come back? i said yes no? yes to all three, out of here."* [ledger H34]
- A card's context line must say what the thing IS, enough to decide without opening the link: who, what happened, what changes. A bare title plus a URL is not context. *"THE CONTEXT LINE GIVES ME ZERO INFORMATION ABOUT THIS MEETING. AT LEAST PUT SOME REAL CONTEXT, DONT WANT TO OPEN THE LINK IF I DONT HAVE TO. NOT ASKING FOR MANY LINES, BUT YOU PUT NOTHING"* [item: ae7ba4y3rsw] [ledger H35]
- A time named in a comment on a card is a SCHEDULE, not a note: 'send this tomorrow at 11:04' must hold the send until 11:04. Today the hub fires the send the moment the commit carries a yes and only then shows the comment, so a timed yes sends immediately and the instruction arrives too late. Until the hub can hold a send (verdict yes + send_at), never treat a timed yes as satisfied: say at once that it already went out, and at what time. *"Okay, but send this message tomorrow morning at 11:04. / send it sunday (tomorrow at 11:06)"* [item: ag66cmd14w] [ledger H36]
- SUPERSEDED by H38 (hub #42, Roussel, 4 Oct 2026). Was: a card that proposes opening a CRM row AND a message carries the drafted message itself, so he judges the row and the text at once; his yes sends exactly that text into the lane. Never 'yes = open the row and draft' with no draft on the card. *"Yes, but just like before, just like, actually, the next card, the question is, did you not also draft the email so that I could judge that too?"* [item: afjnzey38ig] [ledger H37]
- PARTLY SUPERSEDED by H39 (hub #41, Vittoria, and #40 Donarini, same commit): the row yes still never sends a message, but the card now SHOWS the draft. Was: a card that proposes opening a CRM row asks for the row ONLY: 'yes = open the row only (no message)'. No draft is written for it and his yes never carries a message; his words on the card go onto the new row. A message to that person is its own later card that shows what it says and why. Supersedes H37. *"The yes is referred to creating a role in the CRM, but not to actually draft a message, because that person has not. We can also draft a message, but I don't see you having thought about what to put in the draft."* [item: afjnzcu5bcw] [ledger H38]
- A card that proposes opening a CRM row for someone the system means to write to SHOWS THE DRAFT already written, so he judges it at once: the person's unsent Gmail draft when one exists (never a second), else one written from the last mail exchange, with one line on why this text. His yes opens the row and puts that draft into the lane as its own email card where his yes is the send (the row yes never sends, H38); his words on the row card go on the row and rewrite the draft. Supersedes the 'no draft' part of H38; restores H37's draft-on-card with the send kept separate. *"Why did you not already draft? In general, why are you not already drafting these emails so I can give you a bit more than you know? You can draft and go back to me. I can actually tell you whether I like a certain draft that I'm seeing, or whether I don't."* [item: afjnzau334] [ledger H39]
- Every card that came back to him after a CHANGE shows the iteration log on the card itself: each earlier comment of his, dated, and the version it produced, so he decides from the record and not from memory. The same on the CRM review item and on every approval surface. *"If, on a similar card, I have sent it back to you and it's come back to me, then I want to be able to see that iteration. I want to be able to see that log in the card itself. In this card, I don't see the comment that I gave you before. Which is actually the problem, because I'm saying the things that I'm saying based on what I remember. I would also like to have it as physical proof."* [item: aff1qfwd82w] [ledger H40]
- A message to a person never appears as a hub card. It lives on the person's CRM review item; when the person is not in the CRM yet, the hub proposes the import (H39) and the message follows into the CRM. The hub keeps a CRM section that only links to the exact CRM card (active, iterating, waiting for his approval), and the Telegram ping (the SodaPing hub) links to that exact CRM card too. *"I don't want to see them in the approval hub anymore if they are already in the CRM ... Actually, I don't want to see them in the approval hub whatsoever ... There will be a section called CRM, and there, I will see the active being iterated, being requesting my approval ... it will just link me again to that same exact card. I want them to be able to do it all on the CRM."* [ledger H41]
- The Telegram surface is called the SodaPing hub (short: Soda Ping); every ping about a CRM item carries a link to that exact CRM card. *"In the Ping hub on Telegram, I want it to be Soda Ping. By the way, let's change it to the SodaPing hub so that I can say it a bit better ... I would like to be able to click on a link on Telegram and be sent to that exact card of the CRM."* [ledger H42]
- A system change he asks for in a comment is executed as a WHITELIST FIX (no approval card) when the request is clear and specific, does not conflict with a filed rule and needs nothing from him (no secret, no login, no send, no deletion, no env or permission change); the hub shows every whitelist fix in its own section 'Whitelist' as a done line he can judge; a request that fails any test is an approval card. His judgement on a whitelist line ('this needed approval', 'this was fine') is filed as a rule in the whitelist ledger and the classifier applies it next time. *"if it's very clear what I want, and it doesn't really conflict much with what we've put already out there, and it doesn't need further approval ... I don't need an approval card. I need it to be treated more as a whitelist fix ... call it a whitelist fix, whitelist update, or whitelist request to make it as a separate section inside my approval hub ... I'm gonna start to give feedback on these things, and those are also gonna be rules ... build a system again of iteration with rules, etc. for this thing"* [ledger H43]
- A send he asked for is never dropped: when a session's own permission blocks a send he wants (WhatsApp, email, Slack), it posts the approval card for that send the same turn (wa-draft card with the wa-send action, or the draft lane for email), and tells him it did; it never stops at 'blocked, tell me what to do'. *"If you cannot send it, you don't just let it die, okay? You create an approval card if you have to, if your permission doesn't allow you, but you don't just let it die. This is a rule. This is an important rule."* [ledger H44]

## Reference examples (his likes, not yet mapped to a rule)

## Procedure

1. Before composing, answer one question: is there a decision he has not made yet? Something he instructed or committed = no card (the batch-is-the-yes rule (Assumed)); information only = an update (the update-not-approval rule (Assumed)); a finished long job or a failure = PushNotification (the done-is-push rule (Assumed)); a message to another human = a card with the draft (the outbound-needs-card rule (Assumed)).
2. Compose top down: `text` = the decision in one line (the hub prepends `#N · `), `context` = the whole artifact; the right glyph (the glyphs rule (Assumed)); an email card carries its `action` with draft ids (the email-action rule (Assumed)).
3. Post once, then STOP. Never re-ping, never a second card for the same thing (the one-card-once rule (Assumed)). The verdict comes back on Telegram only (the telegram-only rule (Assumed)).
4. On his verdict: act as written, do not confirm (the verdict-as-written rule (Assumed)); his feedback on the card is filed the same turn (the feedback-is-a-rule rule (Assumed)).

# Proactive (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-05)

## How to use this

1. The reader is a session about to pick a to-do up that he did not ask for: `python _system/pipeline.py pickup <slug> --auto --note "what I am doing"`, do the work, then `python _system/pipeline.py ready <slug> --auto --summary-file <md>` (or `--card <id>` for a draft card the lane already made). The steps are `_system/PIPELINE-WORKER.md`; this file is WHAT may be picked up and HOW each type of task is done.
2. When he gives feedback on a proactive card or pickup ("this sucks because ...", "I like this", "this again"), the same turn: `python <_system>/drafts/addrule.py --contract proactive --rule "the rule" --quote "his exact words" [--soft] [--like] [--item <task slug>]`. He never picks the ledger: a sentence about how the message is written goes to `email`, about a contact route to `crm` (RULE-LOOP.md 2b).
3. The ledger is `_system/PROACTIVE-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract proactive --install`): never edit it by hand, the next compile overwrites it.
4. The observer is `pipeline.py` itself: every `pickup --auto` and `ready --auto` writes its decisions to `_system/rule-hits.jsonl` (surface `proactive`): R1, R11, R12, and a flag on call and think-about titles (R3, R2). It never blocks; the gate itself does.
5. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### May it be picked up

**R1.** Proactive pickups are live: the 3 Oct pause until the Tuesday reset (H1) was lifted on 4 Oct 20:48 (H24), so on every to-do the system can do it does the work on its own, unattended, on a schedule, until it is ready for his review. The gate is still `AUTO_PAUSED_UNTIL` in `pipeline.py`: `pickup --auto` checks it on every pickup, and a plain `pickup` on a line he did not ask for is the same pickup; a new pause from him is a new date there, never a session's own choice. "So I fully expect it to happen on its own." *e.g.* `pipeline.py pickup compare-us-number-providers --auto` on Mon 5 Oct: the gate is open (hit `H1 ok`), the to-do is picked up, done, and handed back with `ready --auto` [ledger H1,H24]

**R2.** A to-do that is a decision or a think-about for him ('think about what to ask X', 'decide the message') is his reminder: never picked up, nothing drafted, no one-time answer. "Don't even create this onetime answer because then you create too much." *e.g.* `think about what to ask Mulvaney`: left alone, no pickup, no draft, no card [ledger H14]

**R3.** A call he will make is never drafted or prepared as a message: nothing to do. The person may go on the CRM Call page, and then that step is tied both ways to its to-do line: done in the CRM = ticked on the daily page, and vice versa. "whenever there's a call, nothing." *e.g.* `call Zubiate about the pilot`: no draft; at most `pipeline.py call call-zubiate-about-the-pilot --name "Christopher Zubiate"`, which puts him on the Call page linked to the line [ledger H5]

**R4.** A question he will settle in person (at a call, with the lawyer, on site) is not researched: it goes as a `[ ]` checkbox on that meeting's Notion page. *e.g.* `find out if the UG can hold the IP`, and he sees the lawyer on Thursday: no research, `[ ] can the UG hold the IP?` on the lawyer meeting's Notion page [ledger H7]

### How a type of task is done

**R5.** Research goes into a claude.ai artifact he can iterate on, and it opens with an index that links each section so he can jump through it. The hub card carries only the link to the artifact, never the content. "You don't put the whole thing; you just put a link to the artifact." *e.g.* card `To-do done, your review: compare US number providers` whose `context` is one line and the artifact link; the artifact starts with `Index: Options · What forums say · Why these · Left out` [ledger H4,H10]

**R6.** Research adds a forum layer: real discussions (Reddit, forums, community threads) on what works best, cited, on top of the general search; AI-written top-10 lists are never the only source. *e.g.* a section `What forums say` quoting two r/VOIP threads and a Hacker News comment on porting a number, each linked, next to the vendor pages [ledger H12]

**R7.** A comparison table gives a link for every option (the product's own page) in the table itself, and research always says why each option is in and why notable alternatives were left out. "Why you chose to include an option and not another" *e.g.* each row of the US-number table links the provider's own pricing page; under it, one line per notable option left out, each with its reason [ledger H11,H13]

**R8.** An application (accelerator, fund, grant) gets its own Notion page under Incubators/Programs; answers are reworked from the YC application (YC Tundra Health V3 raw), and under each answer a toggle says which part of the YC application it came from and how it was adapted. *e.g.* `apply to the EWOR fellowship`: a page under Incubators/Programs, each answer followed by a toggle `From YC: "What is your company going to make?", cut to 100 words, hospital ERP angle first` [ledger H6]

**R9.** To identify a person named in a to-do, search the TEXT of his recent messages (WhatsApp, email) for the name, read the conversation, and infer who they are and where things stand. Ask him only after that search. *e.g.* `meet Ines`: a WhatsApp search for `Ines` finds the chat where he asked her to meet and called her a founder in the same field; that is the context, no question to him [ledger H16]

**R10.** When a contact route is unclear, a fresh agent with no context researches it blind; if its answer differs from the session's, flag it to him. The 3 Oct Zubiate test is the reference: the blind agent found the assistant route the session had missed. *e.g.* the blind agent finds Zubiate's assistant, a route the session did not have: the card says `blind check differs: assistant route found`, with both answers [ledger H17]

### The hand-back

**R11.** A proactively done to-do is handed back as ONE hub card linked from the to-do line: the orange circle right after the checkbox is the link to that card. Approving the card ticks the to-do automatically; he never ticks it twice. `pipeline.py ready --auto` does all of it; a second card for the same line is a broken hand-back. *e.g.* `- [ ] [🟠](http://100.85.52.84:4142/#c<card id>) compare US number providers` on the daily page; he approves the card, `pipeline.py sync` ticks the line [ledger H2]

**R12.** Every card the system produced on its own carries `meta.auto=true`, so the hub files it under Automatic; a card a session made because he asked stays under Asked by me. A new producer that runs unasked sets `meta.auto=true`. "Just so that I can quickly see the ones that matter" *e.g.* `ready compare-us-number-providers --auto` posts `meta: {task: {...}, auto: true}`; a draft he asked for in a session posts no `auto` [ledger H3]

### New, not yet mapped

**R13.** A card that proposes reaching out to someone carries the DRAFT, not just the proposal: if you judge a message is warranted, run the whole draft lane and bring the draft with the card so he can judge the text too. His no then teaches you not to draft that shape next time, which is cheaper than the round trip. *"Why did you not already draft? In general, why are you not already drafting these emails so I can give you a bit more... As a rule of thumb, run the whole draft system if you think that a draft is necessary. Then what you will find out is maybe sometimes I tell you, "No, you shouldn't have drafted it.""* [item: afjnzau334] [ledger H19]

**R14.** Instinct is not a research engine: it is the proactive agent that notices and proposes. Research tasks (finding a person, a company, a legal answer) are not given to it and its research output is not compared against mine as if it were. *"we're not using instict for these researches.. only as a proactive agent. we'll figure out how"* [item: anq66nj84o] [ledger H20]

**R15.** Finished to-do work (research artifacts, a Notion page such as the PearX application, the Claude-vs-instinct comparisons) is never a decision card: it goes to the hub's Updates tab (type todo-review, meta.trial or meta.research), where he can comment on any line. The artifact or page itself carries a link back to that single hub item (http://100.85.52.84:4142/#c<card id>), and the hub item links to the artifact. Only something he actually has to approve (a send, a spend, a yes/no) goes to To decide. *"You don't deserve to be a card. You need to be like I've done for the research artifact ... in the artifact itself, you should have a link that links back to the single card of the approval hub. But then in the approval hub, it shouldn't go into the side category, it should go in some other weird update place where I can just give it feedback if I want. This is for every research, I think it's for most tasks, actually. Notion, that Notion page for PearX, is sort of a task, not really something I have to decide on."* [ledger H21]

His yes creates them; he never has to ask for them. Off leash (his word 4 Oct 20:55): propose freely, he corrects the card and the correction becomes a rule. ## Procedure

1. Before picking: is the gate open (R1)? Is the line his own reminder (R2), a call (R3) or a question he settles in person (R4)? Then leave it. Otherwise say concretely how each person would be reached (the say-how-reached rule (Assumed)).
2. While doing it: research is an artifact (R5) with a forum layer (R6) and reasons (R7); an application is a Notion page (R8); a person is searched in his messages first (R9), a blind check only when the route is still unclear (R10); a missing fact is a question AND the work goes on (the ask-and-keep-going rule (Assumed)).
3. Hand back with `ready --auto`: one card on the to-do line (R11), marked auto (R12), artifacts published silently (the silent-artifacts rule (Assumed)). Then stop: approving the card ticks the to-do, nothing else to do.

# Notif (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-04)

## How to use this

1. The reader is the SODANOtif classifier: `buildSystemPrompt` in the box's `sodanotif/daemon.js` appends this file (env SODANOTIF_NOTIF_SKILL, default ~/.claude/skills/notif/SKILL.md) after the /triage rules; these rules win where they conflict.
2. His sentence on a notification (a Telegram reply `notif: ...` or a quote-reply, or a line of the Notifications tab of hub-review) is queued as kind `notif-feedback` and filed by `drafts/ledger_verdict.py` (like, confirmation, rule or case) through `addrule.py --contract notif`, then this skill is recompiled.
3. The ledger is `_system/NOTIF-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract notif --install`): never edit it by hand.
4. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### What a card shows

**R1.** Every line of a card names who wrote it (and the group, for a group) before the snippet. *e.g.* "Caleb: are you taking over the hotel in Orlando?" then "Rocco /GG SMOM Torino: ...", never a bare snippet [ledger H1]

### What counts as missed

**R2.** A conversation is not missed when he wrote later in the same chat: scan the store for a fromMe message after it on the same jid. The same holds on every channel (WhatsApp fromMe, Gmail SENT in the thread, LinkedIn fromMe, Slack his message): a pushed card whose person he answered after it is edited in Telegram into one line '✓ SODANOtif · <channel> · <name>: answered by you at HH:MM, closed', with the evidence logged and a rule hit. *e.g.* Caleb at 07:10, his reply at 07:30: closed, not on the missed list; only Danit's 07:44 was really unanswered [ledger H2,H6]

**R3.** Never show him a list of missed things that was not verified: one false positive makes him re-check the whole card by hand. *e.g.* "I have answered to all these ... the only one I didn't answer to is Danit" [ledger H3]

### What never becomes a card

**R4.** An anomaly a machine can repair is handed to a live session (laptop first, the box as the safety net), never a card asking him to act. *e.g.* a LinkedIn session that expired: the laptop re-logs it; no card "a login window opened, sign in" [ledger H5]

**R5.** stale_hours: 12. No card ever shows a message written more than 12 hours ago (the message's own time, not when a poller found it; a day-only LinkedIn label counts as the end of that day), nor one where he wrote last. The box daemon reads the number from the ledger row (notif-collapse.js staleHours): change it there, never in code. *e.g.* the Jonathan Crowe message of 29 Sep re-emitted on 4 Oct: never a card [ledger H7]

## Assumed (still checked by the observer, not in front)

- In the recap, missed means never pushed as a single card (the join with notification-log.jsonl stays); still suppress what he already answered and label it so it does not read as unanswered. [ledger H4]

## Procedure

1. Every line says who wrote it, and the group when it is a group (R1).
2. Before calling anything missed or unanswered, check that he did not write later in that chat (R2); a false positive costs more than a missed line (R3).
3. Missed means never shown as a single card; label it that way (the missed-label rule (Assumed)).
4. A fault a machine can repair goes to a live session, never to him as a card (R4).

# Whitelist (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-04)

## How to use this

1. The reader is the classifier, `python <_system>/drafts/whitelist_judge.py judge --request "his sentence" --surfaces crm,hub` (one Opus call; this file plus the ledgers of the surfaces it touches are its prompt), and the whitelist job of `job_runner.py` that makes the change.
2. His sentence on a line of the hub's Whitelist section ("this needed approval", "this was fine", "never touch X on your own") is filed by `whitelist_judge.py verdict --quote "his words" --item <card id> --line "the done line"`: a sentence an existing rule already says is a confirmation (`rule-confirmations.jsonl`), otherwise a new row through `addrule.py --contract whitelist`, then this skill is recompiled.
3. The ledger is `_system/WHITELIST-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract whitelist --install`): never edit it by hand, the next compile overwrites it.
4. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### Is it a whitelist fix

**R1.** A system change he asks for is made at once, with no approval card, only when the request is clear and specific, conflicts with no filed rule (read the ledgers of the surfaces it touches) and needs nothing from him. Otherwise it is an approval card. *e.g.* "put the CRM review count in the hub header" on a hub comment: clear, no rule against it, nothing from him: whitelist fix. "make the CRM smarter": vague: approval card. "show me drafts as hub cards again": conflicts with HUB H41: approval card [ledger H1]

**R2.** Never a whitelist fix when the change touches a secret or token, an env file, a permission or allowlist, a send to anyone, a deletion of data or files, a login, or a synced repo on the box by hand. Hard-coded (whitelist_judge.NEVER), re-checked on the files of the commit. *e.g.* "add the Notion token to .env.local so the reader works": env file and secret: approval card, whatever the model says [ledger H2; memory feedback_never_touch_synced_repo_on_box_by_hand]

### How it is reported

**R3.** A whitelist fix is committed with a message naming his sentence; the done line (kind update, meta.section whitelist, text "whitelist fix: <one line>") carries his sentence verbatim, the files and the commit, the test or check that proves it, and the undo command. Never reported done without the commit. *e.g.* `whitelist fix: the hub header shows the CRM review count`; context: his words, `review-api.js, commit a1b2c3d (~/.medtech-crm)`, proof `node --test tests/header.test.js: 3 pass`, undo `git -C ~/.medtech-crm revert a1b2c3d` [ledger H3]

## Assumed (still checked by the observer, not in front)

- When in doubt between whitelist and approval, it is an approval card: a wrong approval card costs one tap, a wrong whitelist fix costs his trust. [ledger none]

## Procedure

1. The never list first, in code: a secret, an env file, a permission, a send, a deletion, a login, a synced repo on the box by hand = approval card (R2).
2. Then the three tests: clear and specific, no conflict with a filed rule, nothing needed from him (R1); in doubt, an approval card (the in-doubt rule (Assumed)).
3. A whitelist fix is reported as ONE done line with the commit, the proof and the undo (R3).
The ledger (his rules) 3 rows of WHITELIST-CONTRACT.md, verbatim

A request that fails any of the three tests is an approval card. *"if it's very clear what I want, and it doesn't really conflict much with what we've put already out there, and it doesn't need further approval ... I don't need an approval card. I need it to be treated more as a whitelist fix"*his ruling 2026-10-04 (HUB H43) · HARD

# Board (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-04)

## How to use this

1. The reader is the research worker of the daily campaign: `gtm-eng/daily-campaign/workers.js` appends this file (env BOARD_SKILL, default ~/.claude/skills/board/SKILL.md) after worker-prompt.md; a rule here about who is picked wins over the prompt where they conflict. A session that changes board-server.js or board-page.html reads it first.
2. His sentence on a board (the comment box a / s open under a card, or a category note) is posted by board-server.js to hub-review `POST /api/feedback {surface: gtm-board}`, queued as kind `feedback`, and filed by `drafts/ledger_verdict.py` (like, confirmation, rule or case) through `addrule.py --contract board`, then this skill is recompiled. A sentence about the wording of a message goes to the email ledger instead.
3. The ledger is `_system/BOARD-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract board --install`): never edit it by hand.
4. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### How a board is reviewed

**R1.** Every board is keyboard-drivable with a visible legend: a ready, s skip (both advance), e edit, Esc out of the box. *e.g.* 60 people on peer-60: a mouse trip per card is the whole cost of the review [ledger H1; memory feedback_review_pages_need_keyboard_shortcuts]

**R2.** j goes BACK and k goes FORWARD (arrows natural); the legend says "j back / k fwd". *e.g.* the vim default (j down) works against his hands and costs a mis-decision [ledger H2; memory feedback_jk_shortcuts_j_is_back]

**R3.** The person's name, the message and the channel details are open on the card; only secondary material collapses. *e.g.* "the contact name is the most important thing, cant have it hidden for me to click" [ledger H3; memory feedback_never_collapse_what_he_must_read]

### What a commit means

**R4.** A committed batch IS the yes: no card to confirm it; a card after a commit only for a fact he could not know at commit time. *e.g.* estonia, card #15 "5 committed, waiting to be sent": zero new information, never again [ledger H4; memory feedback_commit_batch_is_the_yes]

## Assumed (still checked by the observer, not in front)


## Procedure

1. Who goes on a board follows the rules in the first group; a new rule from his skip comments lands there first.
2. The page is driven from the keyboard (R1, R2) and shows what he must read open (R3).
3. A commit is the yes (R4): the runner sends and reports facts, never asks again.

H2On every board j moves BACK and k moves FORWARD (arrows keep their natural meaning) and the legend says 'j back / k fwd'. *"to change for everytime we have this text shortcuts (even in gtm boards if needed: j is back and k is fwd, not other way around)"*his ruling 2026-09-19 01:08 (memory feedback_jk_shortcuts_j_is_back) · HARD

# Cleaning (compiled from the ledger, 2026-10-04)

## How to use this

1. The reader is the periodic sweep's judge (`hub_outdated.py judge`, path B of the cleaning system): this file is put in front of every judge call; the janitor and path A follow the same rules.
2. His sentence on a line of the hub-review Cleaning tab is queued as kind `feedback`, surface `cleaning`, and filed by `drafts/ledger_verdict.py` (like, confirmation, rule or case) through `addrule.py --contract cleaning`, then this skill is recompiled. A `rule:` about cleanup typed on the daily page or in Quick Claude lands here too.
3. The ledger is `_system/CLEANING-CONTRACT.md`, his words, dated, append-only. This file is its compile (`python _system/drafts/compile_skill.py --contract cleaning --install`): never edit it by hand.
4. In front: what the hit counts say is still needed. Assumed: still checked, back in front the moment it fires.

## The rules in front

### What is ticked and removed

**R1.** Tick a sub-item the moment evidence exists (an email reply, a calendar event) and move a date the evidence moved; a tick backed by evidence needs no approval. *e.g.* the notary answered on the share capital: the sub-item is ticked; the appointment moved to Mon 5 Oct 16:00 on the calendar: the date follows [ledger H1]

**R2.** Done and approved items leave the visible list; the history stays in the file. *e.g.* "Already bought the laptop": that sub-item is gone from the page, logged in the task file [ledger H2]

### What lands at all

**R3.** A to-do from a deadline scrape lands on Today only after a check against his history: done before, bar not met, or not yet = it does not land. *e.g.* Accelerator #21 ("we've done it already in the past"), EWOR ("no point yet in applying"): never on Today [ledger H3]

**R4.** A loose item that belongs to an existing umbrella task is proposed under that umbrella. *e.g.* phone plans inside US trip prep; the CDTM WA message inside Healthcare ecosystem push [ledger H4]

**R5.** One status lives in one place: a status change updates every surface that shows it. *e.g.* EWOR: Today and the Notion row change together, never "apply" on one and "To apply" on the other [ledger H5]

### How an item reads

**R6.** A to-do naming a person says who they are and why on its own line (company, how he knows them). *e.g.* "Recontact Alexis Hernot (CEO, asked to be recontacted in October after the AP-HP question)", never a bare name [ledger H6]

**R7.** A vague to-do is reworded into what he actually has to do. *e.g.* "think about what to ask CDTM Health Care" instead of "CDTM Health Care" [ledger H7]

## Assumed (still checked by the observer, not in front)


## Procedure

1. Evidence first: an email reply or a calendar event ticks the item and moves its date, no approval (R1).
2. Done and approved items leave the visible list, the file keeps the history (R2).
3. A scraped deadline is checked against his history before it lands (R3); a loose item goes under its umbrella (R4); one status, one place (R5).
4. A line names who and why (R6) and says what he actually has to do (R7).

H2Register and tone: tu or Lei, for him AND for them, with the quoted words that show it ("mi sente", "mi dice lei", "ti mando"); first name or title; how warm or formal the call was. The follow-up uses exactly this register, never a default.his ruling 2026-09-29 (email H89) · HARD
H3People: everyone named (name, role, organisation) and every email, phone or domain spoken. A dictated address is rebuilt step by step: the transcript is machine-translated, so a name can come out translated ("diamonds point luca" = "diamanti.luca"), and "the same format as your email" means the counterpart's own domain. Every rebuilt address is SMTP-checked before it is used.his ruling 2026-09-29: "lui mi disse l'email e' diamanti.luca e poi ... dominio dopo la chiocciola che ho io" · HARD
H4Dates, times and slots said, as said and as dates.his ruling 2026-09-29 · HARD
H5What we promised to deliver (a demo, a document, numbers, an intro), in their words.his ruling 2026-09-29 (email H87: never ignore a deliverable) · HARD
H6The words they used for their own situation and problem, verbatim, so the follow-up can mirror them.his ruling 2026-09-29 · HARD
H7What was left open, and what they asked of us.his ruling 2026-09-29 · HARD
H8Anything sensitive: competitors or vendors named, things not to repeat in writing, internal politics.his ruling 2026-09-29 · HARD

---
name: trippy
description: THE CURRENT TRIPPY. Deep multi-modal travel search (flights, trains, buses, ferries, rideshare) with learned effort allocation — persistent per-trip boards served by a local webapp, pairwise context duels that feed a big search, and a cross-trip preference wiki. Use for ANY travel-search request the user describes in natural language (origin → destination, dates, time/price constraints) or when they invoke /trippy. Triggers - "trippy", "/trippy", "find me a way from X to Y", "cheapest way to Z", "how do I get to <city> on <date>", "flights/trains/buses from X to Y", "best way from X to Y", "munich to <town>", "night train to <city>", "context duel", "run the big search", "trip board". The superseded one-shot sweep is /trippy-old — do not use it for new work.
