# trippy — deep travel search with learned effort allocation

> **Naming (locked 2026-08-03).** This skill *is* `/trippy`, the up-to-date one. It was
> previously called `trippy-v2`. The older one-shot sweep is now `/trippy-old` and is kept
> for reference only. If you see `trippy-v2` anywhere in code, memory, or an old transcript,
> it means THIS skill.

**Fully separate system** from `/trippy-old`, so either can be killed without touching the
other. All code in `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\v2\` (standalone, zero imports
from the old `sites/` adapters; only the primed `profile-chrome\` cookie state is shared —
data, not code). Design log + precedents: memory `project_trippy_v2.md`.
Built and validated end-to-end on the Munich→Sarzana trip, 2026-06-10/11.

## Where it runs: box does search, laptop does checkout (2026-09-06)

The code now lives in a PRIVATE repo, `https://github.com/alesod23/travel-search` (branch
`master`), synced both ways: the laptop working copy is `travel-search\v2\`, the VPS box clones
the same repo and pushes back every 5 min. The public `alesod23/trippy-public` is a FROZEN
showcase -- never push to it, and never put trip data in it. Trip data (`state/ grids/ specs/
app/trips/ preferences.json rule-feedback.jsonl`) IS committed, because the box needs it to
continue boards; browser profiles, `.env`, logs and `results/` never are.

- **The box does search + boards + duels.** It is reachable from the phone over the tailnet.
  `app/server.js` binds `process.env.TRIPPY_HOST`, defaulting to `127.0.0.1` -- do not bind
  `0.0.0.0`; the box serves the phone on its tailnet interface IP, deliberately not the public one.
- **CHECKOUT STAYS ON THE LAPTOP.** It needs a visible browser driving airline funnels; the box
  cannot do that and never will.
- **The VPS does NOT need a primed Chrome profile to search, and setting one up would NOT help.**
  The blocker from the datacenter is the IP, not the missing browser: momondo, Kayak, Skyscanner,
  flightsfrom and lufthansa.com all 403 the box regardless. Google Flights does not -- which is
  why `gflights.py` is the box's flight engine. Do not burn time priming a profile on the VPS.
- **`gflights.py` output encoding (fixed 2026-09-06, commit 97e8e4b).** Its CLI reconfigures
  stdout to UTF-8 with `errors="replace"` inside `main()`, so a bare run on the Windows console no
  longer dies on a non-cp1252 layover name (Keflavik, Zurich...). Before that fix it raised
  UnicodeEncodeError out of `print()` and exited 1, AFTER the fetch and parse had already
  succeeded. The reconfigure is deliberately NOT at module level: importing `query()` / `sweep()`
  as a library leaves the caller's stdout alone. Consequence worth knowing: calling `show()`
  directly BYPASSES it and you will see mojibake, which is not the bug coming back.

## File map

```
travel-search\v2\
  gflights.py        PREFERRED flight engine (added 2026-09-06). Google Flights, PURE HTTP:
                     no browser, no API key, no auth -- it server-renders full
                     itineraries to a plain curl when you pass a base64 `tfs`
                     protobuf param. Returns price, stops, carrier, local dep/arr
                     times WITH dates, total duration, layover airport + duration.
                     `--max-stops N` is server-side and INCLUSIVE -- N means "at
                     most N", so `--max-stops 1` returns nonstops too. Verified on
                     two IPs, MUC->SFO 2026-09-30: 0 -> 2 options {0}; 1 -> 10
                     options {0,1}; 2 -> 11 options {0,1,2}; unfiltered -> 11
                     {0,1,2}. Neither momondo trap can recur here: a gflights sweep
                     is N SINGLE-route queries, not one comma-joined multi-airport
                     query, so there is no query shape for the stops filter to
                     silently die on, and no top-50 DOM window. Measured: 20
                     (from,to,date) cells -> 187 options in 3.3s with 8 workers.
                     GOTCHA: round-trip aria-labels read "From N euros round trip
                     total." where one-ways read "From N euros." -- miss that and
                     RT silently parses to ZERO options.
  gflights_mc.py     MULTI-CITY / OPEN-JAW engine (added 2026-09-25, us-tour-2026-10).
                     Google does NOT server-render multi-city results (a plain GET
                     returns the form, zero itineraries), so this renders the same
                     `tfs` (trip type 3) in headless system Chrome and parses the
                     leg-1 aria-labels; each leg-1 price is the TOTAL ticket price.
                     `sweep --spec cells.json --out res.json --workers 3`, cells =
                     [{"legs":[[F,T,date],...], "trip":3|1}]. ~20 s per search.
                     GOTCHA (measured): gflights.py returned 0 options on 108/180
                     long (33-day) ROUND-TRIP cells because Google client-rendered
                     them too; the same cells via gflights_mc `"trip":1` returned
                     options for 67/105. ALWAYS re-run 0-option RT cells through
                     gflights_mc before calling a date pair empty.
                     Google caps multi-city at 6 flights.
  momondo2.py        flight engine: multi-airport RT, both sorts, line-walking parser
  ground2.py         ground engine (bus-first): PURE HTTP, no browser. Primitives:
                     reachable <city> = every DIRECT FlixBus connection ("what is
                     directly connected to point B" - cms/cities/<uuid>/reachable);
                     sweep = parallel (from,to,date) lattice pricing via FlixBus
                     search API (date format DD.MM.YYYY!). Mixed bus+train chains
                     are assembled by Claude from lattice cells + train last-mile;
                     cards ship preBuilt segments (builder skips enrichment).
                     Rome2Rio = discovery-only fallback (no live prices).
                     Trip-class pattern: run pure-land as a SEPARATE pass after
                     flights, own category "Pure land (bus + train)".
  flatten.py         results -> compact one-line-per-option for direct LLM ranking
  complete.py        rough D2D completion + Pareto + GC (bulk triage at scale)
  bahn_probe.py      DB price probe (currently bot-blocked, see anti-bot map)
  checkout_f98.py    checkout-tab driver (template for per-trip checkout scripts)
  grids\<slug>.json  grid specs   results\<run-id>\  raw search output
  app\
    server.js        THE PERSISTENT WEBAPP (port 4126, zero deps) — never rebuild per trip
    build_duel_data.py <slug>   enrich options.json -> D2D segment timelines + rule engine
    watch_requests.js <slug> [min]  exits when a request lands -> wakes Claude
    trips\<slug>\    options.json duels.jsonl requests.jsonl status.json meta.json
```

## Pipeline for a NEW trip (the validated loop)

-1. **Step -1 — PREFLIGHT TOOL MAP + LIVE TEST (user-locked 2026-07-19, ALWAYS for a new trip).**
   BEFORE any sweep, when a new trip search arrives:
   (a) **MAP** the tools the trip's geography/modes will need: momondo2 (flights), carrier APIs
   (Ryanair timtbl/farfnd, etc.), LeFrecce BFF (IT rail), bahn.de (DE rail — Comet expected),
   SNCF (FR — Comet), FlixBus/ground2, ferries, plus the airline funnels checkout will later need.
   (b) **TEST each with a ~30s live probe** — one cheap real query per tool: returns data? profile
   primed? cookie wall? login needed? bot-blocked?
   (c) **REPORT one compact checklist to the user up front**: ✓ working · ⚠ needs your input
   (login / accept cookies / captcha — ask for it NOW) · ✗ blocked (name the fallback). The point:
   get all human input out of the way in the first minutes, then the user can leave Claude to work
   long and uninterrupted. Never discover a blocked tool hours into the search.
   Probe results go into the trip's `coverage.md` as a "Tools" table so later sessions inherit the map.
   **Corollary (same lock): Pass 1 must itself be the FULL search** — complete Step-0 bidirectional
   scan + real prices wherever an API allows (LeFrecce, Ryanair, FlixBus). The first board the user
   sees is already a good search; duels/big-search only ENRICH it with preference context. A thin
   Pass-1 that defers the real work to the big search is a defect ("same four options" duel fatigue).

0. **Step 0 — BIDIRECTIONAL COINCIDENCE (added 2026-06-14 after missing the obvious).**
   The fundamental step, must be COMPLETE. Enumerate EVERY direct leg (bus+train+lowcost
   flight) FROM point A toward the B-region, AND EVERY direct leg INTO point B + near-hubs
   (Sarzana+Pisa+La Spezia). Then INTERSECT: a city reachable directly from A AND by a
   SHORT final leg (≤~3h) to B is a candidate connection A→hub→B. (Miss that triggered
   this: swept buses to Milan/Pisa/Genoa but OMITTED Florence + never intersected → missed
   MUC→Florence bus + Florence→Sarzana train, the user's hand-found option.)
   - `ground2.py reachable <city>` (FlixBus directs) for A AND each B-anchor → intersect
     (`state/coincidence.py` template). Layer train directs (FlixBus blind): Munich↔
     Bologna/Verona EC; Florence/Pisa/La Spezia/Genoa→Sarzana regional.
   - GEOGRAPHIC FILTER mandatory: keep only hubs whose final leg to B ≤~3h (else the
     intersection returns Amsterdam/Oslo). Real set: Florence, Bologna, Pisa, Genoa, Milan,
     La Spezia, Viareggio, Verona, Parma.
   - Coincidence → connection: if the plain A→B aggregator search doesn't already cover the
     hub, it's NEW — assemble from single legs with real times + change buffers + transit.
   - BENCHMARK first (easy A→B 1-layover price) = wiggle room: don't chase a hand-assembled
     combo that barely beats the easy trip. Use it to know when to STOP.
   - Two flights = avoid unless SUPER cheap + easy. Prefer flight+ground or pure-ground.
1. **Step 1 — direct-route matrix FIRST, proximity LAST.** Enumerate candidate airports on
   BOTH sides *including far hubs reachable by cheap rail* (D-Ticket regional = free;
   advance-Sparpreis ICE ≈ €30 makes BER a valid Munich departure). Build the direct-route
   matrix via carrier APIs, then score cells = direct-exists × fare-tier × last-mile-under-rules
   × ground access. NEVER rank by last-mile proximity alone (Sarzana lesson: PSA 1h30/0 nonstops
   produced 0 survivors; FLR 3h/nonstops owns the podium; BER wasn't even modeled).
   - Ryanair ground truth, free, no auth:
     `ryanair.com/api/timtbl/3/schedules/<F>/<T>/years/<Y>/months/<M>` (exact times/days)
     `ryanair.com/api/farfnd/v4/oneWayFares/<F>/<T>/cheapestPerDay?outboundMonthOfDate=...`
   - **Verify EVERY route-existence claim against the carrier API** — FlightsFrom/recon-agent
     route claims go stale (the "FMM→PSA 3×/week" route did not exist).
2. **Aggregator pull** (momondo2): multi-airport origins in ONE query (`MUC,FMM,SZG-PSA`
   verified working), round-trip date grid, both sorts.
   `python momondo2.py grid --spec grids\<slug>.json --workers 3` (background, offscreen).
   - **⚠ momondo VIRTUAL-SCROLLS: only ~50 cards stay in the DOM no matter how far you scroll**
     (cards swap as you scroll; `count()` stays ~50). So you ONLY ever see momondo's top ~50 by
     the chosen sort. `target_cards` past ~50 does NOT help — bumping it is a no-op against the
     virtual-scroll cap. **The only way to see options below rank ~50 is to FILTER server-side.**
   - **THE STOPS FILTER IS THE FIX — but it ONLY works on SINGLE-route queries.** momondo's
     path filter `/{date}/stops=-1` (= max 1 layover; `stops=0` = nonstop) is **silently IGNORED
     when origin OR dest is multi-airport** (the commas in `ORD,MKE,MSN-MIL` break the path
     grammar → unfiltered results, but it still "looks" like it ran). Use `momondo2.py` flags
     `--max-stops 1` (+ grid-level `"max_stops":1`) and query **single origin → single dest**
     (`ORD-MXP`, not `ORD,MKE,MSN-MIL`). Verified 2026-06-15: `ORD-MXP stops=-1` → 42×1-stop, and
     surfaced the user's hand-found JetBlue €342 at #1 that the old 45-card multi-origin sweep had
     MISSED entirely. **WHENEVER the trip has a "max N layovers" rule (most short trips do), sweep
     single-route per (origin,dest,date) with `--max-stops N`** — do NOT rely on the unfiltered
     multi-airport sweep + post-filter, because the good 1-stops are below momondo's top-50 window
     and you will never capture them. The multi-airport metro query is fine ONLY for an unfiltered
     "what carriers/shapes exist" scan, never for finding the cheapest clean ≤N-stop option.
3. **Flatten + rank**: `flatten.py` → compact lines → **LLM reads ALL of them directly**.
   Measured economics: 345 options ≈ 50k tokens ≈ $1, single pass — fine. ~70% were
   recombinations of ~12 leg shapes, so a deterministic shape-dedup cuts cost 8×. Crossover
   to needing the GC formula pre-filter ≈ ~1,000 raw options. `complete.py` exists for that scale.
4. **Hard rules + categories.** Apply the user's standing rules (below) in
   `build_duel_data.py`; killed options stay VISIBLE (dimmed, with reason) — never silently
   dropped. Assign each option a **semantic category** (nonstop anchor / far-origin ground
   hack / cheap self-transfer / legacy 1-stop / other-arrival) — flat top-10 lists bury whole
   strategies; per-category representation is mandatory (user demand after Berlin vanished).
5. **Webapp phase**: create `app\trips\<slug>\options.json` (curated cards: fare, legs,
   badges, why, breakdown), run `build_duel_data.py <slug>`, serve. User does **~5-10 duels
   with rich free-text comments** — these surface constraints that are UNDERIVABLE from data
   (tonight: "pickup only after working hours" inverted the champion twice; "8h layover never";
   "arriving 21:00 might be too late, but a café bridges early-afternoon arrivals").
6. **Rerank loop (SECONDARY — see the ⛔ banner below).** Each duel flags `meta.rerankPending`;
   Claude MAY rerank the FULL set (`llmRank` per option) and stamp `meta.duelsAtRerank` so the
   staleness chip clears. But reranking is NOT the point of duels — it is a cosmetic side-effect.
   The PRIMARY job after a 5-duel batch is the **Duel → rules → BIG SEARCH protocol** (below):
   propose rules → user confirms → run the complete bidirectional big search. Every duel note
   becomes a precedent in `project_trippy_v2.md`.
6b. **Status formatting (user rule, Jun 11):** card statuses are STRUCTURED, never prose
   paragraphs: `{state, kind, doneAt, facts[], caveats[]}` in status.json, rendered as
   horizontal dot-separated segments. Rules: `doneAt` = ABSOLUTE date "Jun 11" (never
   "today"); each fact ≤ ~8 words with figures in <b>; caveats get ⚠; prefer full
   horizontal width over vertical lists; wiggle room per case but always scannable.
   Every option carries `pricedAt` (absolute datetime); collapsed cards show
   "checked Jun 11" (amber ⚠ when stale).
7. **Request loop (the 30s promise)**: `watch_requests.js` runs in background and EXITS when
   the user submits anything from a card → completion notification wakes Claude → process at
   priority → write `status.json` (+ option edits + rebuild) → re-arm watcher.
   ⛔ **ARMING IS MANDATORY, PER TRIP, THE MOMENT ITS BOARD GOES LIVE (locked 2026-07-20 after
   checkout clicks sat unseen ~2h).** One `run_in_background` Bash call per upcoming trip:
   `node app/watch_requests.js <slug> 240` — NEVER batch several into one wrapper script (the
   wrapper exits instantly, orphans the watchers, and their exits notify nobody).
   **Cross-session guarantees (all three live as of 2026-07-20):**
   (a) *Backlog scan on EVERY trippy session start*: for each trip compare `requests.jsonl`
   size vs its `.requests-processed` marker (bytes); process any backlog FIRST, then stamp the
   marker with the new size, then re-arm watchers for all upcoming trips.
   (b) *Heartbeat*: watch_requests.js touches `trips/<slug>/.watcher-heartbeat` every 1.5s, so
   liveness is checkable (mtime < 90s = a session is watching).
   (c) *No-session pickup*: schtask `Trippy-Requests-Watch` (every 5 min, hidden VBS →
   `state/trippy-requests-watch.ps1`) detects a queue with pending bytes >3 min old and no
   live heartbeat. NO Telegram pings (user rejected 2026-07-20) — the intended design is to
   SPAWN A HEADLESS CLAUDE (`claude.exe -p ... --permission-mode bypassPermissions`, tg-bridge
   pattern) that processes the backlog itself. That spawn script was blocked by the permission
   classifier and awaits explicit user approval; until then the task only stamps
   `.requests-pending-unwatched` and the backlog is caught by (a) on next session start. Card pills:
   amber "ASK/COMPLETE/CHECKOUT IN PROGRESS" → green "ANSWER/CHECKOUT READY".
   Measured: API-class answers ~30-90s; browser-class ~5 min. After ~2h idle, stop respawning
   the watcher; queued requests persist on disk and process on next re-arm.
8b. **Checkout VERIFICATION is mandatory (user caught this Jun 14).** Driving a URL is NOT
   proof a tab works. After opening each leg, STRICT-verify the rendered page actually shows
   the requested route + date + prices (not just "some times" — a generic page has times too;
   Trainline `bologna-to-munich` returned a default Florence->Sarzana form that fooled a
   weak time-count check). On fail, walk a per-leg fallback chain and re-verify: trains
   Trainline → DB (warm up via homepage+consent first, station IBNRs e.g. Bologna 8000099 /
   Munich 8000261) → Omio → ÖBB; buses FlixBus deep-link. Trainline serves Italian domestic
   legs but NOT international (Bologna→Munich) — use DB there. `state/verify_checkout.py` +
   `repair_tab3b.py` are the templates. Report only what's verified; mark UNVERIFIED honestly.
   - **Verify the DATE too, not just the route.** Trainline `/train-times/` SEO pages IGNORE
     `?departureDate=` and default to TODAY — never use them for checkout. (`state/verify_dates.py`.)
   - **NEVER guess station IDs.** Resolve via `bahn.de/web/api/reiseloesung/orte?suchbegriff=<name>`
     (no auth; returns `extId`: Bologna Centrale=8300217, München Hbf=8000261; IT=`83000…`,
     DE=`8000…`). A wrong ID makes DB search the wrong city while *displaying* the name you
     passed (guessed Bologna=8000099=Essen-Steele → priced Essen→Munich, "Bologna" only a label).
   - **IT regional trains are FLAT-FARE WALK-UP** (no reservation/advance price) — don't fake a
     dated checkout; point to Trenitalia and label "buy on the day". Only advance-book the buses
     (FlixBus) + long-distance trains (Railjet/EC/ICE via DB, where Sparpreis rises over time).
   - **Trenitalia drive** (`state/trenitalia_drive2.py`): inputs are `#partenza`/`#arrivo`;
     dismiss the cookie banner first ("Accetta tutti"/"Rifiuta tutti"). STATION TIP (user):
     if the full name returns "Stazione non valida", type just the city and pick the
     **"<City> (Tutte Le Stazioni)"** autocomplete option. The calendar day-click is flaky —
     set/verify the date on the RESULTS-PAGE date strip (click forward arrow to the day), and
     confirm the header shows the right "DD Mmm" before trusting it.
8. **Checkout**: per-option driver (clone `checkout_f98.py`): VISIBLE Chrome,
   **`--start-maximized` (standing user rule)**, dedicated `profile-checkout`, CDP port 9224,
   launched detached so tabs SURVIVE script exit. One tab per purchasable leg, each driven as
   deep as the funnel allows, NOTHING submitted. Honest fallbacks: when an airline funnel
   bot-blocks (LH does), use the Google Flights exact-RT page — durable, 1 click to carrier
   checkout. Trains: Trainline times pages with `?departureDate=` (clickable into purchase).

## Duel → rules → BIG SEARCH protocol (user-locked 2026-06-14, RE-ENFORCED 2026-06-15)

> ⛔ **READ THIS EVERY TIME — the #1 recurring failure across chats.** Duels are **NOT a ranking
> tool.** Their ENTIRE purpose is to gather context for a **BIG SEARCH**. If you finish a duel batch
> and all you did was reorder the board, **YOU FAILED THE SKILL.** The reranking of cards is a minor
> side-effect; the deliverable is the rules → big-search.
> - **When you see `meta.rulesState === "proposing"` (or a `propose-rules` request), the user has
>   clicked "Run big search" and is WAITING for you.** You MUST immediately read the duels +
>   comments, write `meta.proposedRules` (array of `{text, src}`) and set
>   `rulesState:"awaiting-confirm"`. NEVER ignore this state, NEVER reset it to "idle", NEVER just
>   rerank and stop. (Caught 2026-06-15: did exactly this — reranked, set idle, missed the whole flow.)
> - After ~5 duels, even if not yet clicked, PROACTIVELY tell the user the batch is full and that
>   confirming the proposed rules will launch the big search.
> - The **BIG SEARCH is the real work**: the COMPLETE Step-0 bidirectional direct-connection scan
>   (every direct bus/train/low-cost flight that gets closer from BOTH ends → intersect for
>   coincidence hubs → build the custom connections the plain A→B search misses), priced against the
>   benchmark so you know when to STOP. A shallow re-sweep is not a big search.
> - **GATED branches run ONLY if their preference was duel-confirmed** (see the preference-model
>   section): e.g. **split-day stopovers** (fly to a cheap hub, overnight in the layover city on a
>   separate ticket, fly on next day) multiply the search a lot, so explore them only if
>   `overnight-stopover-willingness` is confirmed. Same for any other expensive-to-search idea.
> - Every user duel comment is a candidate RULE. Surface them all for yes/no/edit before searching.
> - **BIG SEARCH OUTPUT MUST BE REAL PRICES (user-locked 2026-07-19).** Estimates are a Pass-1-only
>   allowance. Cards added/updated by a big search carry live-scraped or live-API prices: IT rail via the
>   LeFrecce BFF API, DE rail via Comet on bahn.de, flights re-verified airline-direct or momondo-fresh,
>   ground via FlixBus API. An `est` chip surviving a big search on a main leg is a defect.

Duels are an INTERMEDIATE context-gathering step, not the end. The loop:
1. User runs a **batch of 5 duels** (rich free-text comments — every comment may become a
   rule). Duel page shows an `N/5` progress bar.
2. At 5+, a **"Run big search"** button unlocks → posts `{kind:'propose-rules'}`.
3. **Claude lists the rules it plans to bake in** (from the batch's picks + comments) into
   `meta.json#proposedRules` with `rulesState:'awaiting-confirm'`; duel page renders them as
   checkbox + editable-text rows + an "add your own" field.
4. User accepts/drops/edits/adds, confirms → posts `{kind:'big-search', rules}`.
5. **Claude runs the big search** = full Step-0 bidirectional coincidence + price every
   viable common-hub connection, NOW applying confirmed rules as filters and the benchmark
   as the stop threshold (knowing when NOT to check). New cards land; append a log section.
6. User can start a fresh 5-duel batch anytime and repeat → a better-informed search.
Endpoints: `api/bigsearch`, `api/confirm-rules`. `meta.lastBigSearchDuels` = batch boundary.

**Batch flexibility (user-locked 2026-06-15):** the loop is NOT rigidly 5-then-search.
- **Go beyond 5:** the "Run big search" button stays enabled at 5+; the user can keep dueling for
  more context and run whenever. Batch bar reads "N duels · enough to search, or keep dueling".
- **Search then re-batch:** after a big search, `since` resets (via `lastBigSearchDuels`); new duels
  start a fresh batch. The batch bar switches off the "done" banner the moment `since>0`.
- **A 2nd+ batch must surface DIFFERENT match-ups, keeping settled winners.** The sampler:
  (a) seeds `seenPairs` from EVERY past duel so already-decided pairings don't repeat;
  (b) prioritises **FRESH options** — cards the BIG SEARCH added, flagged `fresh:true`, not yet dueled
  — and pairs each against a **SECURED winner** (won ≥2 duels, never lost) so new findings are tested
  against the champion; (c) a fresh option drops out of priority once it's been dueled.
- **Therefore: when a big search finds genuinely better options, ADD them as cards with `fresh:true`.**
  If it only confirms the benchmark (0 new cards), the next batch just explores untested existing pairs.

## THE BOARD CONTRACT (results page = the deliverable; user-locked)

The results board is the effort-allocator's CURATED FRONTIER for the locked date pair — NOT a
raw dump, NOT a hand-picked few. It must show:
- The **top ~20 realistically-considerable options**, populated **systematically top-N PER
  semantic category** (so the best of every strategy is visible — the Berlin lesson: a flat
  top-10 buried a whole category). Categories: ✈️ Nonstop anchor · 🛣️ Pure land (bus+train) ·
  🚄 Far-origin ground hack · 🎒 Cheap self-transfer · 🛬 Legacy 1-stop · 🗺️ Other arrival airports.
- **Everything visible** — dominated / rule-killed options stay, DIMMED, with their kill reason
  (the user checks for this; silent dropping is a defect).
- Each card = a **full door-to-door itinerary** (expandable), with `pricedAt`/"checked <date>",
  completion status, and ask/complete/checkout.
- **Views**: Categories (semantic) · Ranking 1-N (flat, cross-modal) · custom-sort (free text →
  request loop → Claude builds a named view in `views.json`).
- **Benchmark sits ON the board** (often dimmed): the naive A→B cheapest, which is frequently
  itself a rule-violator (Sarzana: €106 = 8h30 Sofia self-transfer) — its presence proves the
  curated frontier beats "the easy thing".
PITFALL (caught 2026-06-15): don't collapse the deep results to a few hand-built cards — generate
the board systematically from the completed data to honour the top-N-per-category contract.
PITFALL (caught 2026-06-16): **a thin board (~7 cards) makes the DUELS repeat the same 5-10 options
and the user's work feels wasted.** The board MUST carry the real variety the sweep found — aim for
~15-25 options spanning carriers / arrival airports / times / price points (the sweep had ~45 clean
≤1-stop options; surfacing only 7 was the bug). Two enforcement mechanisms now in code:
(1) build the pool wide (per-trip `build.py` can derive per-leg times for many options via a
`FIRST_HOP` table + the momondo total `dur`, so adding options is cheap);
(2) the duel sampler is **ANCHOR vs COVERAGE-WEIGHTED CHALLENGER** (refined 2026-06-16): ~majority
of duels pair a **rotating top-1/2/3 anchor** (`topAnchor`, weighted 0.5/0.3/0.2 to #1, never the same
anchor 3× in a row) against a **least-shown challenger** (`challengerPick`/`weightedPick`/`shownCounts`,
never the immediately-previous challenger). So the strongest options stay in as useful reference points
WITHOUT the duels feeling repetitive. ~25% probe an unsure rule (random matching options), ~15% are two
coverage picks for breadth, ~10% surface a killed option. `seenPairs` (seeded from all past duels)
blocks exact repeats. Verified: 15 sim duels → top-3 anchor in all, 13 distinct options, anchor rotated.

## UI decisions (locked across the 2026-06-10..15 session)

- **Look**: white/Airbnb scheme, Inter font, coral (#FF385C) primary actions, rounded cards,
  soft hover shadow, pill badges. ✈️ favicon (inline-SVG data URI in `STYLE`). Emojis ALLOWED
  here (explicit user opt-in — overrides the global no-emoji-in-UI rule for this app only).
- **Cards**: rank number OUTSIDE the card on the left; inside, a mode emoji (✈️ flight / 🛣️ land).
  Routes on TWO lines (`OUT …` / `BACK …`), never slash-joined. Header price + days top-aligned;
  D2D split shown (out/back/total). Flight rows highlighted blue + show duration + cost (RT fare
  labeled "RT"); night rows amber. `est` chips on estimates. Subgrid so OUT/BACK + the grey
  ground-extras bars align across both duel cards. The "why" is a toggle/hover chip, not always-on.
- **Status**: structured `{state,kind,doneAt,facts[],caveats[]}` rendered as horizontal segments
  (see 6b); collapsed card shows "checked <Mon D>" (amber when stale). Request pills single-line:
  ⏳ in-progress / ✓ ready.
- **Duel page**: pick → winner gets ring+lift+"⚔️ YOUR PICK", loser dims; click empty space to
  deselect. "Why did you pick it?" = 2 contextual chips + free text. **Saved duels**: hitting
  Complete/checkout mid-duel PARKS that duel as a clickable chip at the top (reopen the exact
  pair) — NOT a status bar. `N/5` batch bar + "Run big search" button (see protocol).
- **Nav** (lively buttons): ⚔️ Go to context duel · 📋 Research log · 🔄 Refresh prices · view
  switcher. Trip index `/`: the whole trip card links straight to results (one link).
- **Refresh-select**: 🔄 → checkboxes appear → pick cards → floating "Send refresh (N)" → request
  loop re-prices just those.
- **Checkout/result windows**: always `--start-maximized`.

## Research log (per trip, user-required for transparency)

`app/trips/<slug>/report.md` — append-only, newest-first, ONE dated section per large operation
(method · coverage · per-item verdicts incl. WHY killed · raw-artifact links), with `{#anchor}`
jump targets. Rendered at `/t/<slug>/report` (md→HTML, tables supported), linked from every nav.
Every big search / deep search appends its section as part of the operation.

## Auto-open (user-locked 2026-06-15) — APPROVED COMET EXCEPTION for this skill

When `/trippy` runs (or after rebuilding the board), **print the clickable results link AND
auto-open it in Comet** (`Start-Process` the URL, or launch Comet at it). This is the one
standing exception to the global "Comet only on explicit request" rule — the user explicitly
wants the trippy webapp in their daily-driver browser. Link form on its own line, plain:
`http://127.0.0.1:4126/t/<slug>/results`.

### HARD RULE — the PERSISTENT WEBAPP is the ONLY deliverable (user-locked 2026-06-15)
**NEVER hand the user a standalone `.html` file as the deliverable. ALWAYS deliver the trip inside
the persistent webapp at `http://127.0.0.1:4126/t/<slug>/results`** — the SAME app every trip (the
"Tuscany/Sarzana" app). Do not invent a per-trip HTML, do not open a `file:///…board.html`. A new
trip = a new DATA FOLDER (`app/trips/<slug>/{options.json,report.md,…}`), never new UI. The user
caught this on 2026-06-15 when a standalone board.html was opened instead of the webapp — that is a
defect. If the trip shape is unusual (e.g. ONE-WAY), adapt the webapp, do NOT escape to a static file:
- **One-way trips ARE supported.** Author `options.json` cards with `out` only and NO `ret` key,
  `daysOnSite:null`, `preBuilt:true`, and ship `segOut`+`totalsOut` (build_duel_data.py is skipped
  for preBuilt and is Munich-geo hardcoded — write a tiny per-trip `build.py` generator instead).
  `server.js` renderer is one-way-aware (guards every `o.ret`/`segRet`/`totalsRet`/`daysOnSite`;
  renders the single leg as "GO"/"TRIP"). EDIT server.js to extend the app for new trip shapes —
  it is the single source of truth, shared across all trips and sessions.
- After editing `server.js`, RESTART node (SHARED_JS is baked at require time) and headless-verify
  the results page actually paints (`web-fetch-pw/fetch.js <url>` → grep for a known card) before
  declaring done — a 200 status is not proof the client JS rendered.

## Preference model (full design in memory `project_trippy_v2.md`)

Seed with parametric GC = price_D2D + v·time_D2D (v≈€8/h, convex past ~6h, soft ceiling ~12h
same-day), THEN go empirical via yes/no duels on REAL trips, segmented by trip class, with
outlier probes. NO vector DB / no fitted ML at ~50-trip scale — a readable precedent wiki +
recompiled formula numbers. The model is an EFFORT-ALLOCATOR (what to fully complete / which
costly sub-searches to run), never the decision-maker.

### ⭐ CROSS-TRIP PREFERENCE WIKI — `travel-search\v2\preferences.json` (user-locked 2026-06-15)
The precedent wiki is REAL and persistent across ALL trips/chats. It is the realization of the
"readable precedent wiki" above — one structured JSON file, NOT a DB/ML/new app.
- **LOAD IT AT THE START OF EVERY TRIP** (before searching). Each rule has: `statement`, `class`
  (trip-class tags incl. `all`/`method`/`user-context`), an optional numeric `value`, `status`,
  `strength` (provisional 1-2 confirmations / established 3-5 / strong 6+), `confirmations`,
  `contradictions`, `evidence[]`, `lastConfirmed`, optional `reconfirm`.
- **APPLY by strength**: strong/established active rules → apply silently as filters/penalties.
  provisional OR shaky (any contradiction) → apply but **quick-confirm with ONE duel this trip**
  before trusting. `user-context` rules (e.g. Genova-beach) apply only when `appliesWhen` matches.
- **GENERALISE + match similar cases**: when a duel/comment expresses something a rule already
  covers (e.g. "nonstop worth €77 on a €400 trip" ↔ the `nonstop-comfort-premium` rule), DON'T
  treat it as brand-new — recognise the match, `confirmations++`, and refine the `value`
  (e.g. average the €/% across data points). A genuinely new comment → add a new provisional rule.
- **UPDATE AFTER EACH TRIP'S DUELS**: increment confirmations/contradictions, bump/drop the
  strength tier, set `lastConfirmed`, add the `evidence` (trip slug + the duel signal). A violated
  rule → `contradictions++` + `status:"shaky"` → must be re-confirmed next time it's relevant.
- **STRENGTH = how much to trust it without re-asking.** The whole point: over ~50 trips this file
  recompiles into a confident, segmented preference function — apply known-strong rules silently,
  spend duels only on the shaky/new ones.
- **The `/rules` page is interactive**: rules grouped into collapsible categories by tier; each rule
  has ✓ (confirm/holds) · ~ (modify) · ✗ (delete) buttons that reveal a comment box → "Send" →
  `POST api/rule-feedback` → appends to `travel-search\v2\rule-feedback.jsonl` (GLOBAL, cross-trip).
  **PROCESS that file** when you see entries: `confirm` → `confirmations++` + `lastConfirmed` (+ apply
  any comment); `modify` → edit the rule's statement/value/class per the comment; `delete` → set
  `status:"retired"` (keep for history, stop applying) or remove. Then bump/drop the strength tier.
  Feedback is queued (no live wake) — process on next activity and tell the user what you changed.
- **DUELS ⇄ RULES (not just create — also CONFIRM, REFINE, CONTRADICT):** when you process a trip's
  duels, for EACH existing rule decide: does a duel CONFIRM it (→ confirmations++, lastConfirmed),
  REFINE its `value` (→ recompute the number from the new data point, e.g. average the nonstop
  premium across €77 and a new gap), or CONTRADICT it (→ contradictions++ + `status:"shaky"`)? Only a
  genuinely-new signal makes a NEW rule. So a rule's number gets sharper every trip, it doesn't just
  accumulate counts.
- **RULE-AWARE duel sampling (the duels TEST unsure rules, not random):** the duel sampler (`api/state`
  now ships `prefs`) spends ~45% of duels deliberately probing the WEAKEST unsure rule (shaky first,
  then fewest confirmations) by building a pair that ISOLATES that rule's dimension — nonstop-vs-1stop
  for the comfort premium, clean-vs-self-transfer for the simplicity premium, GOA-vs-other for the
  Genova bonus, short-vs-long layover for the window. The duel records `probesRule`; map the winner
  back to confirm/refine/contradict that exact rule. (`probeFor(id, top)` in server.js — extend it
  with a new selector whenever you add a probeable rule.) The other ~55% stay rule-agnostic
  (fresh-vs-secured / rank-adjacent) for coverage.
- **PHILOSOPHY (user-locked 2026-06-17): infer STATEMENTS, not a function.** We are not fitting a
  precise GC. We're learning rough statements about what the user will trade to pay less — and they
  can't be deterministic. The biggest one: **time is NOT one quantity.** Distinguish **TRANSIT time**
  (hours actively on a bus/plane/layover bench — aversive) from **FIXED/STOPOVER time** (a day parked
  in a city you can explore — neutral or positive). NEVER count a stopover night as travel time
  (`totalsOut.d2dMin` excludes it; show it as a FIXED row, not a layover). See rules
  `transit-vs-fixed-time` and the `philosophy` field in preferences.json.
- **GATED SEARCH BRANCHES — test the preference with a DUEL before the expensive search.** Some ideas
  multiply the search cost a lot (e.g. **split-day stopovers**: fly to a cheap hub, sleep a night in
  the layover city, fly on the next day on a SEPARATE ticket — cheaper on some trips, + a night + a
  city to see). Do NOT bake these into the big search blindly. Add them to preferences.json as
  `status:"untested"` + `gatesSearchBranch:true`, build ONE illustrative concept card (clearly a
  PREFERENCE PROBE, est pricing, `stopover:true`), let the rule-aware sampler duel it, and ONLY if the
  user shows a preference does the big search explore that branch. (Seeded 2026-06-17:
  `overnight-stopover-willingness` + the asti `SO` concept card + its `probeFor` selector.)

## The webapp (PERSISTENT — a new trip is a data folder, never new code)

`node app\server.js` → http://127.0.0.1:4126 (check port free first).
Routes: `/` trip index · `/t/<slug>/{duel,results,report,checked}` ·
`/t/<slug>/api/{state,duel,comment,checkout,refresh,bigsearch,confirm-rules}`.
Route regex MUST allow hyphens in api paths (`api/[\w-]+`) — `\w` alone breaks `api/confirm-rules`
(silent 404; the "confirm does nothing" bug, fixed 2026-06-15).

### ⛔ WEBAPP INVARIANTS — shared features that EVERY trip inherits and that NO future edit may regress
These live in `server.js` (shared across all trips; a new trip is just a data folder, so it gets
them for free). The user locked these — **after ANY server.js change, restart node + headless-verify
they still work** (`web-fetch-pw/fetch.js <url>` → grep). User demand 2026-06-15: "make sure this
remains the case as we keep making modifications / I keep making requests."
1. **Global PUSH-NOTIFICATION toast (`gtoast` + `STATUSJS`)** injected via `withStatus()` into EVERY
   page (results/duel/checked/report). Bottom-right card, **never covers page content** (do NOT use a
   full-width top bar — it overlapped the header, caught 2026-06-15). Polls `api/state` every 4s:
   running/proposing = persistent spinner toast; `done-big` = green toast with a **horizontal
   countdown line that auto-dismisses (~14s)**, an **X to close**, the countdown **pauses while the
   tab is hidden** (Visibility API, so an alt-tabbed user still sees it), an **OS notification** (fires
   even when the tab is unfocused, on granted permission), and an **in-place board refresh**
   (`window.load()`, NOT a forced page reload). Uses a `localStorage` seen-signature (`meta.bigSummary`)
   so a completion that happened while away still shows once on next open, but never re-nags.
2b. **Dates + LOCAL times on every segment (user-locked 2026-07-19, ALWAYS).** Any itinerary spanning
   more than one calendar day: every segOut row's `t` carries a day label + local time ("Tue 15:08",
   or "d1/d2" for flexible-date cards), flight rows state BOTH ends' local clocks with tz names
   ("ORD 15:08 CDT -> BOS 18:47 EDT"), the arrival row names the full DATE ("Milano Centrale - Thu
   Aug 6, 16:30 local"), and `totalsOut.doorDep/doorArr` include the day label. All times are LOCAL
   to the place where they happen — never the origin's clock. `d2dMin` must be true tz-corrected
   elapsed and never 0 on a live card (caught 2026-07-19: placeholder d2dMin=0 shipped).
2. **One-way support**: cards may have `out` only (no `ret`), `daysOnSite:null`; renderer guards every
   `ret`/`segRet`/`totalsRet`/`daysOnSite`. Durations are ACTUAL travel time, never local-clock spans
   (timezone inflation) — store `totalsOut.d2dMin`; per-leg flight times on each row. **Total travel
   time goes on the FINAL leg row** as `"7h55 (14h total)"` (NOT on the first row). **Drop tiny minutes:
   <5 min → just `"14h"` (not `14h03`)** — applies to `dur_str` (per-trip build) AND `fm()` (shared).
3. **Duel → rules → BIG SEARCH protocol** (see that section) wired end-to-end: N/5 batch bar,
   Run-big-search, propose-rules → editable confirm panel → big-search. Duels are NEVER ranking-only.
   The page is the **CONTEXT ARENA** (`/duel` route) with TWO view tabs: **⚔️ Duel** (pairwise) and
   **📝 Dump** (a free-form textarea → `POST api/context` → `trips/<slug>/context-dump.jsonl` +
   `requests.jsonl`). The Dump view is for context the user just wants to state outright (constraints,
   preferences, dealbreakers, budget changes, stopover ideas) without a duel. **PROCESS every context
   dump**: fold each into the relevant rule(s) in `preferences.json` (confirm/refine/new), the
   objective, or the next search inputs — then tell the user what you did with it. Past dumps render
   in the Dump view from `state.contextDumps`.
4b. **Trip index splits Upcoming vs Past (user-locked 2026-07-19, ALWAYS rule)**: `/` groups trips by
   whether their latest TRAVEL date (max YYYY-MM-DD in cards' `out`/`ret` + trip header; slug `-YYYY-MM`
   month-end fallback; pricedAt/check dates must NOT count) is before today → "Past trips" section at the
   bottom, dimmed; everything else under "Upcoming trips". Implemented in server.js index route (`lastDate`).
4. **Dedicated pages with their own nav buttons**: `/checked` (Airports & stations checked, fed by
   `coverage.md`) and `/rules` (the cross-trip preference wiki rendered from `../preferences.json` —
   ALL rules incl. strong, with strength badges, **grouped into collapsible category sections**, each
   rule with ✓/~/✗ + comment-box feedback → `api/rule-feedback` → `rule-feedback.jsonl`). Nav (keep buttons SMALL/uncrowded — `.nbtn`
   padding 6px 12px / 12px font): Live results (grey) · Duel (coral) · Airports checked (green) ·
   Rules (purple) · Research log (dark) · Refresh (blue). Route regex must list every page name.
When the user asks for a UI/behaviour change, implement it in `server.js` (shared) so it applies to
ALL trips, never as a one-off in a single trip's data — then add it to THIS list.

## Multi-city / companion / "is this price real?" layer (built on us-tour-2026-10, 2026-09-25..28)

Reference implementation: `state/build_us_tour*.py`, `state/build_caleb.py`, `state/us_tour_booking.py`,
`state/verify_us_tour.py`, `state/refresh_us_tour.py`, `state/qa_us_tour.py`. Copy the pattern for the next big trip.
- **Whole-trip cards** (`tier:"plan"`) hold the full chain; each row carries `#<leg card id>`. Every row, ground step
  included, is its own dated line with a price (user rule: a cost he cannot see does not exist).
- **Event trips**: read the event's own schedule page FIRST (arrive-by / leave-after / nights); keep it in a data file
  (`results/<slug>/orlando.json` pattern), never hard-coded.
- **Booking rows** (`o.booking[]`): what, basis (one-way / round trip = both directions / open-jaw = one ticket /
  pay on board / hotel / your figure), price, url to the page where THAT option is visible (GF tfs search for the exact
  route+date; southwest.com for Southwest; operator dated search for trains/buses), `how` = exact row to pick,
  `ticket:true` for flights + intercity trains/buses (highlighted; "Open all" opens only those), `verified` stamp.
- **Real prices**: `verify_us_tour.py` re-opens every flight link (HTTP, browser for multi-city / client-rendered RT),
  the re-checked price overrides the sweep everywhere. A blank page = failed render, never "gone". Stale sweep prices
  cause pick churn: refresh the SWEEPS, not only the links.
- **Hard invariant**: total = sum of itinerary lines = sum of booking rows (to the cent) + completeness check; the
  build refuses to publish otherwise. Money shown with cents.
- **Pencil** (webapp, shared): edit any booking price -> row, its line (`seg`) and the total move; persisted in
  `price-overrides.json`, also queued in requests.jsonl. **Refresh button** runs `meta.refreshCmd` (per-trip executor)
  and the page reloads itself when done.
- **Companions** (`?person=<name>&match=<id>`, "Show <name>'s" button): a companion copies the user's WHOLE chain
  until plans split (same arrival or same day, same ground steps, event, hotel spend, shared flights); only the tail
  is searched. "SAME FLIGHT" is relative to each user option.
- **Once the companion has BOOKED** (2026-09-30): stop building cards for them. Record their flights in
  `app/trips/<slug>/<name>_booked.json` (null for anything not known: never guess from chat), and have the build write
  `meta.booked` (one chip per flight), `compMatch {flightId: 'day'|'flight'}` on every card and `comp` on the
  matching itinerary + booking row. The results page (shared `app/server.js`) then shows multi-select chips that
  keep only the user's options on the companion's day/flight, with yellow = same day and green = same flight marks.
  The user's default for a shared leg becomes the companion's flight; keep one card with the alternative.
- **Luggage**: backpack + carry-on always; fares without a carry-on get the fee added (carrier table in build_us_tour.py).
- **Offer both gateways** when a free bed exists near one (Miami): a VIA-X and a STRAIGHT-TO-Y card per ticket type.
- **Trip chat** (box feature) only stores messages on the laptop: nothing answers them unless a session reads
  `trips/<slug>/chat.jsonl` (reply via `POST api/chat` role assistant, then `api/chat-ack`).
- Run `state/qa_us_tour.py` (29 end-to-end checks in headless Chrome) after any change before telling him it works.

## Anti-bot / data-source map (verified 2026-06-10/11)

| Source | Status | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Google Flights (`gflights.py`) | NOT blocked. Verified from BOTH the VPS datacenter IP and the laptop's residential IP (2026-09-06, identical results). Plain HTTP + `tfs` param, no browser, no key. **Preferred flight source since 2026-09-06.** | fares discovery |
| momondo | headless BLOCKED (drift); `--offscreen` works; Italian block page detected. **VIRTUAL-SCROLLS = top ~50 cards only**; to see cheaper clean options below rank 50 you MUST use the `/stops=-1` path filter (`--max-stops`), which works ONLY on single-origin→single-dest queries (multi-airport commas silently disable it). | fares discovery |
| Ryanair APIs | open, free | route truth + per-day fares |
| Trainline SEO pages | plain HTTP 200, embedded JSON journeys | schedules (~today+5 pinned; date param ignored) |
| Trenitalia LeFrecce | **FULLY WORKING via BFF (verified 2026-07-19)**: GET `lefrecce.it/Channels.Website.BFF.WEB/website/locations/search?name=X&limit=5` → ids; POST `.../website/ticket/solutions` `{departureLocationId, arrivalLocationId, departureTime:"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.000", adults:1, children:0, criteria:{frecceOnly:false, regionalOnly:false, noChanges:bool, order:"DEPARTURE_DATE", offset:0, limit:N}, advancedSearchRequest:{bestFare:false}}` from plain curl, no auth → live journeys + REAL prices (found ICN 89512 REG→Asti direct €59.90). The old "solutions 400s" verdict is obsolete. | exact IT trains + prices — USE THIS, never estimate IT rail |
| bahn.de | bot-blocks automation profile (Fehler 751); web API 403; vendo host dead | exact DE prices need Comet (user OK) or DB app |
| Lufthansa funnel | bot-blocks form drive | go via Google Flights exact-RT handoff |
| omio | NOT blocked; form selectors rotted; discovery script died mid-typing (retryable) | user wants it; fix = generic-input drive or URL discovery |

## Standing user rules (trip class: short EU, ≤10d — from duels/precedents)

- NO transit overnights (airport benches AND forced en-route hotel nights). Kills, not penalties.
- NO ~8h layovers; ≤3h fine; 4-7h heavily penalized.
- Days ON SITE counted strictly (8 = 8 full days; +1d arrivals eat a day).
- OUT arrival window: not morning (pickup only after working hours), not ~21:00+ (too late);
  early-afternoon OK (café bridge). Ask, don't assume, for each trip's pickup logistics.
- Hassle premium for mixed-ticket hacks: bracketed €11 < x < €92 (duels 4-5).
- **LH-group one-ways are never the play** (measured: OW €288/€208 vs €164 full RT) — mixed
  itineraries only work when BOTH legs are low-cost carriers.
- €40 flat per unavoidable night.
- **Luggage baseline = backpack + full-size carry-on (user rule 2026-09-26, also for companions).** Any fare without a
  carry-on gets the bag fee (or the upcharge to the fare that includes it, e.g. United Basic domestic) ADDED to its
  price, per direction. Never show a personal-item-only fare at its bare price. (Replaces the old personal-item baseline.)
- Apply the discount stack silently: age 24, D-Ticket, Carte Avantage Jeune, MegaVolotea.
  **On every COMPLETE request: apply age/profile wherever it can change the price** —
  DB (youth/Sparpreis Young), Trenitalia/Italo (Giovane), SNCF (Avantage Jeune), omio
  (set traveler age). NOT relevant: FlixBus, low-cost airline fares (age-flat).
- Visible result/checkout windows: ALWAYS `--start-maximized`.

## Session discipline

- **Preflight: reap stale browsers BEFORE any sweep** (locked 2026-08-02).
  `...\Python312\python.exe C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\reap.py --kill`
  v2's `profile-chrome` / `profile-checkout` are launched detached on fixed CDP ports and
  deliberately never closed (login state), but nothing reaped them: three orphaned trees
  survived **30 h** with dead parents on 2026-08-02. The reaper is age-based (6 h default),
  so it never touches a browser a live run is using. At end of trip: `reap.py --kill --all`.
  It only matches browsers whose `--user-data-dir` is under `travel-search\`, so the user's
  daily-driver Comet can never be hit.
- Lock the objective to `travel-search\state\objective.txt` before searching; confirm before pivots.
- Date verification: results must be for the asked dates; a "today" fallback is a negative result.
- 2 failures with one approach → switch strategy (bahn.de taught this twice).
- Killed/dominated options stay visible with reasons — transparency is a feature the user checks.
- Validation gate: user compares output to a manual momondo search before trusting structural changes.

# Add Logos Skill

## Purpose

Find a real company logo online, download it, remove the background, save the
result to the user's logos folder. The default output is a clean, transparent
PNG cropped to content, ready to drop into any document, deck, or HTML page.

## Default logos folder

`C:\Users\Alessandro\logos\` — every output goes here unless the user names a
different folder. Files use the slug `<slug>.<ext>` for the original and
`<slug>_nobg_crop.png` for the final transparent crop.

## Workflow

### 1. Identify the company and slug

- Accept the company name from the user, or parse it from an HTML/text source
- Pick a short lowercase slug for the filename (e.g. `unternehmertum` → `utum`,
  `Boston Consulting Group` → `bcg`)

### 2. Find a logo source — priority order

Try these in order, stop at the first success:

**A. Wikimedia Commons (most reliable for well-known companies)**
```
https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php?action=query&titles=File:FILENAME.svg&prop=imageinfo&iiprop=url&iiurlwidth=500&format=json
```
- The `thumburl` field returns a rendered PNG (already alpha for SVG sources)
- Known filenames: `Boston_Consulting_Group_2020_logo.svg`, `HEC_Paris.svg`,
  `Logo_of_the_Technical_University_of_Munich.svg`, `Volkswagen_logo_2019.svg`
- For unknown filenames: WebSearch `COMPANY site:commons.wikimedia.org`

**B. Company website — header logo**
- WebFetch the homepage and ask: "Find the URL of the logo image in the header"
- If the logo is inlined as `<svg>...</svg>` in the HTML (no separate URL),
  Read the homepage HTML directly and save the SVG markup to
  `logos/<slug>.svg` — inline SVGs are already transparent and need no
  further processing
- Common patterns: `/assets/images/logo.png`, `/img/logo.svg`, `/logo.png`

**C. GitHub (for open-source / dev tools)**
- `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ORG/REPO/main/web/public/icon1024.png`
- `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ORG/REPO/main/public/logo.png`
- WebFetch `https://api.github.com/repos/ORG/REPO/contents/web/public` to list

**D. Favicon / apple-touch-icon (raster fallback)**
- `https://company.com/apple-touch-icon.png` (180x180 typical)
- `https://company.com/assets/images/favicon/android-icon-192x192.png`
- `https://company.com/favicon.ico` (last resort, very small)

**Do NOT use Clearbit.** `logo.clearbit.com` is blocked in this environment
and always fails with DNS resolution errors.

### 3. Run the helper script

The helper `process_logo.py` (in this skill folder) does download +
background-removal + crop in one shot:

```powershell
& "C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe" `
  "C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\skills\add-logos\process_logo.py" `
  "<url>" "<slug>"
```

Outputs into `C:\Users\Alessandro\logos\`:
- `<slug>.<ext>` — original download (png/jpg/gif/svg/webp)
- `<slug>_nobg.png` — background removed (transparent PNG)
- `<slug>_nobg_crop.png` — trimmed to content bbox with 4px padding

For SVG sources the script saves the SVG as-is and skips bg removal (SVGs are
already transparent).

**Background-removal strategy**:
1. **Flood-fill color key** (default for logos). The script samples the 4
   corner pixels. If at least 3 agree within tolerance, it BFS-flood-fills
   that color out from the corners with alpha=0. This handles 90%+ of
   logos perfectly (white, off-white, brand-colored solid backgrounds)
   and preserves color regions INSIDE the logo body that happen to match
   the bg color.
2. **rembg ML fallback** (for non-solid backgrounds). If the 4 corners
   disagree, or `REMBG_FORCE=1` is set, the script falls back to `rembg`
   with the `u2netp` model (4.7MB). Override the model with
   `REMBG_MODEL=u2net` for the heavier 176MB version.

Tune the color-key tolerance via `BG_TOLERANCE=N` (default 30, range 0-255).

Flags:
- `--logos-dir DIR` — override the logos folder
- `--no-bg-remove` — keep only the original
- `--no-crop` — keep the nobg version uncropped

The script prints a single JSON line with all output paths and any errors.

### 4. Validate the result

- Read the produced `_nobg_crop.png` to confirm it shows the logo (not a
  loading state or transparent void)
- If the result looks wrong (e.g. rembg ate part of the logo, or the
  background was complex enough to confuse u2net), retry with a different
  source — usually Wikimedia or an SVG from the company's brand page

### 5. Report

Tell the user:
- The source URL used
- The three output filenames + their sizes
- Any logos that could not be found (offer text fallback)

## (Optional) Wire into an HTML file

If the user is replacing CDN URLs in an HTML file, after the logos are on
disk, use the Edit tool to swap each `src="https://..."` for
`src="logos/<slug>_nobg_crop.png"`. Keep the relative path simple by
co-locating the HTML and the `logos/` folder.

## Known Logo Sources (Alessandro's companies)

| Company | Source | Slug |
|---------|--------|------|
| BCG | Wikimedia: `Boston_Consulting_Group_2020_logo.svg` | `bcg` |
| HEC Paris | Wikimedia: `HEC_Paris.svg` | `hec` |
| TU Munich | Wikimedia: `Logo_of_the_Technical_University_of_Munich.svg` | `tum` |
| UnternehmerTUM | unternehmertum.de inline SVG (header) or android-icon-192 | `utum` |
| VW | Wikimedia: `Volkswagen_logo_2019.svg` | `vw` |
| Langfuse | GitHub: `langfuse/langfuse/main/web/public/icon1024.png` | `langfuse` |
| K&L Gates | `https://www.klgates.com/assets/images/logo.png` | `klgates` |
| CDTM | Wikimedia: `CDTM_Logo.jpg` (search Wikipedia for CDTM) | `cdtm` |
| LBO France | Wikimedia: `Logo_LBO_France.gif` | `lbo` |

## Dependencies

The helper script needs (already installed):
- Python 3.12 at `C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe`
- `Pillow` — image I/O, flood-fill color key, crop
- `rembg` + `onnxruntime` — ML fallback for non-solid backgrounds. Default
  model is `u2netp` (4.7MB, auto-downloads to `%USERPROFILE%\.u2net\` on
  first use). Override with `REMBG_MODEL=u2net` for the 176MB model when
  the lightweight one misses detail.

If a fresh machine is missing these:
```powershell
& "C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe" `
  -m pip install --timeout 180 Pillow rembg onnxruntime
```

## Notes

- Run parallel downloads when processing multiple logos
- If a file is < 500 bytes after download, it's likely an HTML error page —
  the script's magic-byte sniff catches this and exits non-zero
- For very small institutions (e.g. CDTM), check Wikipedia's infobox via
  `WebFetch https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INSTITUTION_NAME` and pull the
  `upload.wikimedia.org` URL
- GIF logos (e.g. LBO France) work fine in HTML — don't reject them, but
  rembg may not handle animation; for animated GIFs grab a single frame first
- For logo icons rembg's u2net/u2netp models often FAIL — they're trained
  on photographic foregrounds and can wipe entire flat-color logos. The
  flood-fill color key is the right primary strategy for graphic logos;
  rembg is only the fallback when the input image has a non-solid bg
  (gradient, photo backdrop, branded pattern)

# AHK Skill

## Purpose

Create AutoHotkey v2 scripts and write them directly to the user's AHK folder.

## AHK Folder

```
C:\Users\Alessandro\OneDrive - HEC Paris\Documents\AutoHotkey\
```

## File Naming Convention

Match the existing convention: `description (hotkey).ahk`

Examples from existing scripts:
- `CV - PRINT without Markups (alt + f1).ahk`
- `mousepos (alt + f3).ahk`
- `remove apostroph (alt + f2).ahk`
- `Open Grammarly AI (ctrl alt i).ahk`

## Default: AHK v2 Syntax

Always use v2 unless the user asks otherwise or the script clearly fits v1 patterns.

### AHK v2 Template

```ahk
#Requires AutoHotkey v2.0

; Hotkey modifier symbols: ! = Alt, ^ = Ctrl, + = Shift, # = Win
; Example: !F1 = Alt+F1, ^!i = Ctrl+Alt+I

HOTKEY:: {
    Send("...")
    Sleep(100)
}
```

### Common v2 Patterns

```ahk
; Send text
Send("Hello World")

; Send key combinations
Send("^c")         ; Ctrl+C
Send("^{Home}")    ; Ctrl+Home
Send("{Enter}")    ; Enter
Send("{F6}")       ; F6
Send("+{F10}")     ; Shift+F10
Send("{Up 3}")     ; Up arrow 3 times

; Click
Click(x, y)
Click("right")

; Type literal string (ignores special chars)
SendText("some literal {text}")

; Pause between actions
Sleep(500)   ; 500ms

; Open a URL or app
Run("https://www.google.com")
Run("notepad.exe")

; Window targeting
WinActivate("Window Title")
WinWaitActive("Window Title")

; Clipboard
A_Clipboard := "text to copy"
Send("^v")

; Show a tooltip
ToolTip("message", , , 1)
Sleep(2000)
ToolTip(, , , 1)  ; clear it
```

### Modifier symbols

| Symbol | Key |
|--------|-----|
| `!`    | Alt |
| `^`    | Ctrl |
| `+`    | Shift |
| `#`    | Win |

## Workflow

1. Understand what the user wants the hotkey to do
2. Choose an appropriate hotkey (ask if not specified, suggest one that doesn't conflict with common ones)
3. Write the AHK v2 script
4. Name the file following the convention
5. Write it to `C:\Users\Alessandro\OneDrive - HEC Paris\Documents\AutoHotkey\`
6. Tell the user to: right-click the tray AHK icon → Reload, or double-click the new .ahk file to run it

## Notes

- Sleep values: use 100ms for fast keypresses, 500ms when waiting for UI to respond
- If the script types text that may contain special AHK characters (`{`, `}`, `^`, `!`, `+`, `#`), use `SendText()` instead of `Send()`
- All-day scripts that should persist: remind the user to add them to Windows startup (shell:startup folder)
- The user has existing scripts in both v1 and v2; never touch or modify the v1 scripts

# /cdtm — CDTM kickoff taskforce assistant

User invocation: `/cdtm $ARGUMENTS`

All work lives in `C:\Users\Alessandro\cdtm-taskforce\`.

## Step 0 — get current (ALWAYS, before doing anything)
Read, every time, in this order:
1. `C:\Users\Alessandro\cdtm-taskforce\_START-HERE.md` — orientation, dates, people, how-to-work.
2. `C:\Users\Alessandro\cdtm-taskforce\progress.json` — live status of the 8 workloads + open items.

Pull in only when a workload needs them:
- `SESSION-NOTE-2026-06-17-raunaq.md` — the full plan (WL1-WL8, materials, joint-with-Raunaq flags).
- `CONTEXT-dates-class.md` — class (Fall 2026), all dates, key emails.
- `links.json` — every template/deck/form link from the master sheet.

Then give a 3-line snapshot: Done / In progress / Next, plus any `open_questions`.

## Route on $ARGUMENTS
- empty or "status" → print the snapshot, ask which workload to pick.
- "wlN" / "workload N" / a phrase matching a workload → open that workload in SESSION-NOTE, grab its materials from links.json, start it, and set its `status` to `in_progress`.
- an item that surfaced from /daily or /triage → map it to the right workload (or a sub-task), then proceed.
- "weekly" / "update raunaq" → run `weekly_update.py` to refresh the Raunaq draft.

## When a workload is worked on
- Append a dated `{ "date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "note": "..." }` to that workload's `log` in progress.json, written in Alessandro's voice (the latest note feeds the weekly email to Raunaq).
- Flip `status`: todo -> in_progress -> done.
- `open_questions` are Raunaq-facing (they render as "On your side / open" in the weekly email) — phrase them as questions/notes to him; clear them when resolved.
- **Then sync the KB bridge (below).** `progress.json` stays the single source of truth; the wiki only mirrors a summary.

## KB bridge — keep the wiki in sync (run after any progress.json change)
`/cdtm` is the maintainer of the onboarding workstream's wiki presence. The bridge page is `C:\Users\Alessandro\vault_kb\Projects\cdtm-onboarding.md`. Inside it is a delimited block:

```
<!-- CDTM-ONBOARDING:STATE:START -->
## Current state (auto — rewritten by /cdtm from `~/cdtm-taskforce/progress.json`; do not edit)
... bullets ...
- Last sync: YYYY-MM-DD (from progress.json @ <progress.json date>)
<!-- CDTM-ONBOARDING:STATE:END -->
```

Rule: **rewrite ONLY the text between the START/END markers** — never touch the durable prose outside it, and never put live status anywhere else in the wiki. Regenerate the bullets from the current `progress.json`: a one-line workload roll-up (which WLs are todo / in_progress / done), the few live highlights (what just moved, what's booked, what's blocked), the key fixed dates, the open items, and a `Last sync` line. Keep it compact (≈6 bullets) — it is a summary, not a copy of the logs. Don't run the full `/kb-maintain`; just edit this one block. (Durable scope, dates, and the 8-workload list outside the block change only when the plan itself changes.)

## Files & commands
- Weekly draft generator: `python weekly_update.py` (drafts to cdtm Gmail drafts, never sends). Scheduled task `CDTM-Kickoff-Weekly-Update` runs it every Fri 16:00.
- Email drafts: `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\gmail.py draft --account cdtm --to <..> --cc raunaq.jain@cdtm.com --body-file <..>` (use `--body-file` for multi-line; `*text*` = bold in the HTML part).
- Calendar: Google Calendar MCP. Onboarding calendar id is in CONTEXT-dates-class.md.

## Don't
- Don't send emails or create/modify calendar events without an explicit go-ahead.
- Don't reconstruct state from memory — always read `_START-HERE.md` + `progress.json` first.

---
name: design-sync
description: >
  Keep Claude Design projects in step between teammates through the tundra-design GitHub repo:
  set up a new teammate's machine, add or share a project, run the end-of-day sync (fresh mirror,
  3-way merge of the other person's pages into yours, push via DesignSync, verify, record), stack
  pages from one project into another, check mirror status. Use on "/design-sync", "set me up for
  the design sync", "add my project to the sync", "share this project with <name>", "eod sync",
  "pull <name>'s pages", "stack this page into my project".
---

# /design-sync

Repo: `C:\Users\Alessandro\tundra-design`. The procedure lives in `sync/README.md` (same repo); read it before acting and
follow it step by step. `sync/ONBOARDING.md` is the first-time path for a new teammate. Fix those
files, not this one: every teammate's Claude reads the same copies after `git pull`.

## Modes

- **setup** ("set me up"): follow `sync/ONBOARDING.md` from the top. Run `python sync/setup.py --person <name>`,
  then walk the human through the two manual steps it prints, then verify with the check in ONBOARDING step 4.
- **add project** ("add my projects"): ask which Claude Design projects to mirror. Take the UUID from each
  `claude.ai/design/p/<uuid>` link. Add `"<key>": {"<me>": "<uuid>"}` to `sync/projects.json`,
  commit, push. The mirror picks it up within 3 minutes; confirm with `python sync/dsync.py status`.
- **share** ("share project X with <name>"): a shared key has both people's ids. The two copies must hold the
  same files, so the second person seeds an EMPTY project of theirs from the first person's mirror (README,
  "Sharing a project"). Never seed into a project that already has work in it.
- **eod** (no args, "eod", "sync"): README section "EOD procedure", for every shared key.
- **stack** ("take page P from <name>'s project K into mine"): README section "Stacking pages".
- **status**: `python sync/dsync.py status`, plus `curl -s 127.0.0.1:4190/status` (lastPoll = the Chrome
  extension is alive) and the tail of `sync/mirror.log`.

## Hard rules

- Refresh the mirror right before any merge (`python sync/dsync.py mirror`). The person edits in the web UI
  between your turns; merging from a stale copy destroys their edits.
- DesignSync pushes use `localPath` only. Never inline `data`, never `get_file` to pull a deck (it floods context).
- If `verify` fails, do NOT `record`. Report it.
- Never edit `live/<other person>/` and never hand-edit `live/` to change a design; edit in claude.ai/design.
- The mirror server and the Chrome extension must never open a visible window.

---
name: fede-coach
description: >
  Vendor-neutral AI building coach for beginners, operators, and AI-built
  prototypes. Use when a user is new to AI coding, has workflow ideas, has a
  localhost or "works on my machine" app from Cursor, Claude, Codex, Replit,
  Lovable, Bolt, or similar, and asks what to build first, how to share it,
  turn it into a URL, put it online, make it beta-ready, or make it safe for
  teammates, beta users, or customers. Focuses on workflow mapping, ICE
  prioritization, version plans, staging rules, field hacks, live URLs, and
