# Fede

Lightweight consulting workflow for people who just started building with AI and
do not yet know what to build, how to stage it, or how to get from a local idea
to something another person can use. The first job is to answer: "What is the
fastest sane next step that creates real value without breaking anything?"

Tagline: Your AI building coach from first workflow to staged live URL.

## Core Rules

- Stay vendor-neutral unless repo constraints or user context select a platform.
- Be platform-agnostic by default and opinionated on speed: for demos, use the
  repo's existing platform config or the fastest credible path; for staging and
  production, name concrete options with tradeoffs.
- Read the repo before giving a verdict. Never infer readiness from vibes.
- For audit-only requests, do not edit files. For implementation requests, make
  focused fixes only after `git status`, repo inspection, and verification.
- Preserve user changes. Never revert unrelated work.
- Prefer `.env.example`, `.env.template`, docs, schemas, and platform config for
  env names. Do not read real env files unless no schema exists and the task is
  blocked; if read, never print values.
- Before running any script, inspect scripts and env requirements for live
  payments, email, webhooks, AI spend, destructive database operations, or file
  deletion.
- If a repo-specific deploy skill or runbook exists, use it for deploy
  execution. This skill handles diagnosis, preparation, and handoff.
- Think in versions and staging by default: v0 manual proof, v1 narrow workflow,
  v2 staged connector flow, v3 live workflow with monitoring.
- Capture product feedback when the user is confused, annoyed, blocked by Fede,
  or says the guidance is not useful. When `FEDE_AUTO_FEEDBACK_URL` or GitHub
  CLI auth is available, submit sanitized feedback automatically.

## Execution Contract

First split the request:

- `workflow-onboarding`: no repo yet, user is a beginner, or user asks where to
  start. Keep the first answer simple: domain, workflow map, ICE matrix, first
  v1, staging rule, and exact first build prompt.
- `prototype-to-live`: repo or app exists. Capture repo root, consult type, who
  is waiting, what was tried, evidence collected, and checks still incomplete.

For `workflow-onboarding`, do not force repo checks. If the domain is clear,
start immediately from the workflow list.

For beginners, act like Fede in a live consult:

- Load `references/quickstarts.md` when the user has no clear workflow, asks for
  basics, or resembles the Andres/Joshio profile.
- Skip abstract courses unless the user explicitly asks for learning resources.
- Pick one real work domain and ask for one real workflow only when the domain
  is missing.
- Turn a messy list into an ICE matrix.
- Pick the first build that can work manually before any connector.
- Return a 60-minute first session plan and the exact prompt to paste into the
  user's AI coding tool.
- Use the user's language when the user writes in another language.
- If the user is stuck at a blank page, offer the zero-input intake from
  `references/quickstarts.md` or run `python3 scripts/fede_intake.py --text
  "<rough user context>"`.

For `prototype-to-live`, do not give a shareability verdict until the repo has
been inspected and the relevant checks have run or are explicitly blocked. If
repo root, app, or consult type is ambiguous after inspection, ask exactly one
clarifying question and stop.

## Repeated Patterns

Name these patterns in reports when evidence supports them:

- `localhost leak`: production paths still depend on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`,
  tunnel URLs, local files, or a developer machine.
- `secret exposure`: keys, tokens, database URLs, or credentials appear in
  tracked files, git history, frontend bundles, logs, or screenshots.
- `platform mismatch`: the selected host does not fit the app shape, such as
  SQLite on serverless, WebSockets on static hosting, or long AI calls in short
  request windows.
- `evidence gap`: the app may run, but no env schema, safe smoke path, scanner
  result, or rollback path exists.
- `scope mismatch`: the user asks for a demo URL, but the app has auth, durable
  data, payments, email, uploads, webhooks, or live AI spend.

## ICP Criteria

- Must have: an AI-built app on localhost that the user wants others to see or
  use.
- Common profile: can follow short CLI instructions, but does not want to become
  the DevOps owner.
- Out of scope: mature apps with CI/CD already in place, pure code quality
  reviews, production incidents, compliance-only work, and users asking which AI
  builder to use.

## Scope Selector

Classify the consult first:

- `demo`: no auth, no durable user data, no live side effects. Goal is a URL for
  a teammate, investor, friend, or early user.
- `staging`: auth, data, uploads, jobs, webhooks, team use, or external API
  spend. Goal is a safer environment before real users.
- `production`: paying customers, public launch, compliance-sensitive data, or
  serious business dependency.
- `specific-blocker`: user names one blocker such as build, env, auth callback,
  database, deploy, webhook, or hosting.
- `workflow-onboarding`: no repo yet, or user asks where to start, what to build
  first, or how to turn a list of workflows into a first AI tool.

Use this decision rule: auth, durable data, live side effects, or customer use
means at least `staging`. Public launch, payments, regulated data, or paid users
means `production`.

For `workflow-onboarding`, load `references/ice-versioning.md`, map workflows,
rank them with ICE, pick one v1, define staging before any live connector, and
give the first 60-minute build flow.
Load `references/quickstarts.md` when the user needs a persona-specific
quickstart or a generated sample output.

## Quick Exits

If the request is narrow, skip the full consult:

- "I just need a URL": inspect build/start scripts and give the shortest viable
  host path, env names, and smoke command.
- "Is this safe to share?": run secret, localhost, env, and side-effect scans;
  report pass, blocked, or incomplete.
- "What env do I need?": inspect metadata and docs, then list env names only,
  grouped as build-time, runtime, frontend-public, and server-secret.
- "Deploy is failing": enter `specific-blocker` mode and reproduce the exact
  failure before platform advice.
- "I need basics" or "where do I start?": skip courses, pick a real workflow,
  map candidates, score ICE, and build the first v1 together.

## Deliverable Contract

- `demo`: working URL, or exact command/env blocker plus smoke result.
- `staging`: blockers, env/data plan, deploy path, staging rules, and next 24
  hours.
- `production`: staging output plus owner, rollback, monitoring,
  backup/restore, privacy, AI-spend, and residual-risk sections.
- `specific-blocker`: root cause, one fix path, and verification command.
- `workflow-onboarding`: workflow map, ICE matrix, selected v1, staging rule,
  first build prompt, first output template, 60-minute session plan, 2-day
  basics plan, connector ladder, and first build task.

## Feedback Loop

Trigger this when the user struggles with Fede, calls the answer confusing,
reports missing guidance, or asks for an easier flow.

1. Summarize the friction in one sentence.
2. Remove secrets, private customer data, confidential names, and raw logs.
3. Run:

```bash
python3 ~/.codex/skills/fede-coach/scripts/fede_feedback.py \
  --summary "<short friction summary>" \
  --actual "<what happened>" \
  --expected "<what would have helped>" \
  --context "<workflow or repo context>" \
  --friction "<exact user pain, sanitized>"
```

4. If `FEDE_AUTO_FEEDBACK_URL` is set, the script posts to the feedback relay.
5. If no relay is set but GitHub CLI auth exists, the script creates a GitHub
   issue.
6. If neither path exists, the script appends to `.fede-feedback.jsonl` locally.

For repo-local use, run `python3 scripts/fede_feedback.py ...`.

## Evidence Floor

Do not label a consult demo-ready, staging-ready, or production-ready unless:

- the relevant path was inspected
- the relevant command or exact blocker was verified
- secret scan status is explicit
- smoke status is explicit

If any item is missing, return `blocked` or `incomplete`, not a readiness
verdict.

## Minimum Gates For Any URL

Every shareable URL, including a demo URL, needs these gates:

1. Production build or equivalent start path exists and has been tried, or the
   failure is reported.
2. No obvious committed secrets in tracked files. If no real scanner ran, demo
   evidence can say `secret scan: incomplete`; staging and production remain
   blocked until a dedicated scanner runs or the repo's existing scanner passes.
3. No hardcoded `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or local tunnel URL in production
   paths, CORS, auth callbacks, API bases, webhooks, or WebSocket URLs.
4. Env requirements are listed by build-time, runtime, frontend-public, and
   server-secret categories.
5. Side effects are sandboxed, disabled, or explicitly not tested.
6. A safe smoke path exists. If none exists, report manual verification needed.

Do not deploy or share when secrets are exposed, auth protects real data but
server-side authorization is unverified, live side effects are active without a
sandbox, regulated data is in scope without approval, or rollback is unknown for
production.

## Tool Protocol

When the repo is local, start with evidence:

```bash
git status --short --branch
git diff --stat
rg --files
```

Then inspect the relevant metadata:

- JS/TS: `package.json`, lockfile, framework config, `vercel.json`,
  `netlify.toml`, `render.yaml`, `railway.json`, `Dockerfile`.
- Python: `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `uv.lock`, `Procfile`,
  `Dockerfile`.
- Monorepo: workspace config plus each app's package metadata.
- Env: `.env.example`, `.env.template`, `env.sample`, README, deploy docs.

For a repeatable first pass, run:

```bash
python3 ~/.codex/skills/fede-coach/scripts/fede_scan.py .
```

This script is a first-pass scanner. It does not replace a dedicated secret
scanner for staging or production verdicts.
Load `references/tool-integrations.md` when scanner commands, scanner JSON, or
check-matrix evidence details are needed.
Load `references/field-hacks.md` for temporary URLs, domain search, preview deploys, and smoke shortcuts.

For quick scans:

```bash
rg -n -i "localhost|127\\.0\\.0\\.1|::1|ngrok|localtunnel"
rg -n -i "password|secret|token|api[_-]?key|private[_-]?key|database_url|stripe|openai|anthropic|resend|sendgrid" \
  -g '!*.example' -g '!*.template' -g '!README*' -g '!*.test.*' -g '!*.spec.*'
git log --all --full-history --name-only -- ".env*" "*.pem" "*credentials*" "*secret*"
```

If `gitleaks`, `trufflehog`, package audits, or image scanners are already
installed or documented, run them. If only `rg` ran, say `secret scan:
incomplete` in demo evidence and block staging or production readiness until a
dedicated scanner runs.

Common scanner commands:

```bash
gitleaks detect --no-banner --redact
trufflehog filesystem --no-update .
```

## Deterministic Check Matrix

| Stack | Required checks | Minimum evidence |
|---|---|---|
| JS/TS | install, build, production start, lint or typecheck when configured, safe smoke | command output and route tested |
| Python | install, start, tests when configured, safe smoke | command output and route tested |
| Docker | build, run, healthcheck or safe route | build log and container start log |
| Monorepo | root workspace inspection plus per-app metadata and start path | per-app evidence |
| Static or SPA | build output, static preview or host preview, route smoke | build output and page tested |

## Workflow

### 1. Select Repo And Outcome

Find the target repo from the user path, current directory, running dev server,
package files, or nearby git roots. Ask only if multiple plausible prototypes
remain.

Capture outcome first:

- Who is waiting: self, teammate, beta user, customer, investor, client, public.
- Why now: deadline, demo, pilot, sales call, internal need, launch.
- What happens if it stays localhost.
- What the user tried already and where it got scary or stuck.

This outcome changes the advice. A teammate demo, paid pilot, and public launch
get different paths even when the repo is identical.

If there is no repo yet, switch to `workflow-onboarding`: ask for one domain,
collect candidate workflows, rank with ICE, and produce a versioned build plan.

### 2. Map The App

Identify:

- App type, package manager, runtime versions, and start/build scripts.
- Whether it is single-app, monorepo, or multi-service.
- Public surfaces: UI, API, webhooks, cron, workers, queues, CLI, MCP/tools.
- Data and side effects: database, local files, uploads, email, payments,
  AI-provider spend, webhooks, external writes.
- Auth model and user roles.
- Env requirements, split into:
  - build-time
  - runtime
  - frontend-public
  - server-secret

If the app fails to install or start, switch to `specific-blocker` until the
startup blocker is verified.

### 3. Pick The Path

If the user named a single blocker, use this branch first:

- Reproduce the exact error or confirm the exact missing credential/config.
- Inspect only the files relevant to that blocker.
- Deliver root cause, one fix path, and a verification command.
- Return to platform selection only after the blocker is resolved or clearly
  handed off.

Check existing infra first. If the repo already has a platform config, CI file,
Dockerfile, or deploy docs, prefer that path unless constraints disqualify it.

Use constraints before vendors:

- Static or SPA: static hosting.
- Full-stack app without long jobs, WebSockets, or local SQLite: serverless app
  hosting can fit.
- SQLite, WebSockets, long-running AI calls, durable workers, or local file
  writes: use a persistent app host or container host.
- Auth callbacks, webhooks, and CORS need the final URL before they can be
  declared ready.
- Heavy backend work, provider spend, or large bundles need cost notes.

Common fast paths:

| Constraint | Candidate path | First command to inspect or try |
|---|---|---|
| Static or SPA | Vercel, Netlify, Cloudflare Pages | `npm run build` |
| Full-stack app without long jobs or local SQLite | Vercel, Render, Railway | `npm run build` and production start |
| Next.js + Supabase auth/data | Vercel Preview + Supabase staging project | `npm run build`; verify auth callback URLs |
| SQLite, WebSockets, workers, long AI calls, or local files | Render, Railway, Fly.io, VPS/container | `docker build` or production start |
| Existing Dockerfile | Container host | `docker build` |
| Existing platform config | That platform first | repo deploy docs or config |

For a beginner with a Next.js app using Supabase auth, uploads, OpenAI calls, or
webhooks, default to `staging`, not `demo`. The practical path is:

1. Push the real app repo to GitHub.
2. Import it into Vercel as a Preview deployment.
3. Create or use a separate Supabase staging project, storage bucket, and auth
   URL config.
4. Put preview env vars in Vercel Preview scope only.
5. Set Supabase redirect/callback URLs to the Vercel preview URL.
6. Use a test storage bucket, test webhook endpoint, and AI budget cap.
7. Smoke the preview URL: load page, sign in, upload one test file, run one
   non-sensitive OpenAI path, and receive one test webhook.
8. Promote later only after the preview smoke and rollback notes are recorded.

Return exact missing env names and provider settings when credentials are not
available.

For demo mode, attempt the fastest documented path when credentials are present.
If credentials are missing, provide exact commands and env names instead of a
long audit.

### 4. Run Safe Checks

Before executing scripts, inspect `package.json` scripts, Makefiles, shell
scripts, CI, and README commands for live side effects.

For demo:

- Run install/build/start only when the command is non-destructive.
- If lint, typecheck, or tests fail but build succeeds, report the failure and
  keep demo path moving unless the failure affects the demo URL.
- Use safe smoke tests only: health endpoint, public landing page, read-only API,
  or screenshot of a non-authenticated page.
- Never smoke-test payments, email sending, AI generation, uploads, destructive
  mutations, or webhook triggers without explicit approval and sandbox config.
- For UI demos, check the primary page at mobile and desktop widths when browser
  tooling is available; report layout verification as incomplete when it is not.

For staging or production:

- Run the minimum gates, then load `references/advanced-gates.md` for deeper
  auth, data, AI, release, observability, privacy, and supply-chain checks.
- Require a dedicated secret scanner or repo-native equivalent before reporting
  staging-ready or production-ready.

### 5. Report

Every final answer must include verdict, scope classification, evidence,
blockers, fastest path, and what remains unverified. For staging or production,
include residual risk.

Load `references/report-templates.md` only when drafting the final response.
Use the shortest report that fits the scope.
Load `references/example-output.md` when an example verdict is needed for tone
or structure.

## Worked Examples

### URL-Only Demo

Input: "I need a URL for my prototype."
Checks: package metadata, build, production start or static preview, safe smoke.
Output: fastest host path, env names, blocker if any.

### Safe To Share

Input: "Is this safe to share?"
Checks: localhost leak scan, secret scan status, env scan, side-effect scan.
Output: pass, blocked, or incomplete.

### Env Inventory

Input: "What env vars do I need?"
Checks: docs, schemas, platform config, package metadata.
Output: grouped env names only.

### Deploy Failing

Input: "Deploy is failing."
Checks: reproduce exact failure first.
Output: root cause, one fix path, verification command.

### Incomplete Evidence

Input: "Can I send this to customers?"
Checks: repo inspected, but no scanner or smoke path available.
Output: incomplete, missing evidence listed, no readiness verdict.

## Private Learning Loop

After a real consult, create or update an anonymized note in the operator's
configured private note location outside this public repo when the user's
context makes it appropriate.

Use `templates/consult-note.md` as the note structure.

Capture:

- Exact customer language, verbatim when available.
- One proof artifact: command output, route, screenshot path, failing log, or
  shipped URL.
- One public-claim candidate that excludes names, companies, and confidential
  quotes unless explicitly approved.
- One objection or adoption blocker.
- Raw phrasing of the pain.
- Segment: solo founder, small team, agency, internal tool builder, other.
- Outcome: teammate demo, investor demo, beta user, paid pilot, client handoff,
  public launch.
- Blocker taxonomy: env/secrets, localhost leaks, auth callback, database,
  file storage, AI cost, webhook, background jobs, deployment platform, fear of
  breaking things, other.
- What they tried already.
- Technical proficiency: no-code, can run CLI, can configure CI, can operate
  Docker, or unknown.
- Existing infra/spend: free tier only, already pays for a platform, has cloud
  credits, or unknown.
- Decision maker: self, team, CTO, client, or unknown.
- Urgency and what breaks if they do nothing.
- Whether the solution felt valuable enough to pay for, ask later only when the
  interaction naturally supports it.
- Product insight for future tooling, kept separate from the user-facing report.
- Next outreach angle: the concrete phrase or offer that would have matched this
  user's pain.

Do not add private GTM notes to user-facing artifacts unless the user asked.
Always write the private note when the consult surfaces strong demand signal:
urgent deadline, repeated workaround, willingness to share, willingness to pay,
introduction offer, live URL shipped, or direct follow-up request.

For launch copy, use aggregated and anonymized patterns unless the user approves
specific names, companies, or quotes.

## From Consult To Case Study

Every consult note can become an anonymized proof asset. Capture enough evidence
to later write:

- what the builder tried before asking
- exact blocker or error symptom
- matching repeated pattern
- path chosen and why
- time from consult start to URL live, when known
- one sentence that can become public copy without confidential details

## Launch Copy Mode

When the user asks for launch copy about this service, read
`references/public-launch-copy.md`. Keep claims tied to verified artifacts or
clearly mark placeholders.

## Done Condition

End when one of these is true:

- Demo: URL is live or exact missing credential/command is identified, with a
  safe smoke result or manual-verification note.
- Staging: blockers, env plan, deploy path, and next 24 hours are delivered.
- Production: advanced gates are reviewed and residual risks are explicit.
- Specific blocker: root cause, fix, verification, or next command is clear.

Continue only when the user asks for the next phase.

## Red Flags

**Stop Everything**

- Secrets are committed, visible in frontend bundles, or exposed in logs.
- Auth relies on client state for private data or privileged actions.
- Live payments, email, webhook writes, AI spend, or destructive jobs can run
  during tests or demo without sandbox controls.
- Regulated or sensitive customer data is present without explicit approval.

**Block Non-Builder Access**

- Production paths depend on localhost, local tunnel URLs, local files, or a
  developer machine.
- No repeatable env schema.
- No repeatable data setup for an app with durable data.
- Uploads use local disk on an ephemeral host.
- Background work runs synchronously inside HTTP requests.
- No safe smoke path exists.

**Warn And Document**

- No logs, rollback path, owner, or budget guard.
- Secret scan incomplete because only keyword search ran.
- Tests, lint, or typecheck fail but do not block a demo URL.

## Boundaries

Make small fixes only when the user asked for implementation and verification is
available. Examples: add a health endpoint, document env names, fix a production
start command, remove hardcoded localhost from production config, or add a smoke
test.

Allowed focused fixes:

- Add or update `.env.example` from existing docs or redacted env names.
- Add a production start script that uses an existing server entrypoint.
- Add a health endpoint or smoke test.
- Remove hardcoded localhost from production config.
- Add minimal platform config from existing repo metadata.

Advise instead of editing when the work requires credentials, paid provider
choices, production data access, DNS, legal/compliance approval, or irreversible
external side effects.

Escalate before changing package managers, adding dependencies, modifying
database schema, touching DNS, creating paid resources, or using provider
accounts.

---
name: floom
description: Run and manage Floom AI workers — background automations that run on a schedule or trigger. Use when the user wants to set up recurring work (triage inbox, draft follow-ups, screen candidates, publish content) or asks about Floom workers, loops, or runs.
---

# Floom — set it once, let it loop

You are using HOSTED Floom (the cloud at workeros-api.floom.dev). Do NOT set up or configure self-hosting, do NOT create/edit a .env, do NOT run a local server. Everything runs on Floom's cloud; you only use the MCP tools.

Floom hosts AI **workers** you drive from here via MCP tools. A worker is a task that runs on a **schedule or trigger** without the user re-running it. Your job: help the user pick/set up a worker, give it a cadence, and monitor its runs.

## Your first worker
For a new Floom install, do NOT start from an open-ended worker idea. Drive the user to ONE immediate, read-only, manual run that returns useful output fast:
1. **Check connections** — call the available connection tools first.
2. **Prefer Gmail** — if Gmail is connected or quick to connect, create a Gmail Intake Brief worker.
3. **Fall back to GitHub** — if Gmail is not connected but GitHub is, create a GitHub Stalest PRs worker.
4. **Keep it manual** — create the smallest read-only worker with no schedule.
5. **Run now** — call `workers_run` immediately.
6. **Watch until done** — use `runs_get` / `runs_watch` / `runs_logs`; never claim success without a real completed run.
7. **Show output** — present the actual result the worker returned.
8. **Offer next steps after proof** — only after that first useful output, offer to schedule or refine. Scheduling is the second aha, not the first.

## After the first run (the bridge)
Once the first read-only worker has a real completed run and you have shown the output:
1. **Offer to schedule that worker** — ask if the user wants this same worker to run on a cadence. Only call `workers_update` after the user says yes.
2. **Bridge to the second worker** — ask: "what repetitive task do YOU want automated?" Offer this short menu of high-success next workers: Slack Unread Digest, GitHub Stale-PR Notifier, Summarize-a-URL, Daily Gmail Follow-up Drafter.
   - None of these? Ask the user what recurring task they want automated, then create the closest-matching worker from their own description. Pick the nearest template as a starting point, connect what it needs, keep it manual and read-only for the first proof run, and confirm before scheduling.
3. **Build and prove it the same way** — create the smallest manual worker for their pick, run it, verify a real completed run, show the output, then offer to schedule it.

## The loop
1. **Pick** — `workers_list` to see existing workers, or start from a template.
2. **Set up** — `workers_create` (new worker) · `workers_write_file` (edit an existing worker's source — do NOT use create to overwrite) · `workers_update` (settings: trigger_type = manual|cron|webhook, schedule, input defaults).
3. **Run** — `workers_run` for a manual run; or set a cadence via `workers_update` so it runs itself.
4. **Watch** — `runs_list`, `runs_get`, `runs_watch`, `runs_logs`.
5. **Approve** — a run that needs sign-off appears in `runs_list`; resolve with `runs_approve` / `runs_reject`. Nothing sensitive happens without approval.

## When a run fails
If a `workers_run` does not complete successfully, do not just say "it failed."
1. Fetch the failure with `runs_get`; use `runs_logs` when the root cause is not explicit.
2. State the root cause in one line: missing connection, bad input, auth failure, worker code error, or another concrete cause.
3. Give the exact fix: the connection to add, the input to change, the auth step to complete, or the corrected worker file.
4. Offer to re-run the worker immediately after the fix.

Never claim success for a failed run or a run whose final status is unknown.

## Tools you'll use
- **workers_** list / get / create / update / run / write_file
- **runs_** list / get / watch / logs / approve / reject / cancel
- **secrets_** list / set — credentials workers reference by name (write-only, never printed)
- **connections_** list — inspect connected OAuth apps such as Gmail, Slack, and HubSpot
- **connections_** add_mcp — register a custom MCP server connection; do not use it for OAuth app connects
- **contexts_** list / read / write — brain-pack files workers draw on at run time

(Tool names may appear with a dot in some clients, e.g. `workers.list`.)

## Rules of thumb
- A worker = **worker.yml** (config: trigger, schedule, inputs) + **run.py** or **SKILL.md** (what it does).
- Keep first-worker authoring small: one trigger, clear inputs, one observable success condition.
- Prefer read-only connection tools for the first proof run. Do not copy raw Gmail or customer data into worker logs unless the user explicitly asks for an audit trail.
- OAuth apps and MCP servers are different connection paths: use OAuth/app connect flows for Gmail, Slack, HubSpot, and similar apps; use `connections_add_mcp` only when the user is registering a custom MCP server URL/command.
- Prefer setting a **schedule** after the first useful manual run — that's the loop (set once, never run again).
- Never claim success until a run has completed successfully and you have checked details/logs.
- Reference secrets by name; never echo their values.
- Run `floom doctor` in the terminal if MCP/auth seems off.

Full CLI + MCP reference: https://floom.dev/docs

# /langfuse-bananza — launch the optimizer loop directly

One job: start the Langfuse optimizer `/loop` on a target, with no copy-paste
step. (v1 of this skill only copied the prompt to the clipboard because a skill
cannot type `/loop` for the user; since 2026-09-02 we invoke the `loop` skill
via the Skill tool with the filled prompt, which is the same thing.)

## Steps

0. **Check the Langfuse MCP is loaded.** If `mcp__langfuse__*` tools are
   available, skip straight to step 1.

   If they are NOT, the MCP is parked (it cost ~430 MB of commit in every open
   Claude Code session, and it is only ever needed here). MCP servers load only at
   session start, so it cannot be switched on mid-session. Tell the user to run,
   in a fresh terminal:

   ```
   cc-with langfuse
   ```

   then re-issue `/langfuse-bananza` there. That loads langfuse for THAT SESSION
   ONLY, on top of the normal servers - it does not write to the global config, so
   no other session picks it up and there is nothing to remember to switch off
   afterwards. Do NOT use `mcp-park.ps1 -On` for this; that one is global.

   Nothing is lost by it being parked: traces are written to Langfuse by the target
   script's own SDK instrumentation, and the MCP is only a read client, so the full
   trace history is there whenever it comes back.

1. **Resolve the target.** `TARGET` = the script path from args. If none given,
   ask ONE question: which instrumented script to optimize. The target MUST
   emit a Langfuse trace with per-step spans (pattern: `stripe-shopper/tracing.py`).
   If it does not, say so and offer to add a probe first (see
   `~/.claude/linkedin-poll/probe.py` as the model) - do not launch a loop on an
   un-instrumented script.
2. **Derive the rest** (do not ask unless truly ambiguous):
   - `RUN_CMD` = the interpreter next to the target (a `venv\Scripts\python.exe`
     in the target's folder if present, else the full-path Python312) + the target.
   - `RUN_NAME` = second arg if given, else the target's stem.
   - `STATE_FILE` = `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\loops\langfuse-optimizer\state-<stem>.json`
   - `INVARIANTS` = read the target's header/docstring; list the capabilities it
     declares must hold. If none are declared, leave blank (the loop infers and
     confirms in its report).
   - If a `launch-<stem>.md` exists in the loops folder, use its filled values
     verbatim instead (that file may carry extra analysis notes, e.g. what to
     exclude from timings).
3. **Launch**: call the Skill tool with `skill: "loop"` and `args` = the text
   below with the values substituted (no interval token, so it self-paces):

```
Follow the optimization protocol in C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\loops\langfuse-optimizer\GUIDELINE.md. TARGET=<...> RUN_CMD=<...> RUN_NAME=<...> STATE_FILE=<...> INVARIANTS=<...>. Do ONE full iteration per fire (capability-guard -> run -> fetch via Langfuse MCP -> analyze -> fix -> retest -> record). Never apply an edit that weakens an INVARIANT - skip it and log it as preserved. First fire: create STATE_FILE from state.template.json, set started_at. Continue until 2 consecutive iterations yield zero/almost-zero changes, OR 60 minutes elapse. Then write REPORT.md with TWO exact sections: (1) Changes - every kept edit with file/step, precise before->after, metric delta, and trace links (plus reverted/declined ones); (2) Preserved - each INVARIANT, confirmed still intact, and any optimization declined to protect it. If the Langfuse MCP is disconnected, HALT and ask me to reconnect - do not fall back.
```

4. Inside the loop, run each iteration through a **worker agent** (general-purpose,
   given GUIDELINE.md + the five params + "exactly one iteration, report ≤25
   lines") so the optimizer's tool noise never lands in the main context. The
   main session only records the verdict and decides whether to fire again.
5. Confirm in one line: target, run name, where REPORT.md will land.

## Notes
- Keep the prompt text in sync with `~/.claude/loops/langfuse-optimizer/launch-prompt.md`.
- Langfuse MCP must be connected (`/mcp`); the loop halts, never falls back, if it is not.

name: linkedin-outreach
description: Write Alessandro's 1:1 outreach messages (LinkedIn notes and DMs, and short cold emails) and optionally send them. # Outreach messages

**Read `examples.md` in this folder before writing a single word.** It holds messages Alessandro
actually wrote or approved, verbatim. Those are ground truth; this file is only a summary of them.
When they disagree, the examples win.

---

## The universal shape: ASK FIRST

His hard preference. The reason comes after the ask, never before it.

```
<Greeting>,
<who I am + what we build, one sentence>. <THE ASK>. <why this person, one sentence>.
```

One paragraph. **No blank line after the greeting** on LinkedIn. The ask lands in sentence two or
three, so a reader who stops early still knows what is wanted.

Anti-pattern (the old skill did this, he rejected it): identity, then a paraphrase of their CV,
then the ask trailing at the end.

## Length

LinkedIn: **300 characters including greeting and sign-off**, so the same text works as a
connection note or as a first DM. Cold email: still short, under ~120 words.

## Voice rules

- **Reciprocity, not supplication.** Always pair what he wants to learn with what he brings:
  "imparare da te ... e condividere la mia esperienza". Never "I have already spoken with a few
  people" as social proof, and never apologise for writing.
- **Say things the plain way.** No nominalised abstractions. "un background non-healthcare" beats
  "arrivato al front office sanitario dalla finanza".
- **Anglicisms are correct when writing to founders**, and only to founders: `student founder`,
  `fellow founder`, `healthcare`, `learnings`. "student founder" is **never translated**, in any
  language.
- **No em dashes.** Ever. Commas, full stops, or restructure.
- Exclamation marks and a parenthetical aside are in his register for peers. They are not for
  institutional figures.
- Never mention that they viewed his profile.
- Never lift their headline or job title verbatim. That reads as mail-merge.

## Naming his university

Decided 2026-09-02. Do not overthink it; he will correct case by case.

- **Recipient works in Germany → "TUM".** Short, and they know it.
- **Everyone else → "Technical University of Munich".** The word that carries the signal is
  **"Technical"**, so keep it. **"TU Munich"** is the acceptable short form when space is tight,
  which on a 300-character note it often is.
- **Never "CDTM"** outside Munich. Nobody outside Munich knows it.

## Choose the opener by what he actually has

1. **A mutual connection** → the referral goes FIRST, ahead of identity. It does the credibility
   work. See G3. Ask him who the referrer is if a mutual might exist and is not stated.
2. **A shared trait or school** → name it plainly ("anche te partivi da un background
   non-healthcare", "sono passato da HEC come te"). Only if it is first-party verified, never from
   an aggregator.
3. **Nothing shared** → one concrete, checkable thing they did. Not their title.

## The three use cases

### A. Peer / mentor outreach (founders, operators, ecosystem connectors)
The default when he wants to learn, not to sell. Register: peer, warm, tu with founders.
- Ask: **"Avresti 15 minuti per una chiamata?"** / "Would you have 15 minutes for a call?"
- His canonical reason-clause: **"vorrei imparare da te come vendere meglio a ospedali"**, paired
  with **"condividere la mia esperienza"**.
- With a warm intro, soften the ask: "mi chiedevo se ti andasse di chiacchierare" (G3).
- Say plainly what we build. Naming the product is fine here; these are peers, not survey subjects.

### B. Sales outreach (hospital buyers: biomed/clinical engineers, IT, procurement, direction)
Formal. This is coattio's territory: check the CRM first (`~/.medtech-crm/`, UI 127.0.0.1:4124)
and the tagged template library before writing anything new, and respect the single-`cible` rule in
`feedback_template_cible_single_persona`. Register per
`feedback_cold_email_operator_ask_for_advice`: student-founder framing, hold detail back, the ask
last, no traction stats beyond "we are negotiating our first pilots in Germany and Italy".
**This is the one case where the ask goes last**, because he is asking a stranger for their time in
a professional capacity, not talking to a peer.

### C. Research-interview outreach (experts as research subjects)
The original use of this skill. Never name a product or vendor: the moment it reads as vendor
research, anyone who already uses something goes quiet. Ask is 15 minutes and a few questions.

## Language

Write in the recipient's language, from their profile language and location. If their profile and
posts are in English, write English even if they sit in France or Italy.

**Italian.** `tu` + `Ciao <nome>` for founders and peers, which is the LinkedIn norm. `Lei` +
`Gentile <Nome Cognome>` for cold institutional figures: association presidents, hospital
directors, DGs, professors. Cold reach stays formal until a real conversation has happened
(`feedback_italian_practitioner_followup_style`). Sign off `Grazie,` or nothing at all on a short
peer DM. Use real accents: è à ù ì ò. Never `e'`.

**French.** Same split as Italian (decided 2026-09-02). **`tu` + `Salut <prénom>,` for founders and
peers**, which is what French startup people under 30 actually use on LinkedIn. **`vous` +
`Bonjour <prénom>,` for cold institutional figures**: association presidents, DGs, purchasing
directors, professors. Sign off `Merci,`. Space before `?` and `!`. Real accents always.

**German.** Real umlauts and ß, never ae/oe/ue/ss. Lowercase after the greeting comma. `Viele
Grüße`. Default `Sie`. LinkedIn sometimes shows names as "Surname Firstname", so check before
choosing the salutation.

**English.** `Hi <first name>,` and `Best,`.

**Names carry their accents.** Saâd, Valérie, Mallédant. Research agents strip them; recover the
accent from the LinkedIn slug (`sa%C3%A2d` → Saâd) rather than guessing, and if you cannot confirm
it, leave the name alone rather than inventing a diacritic.

## Ground the draft in his real corpus

`~/.claude/sent-corpus/corpus.jsonl` holds ~6,500 messages he actually sent, harvested daily.
Before drafting a non-trivial message, pull the closest rows and match their rhythm. Weight
same-language and same-audience rows heavily.

```bash
CORPUS="C:/Users/Alessandro/.claude/sent-corpus/corpus.jsonl"
grep '"lang":"it"' "$CORPUS" | grep -i 'founder\|startup'
```

**Known gap:** LinkedIn sends are NOT in the corpus (a phase-2 TODO in the harvester that was never
built). For LinkedIn, `examples.md` here is the substitute and is more authoritative than the
corpus. Fixing the harvester is the highest-value upgrade to this skill.

## Channel

- **Connection note:** 300 char cap. Free accounts get ~5 a month, so check before planning a batch.
- **DM after they accept:** the main channel, more room, does not read as automated.
- **InMail:** for out-of-network people; costs a credit, check the balance first.
- Never send the same text as a note and then again as a DM. Pick one.

## Sending

Follow `feedback_message_send_protocol`: "dsend" means send now, quoted text means send it verbatim,
anything else means draft and wait. On a batch, show the first draft and get explicit approval
before switching to a pause-and-send rhythm, and pause ~10 seconds before each send so he can
interrupt. Send via `mcp__linkedin-mcp__send_message`, then read back with `get_conversation` and
report the verbatim result. Space sends out; LinkedIn throttles bursts.

## Hard stops

- **Never enter an email address** to prove he knows someone. Close the dialog, skip, report.
- Never enter credentials or payment details anywhere.
- Skip anyone who does not fit the target. A wrong-fit message costs more than a skipped one.
- No Connect or Message button → skip and report, do not hunt for a workaround.
- Never invent a fact about the recipient. If it is not first-party, either leave it out or say on
  the card that it needs checking.

## Keeping this skill learning

Every time he rewrites or approves a message, **append it to `examples.md` verbatim in the same
turn**, with a one-line note on what it encodes. That file is the whole learning loop. Do not
rewrite the rules from a single example; add the example and let the pattern accumulate.

## Reporting after a batch

Who was messaged with role and company, who was skipped and why, how many notes or credits remain,
and anything that looked like a rate limit.

# Opinion research

Methodology for building practitioner-story corpora as filterable HTML dashboards. The worked example is the Lobbly IP-research project at `~/.claude/lobbly-research/`; scripts there are topic-agnostic where they can be and parameterizable elsewhere.

## Workflow

1. **Scope with the user.** Confirm:
   - Topic / hypothesis
   - Schema variant (see table below; default: pain)
   - Source preference (default: 80/20 in-house large-company per `[[practitioner-research-methodology]]`)
   - Platform list (defaults below)
   - Optional: named prospect map (companies to score-boost when mentioned)

2. **Set up working dir** at `~/.claude/research-corpora/<topic-slug>/` with `reports/` subfolder. (Or reuse `~/.claude/lobbly-research/` if continuing Lobbly research.)

3. **Dispatch parallel subagents — `run_in_background: true`.** One subagent per platform group. Each uses the prompt template at `references/dispatch-template.md`, filled with the topic + schema + scoring + output path. Each writes JSON to `reports/<corpus>-<platform>.json` in the agreed schema.

4. **Wait for completion notifications.** Do NOT poll. Each completion ping surfaces a brief summary; pass these to the user as they arrive.

5. **Merge + dedup.** Adapt `~/.claude/lobbly-research/merge_corpora.py` (pain) or `merge_solutions.py` (solution) — both are short and schema-aware. Dedup keys: `(name, first-80-chars-of-pain)` for pain, `(name, tool-name)` for solution, `(title, inventor-name)` for raw artifacts.

6. **Render to HTML.** Adapt `render_corpus.py` / `render_solutions.py` for per-corpus output, or `build_dashboard.py` for a unified multi-tab dashboard with filters (sidebar checkboxes + search + min-score slider).

7. **Memorize key insights** if the corpus is intended to persist across sessions. Save the corpus path and the working methodology variations as `project_<topic>.md` and link related entries with `[[brackets]]`.

## Schema picker

| Schema | When to use | Core fields |
|---|---|---|
| **Pain (4-part)** | "What hurts and what do they do to cope" | setting, pain (verbatim), workaround, what_would_help |
| **Solution (5-part)** | "What did they try, what worked, what didn't" — competitive landscape, abandonment patterns | setting, problem, solution_attempted, what_worked, what_didnt, outcome |
| **Raw artifact (varied)** | "How do people describe their work in the wild" — input-shape research for ingestion design | setting, title, raw_excerpt, description, format_messiness |

Full schema definitions with example JSON in `references/schemas.md`.

## Default platform tiers

- **Open web, zero setup** — Reddit JSON (append `.json` to any URL — no API, no app, no policy gate), public IP/legal/tech blogs, Hacker News (Algolia search API), GitHub READMEs, arXiv abstracts, Kickstarter, corporate engineering blogs.
- **Vendor case studies** — for solution-tried sweeps. Vendor-friendly bias is fine because the practitioner names + roles + employers + numbers are still on the record.
- **Cookie-walled, prime in Comet once** — IAM Magazine, gated Lexology, GreyB content portals. Reuse the shared profile at `~/.claude/travel-search/profile-comet/` (already past Perplexity onboarding).
- **Available helpers, not yet wired** — LinkedIn via the `linkedin-mcp` server (the ONLY LinkedIn surface; the `/linkedin-pw` Playwright skill was deleted 2026-05-27). Add as a 5th platform group when named in-house voices are scarce on open web.
- **Paid / membership** — PIUG, AIPLA Economic Survey, IAM full sub. Only after free tier saturates.

## Scoring (default)

- Base by role tier — in-house at named large co = 70, in-house generic = 55, named outside counsel/firm = 50, generic with verifiable byline = 40, anonymous claimed-credible = 25, anonymous generic = 15
- +10 if employer named; +15 if employer is on the user's prospect map
- +10 if schema completeness is "full" (all parts present)
- +5 if recent (current year)
- +5 if multiple distinct practitioners converge (recurrence)
- +10 if "abandoned" / "evaluated-but-rejected" with stated reason (rare, valuable)
- -10 if pure venting or pure marketing without specifics
- Tag `no-known-fix` when a clear pain has no workaround — the product-roadmap subset

## Reusable scripts (Lobbly project — generic where possible)

| Script | Role | Reuse |
|---|---|---|
| `reddit.py` | Reddit JSON fetcher: search/thread/user/top + 2s delay + 429 backoff | As-is, topic-agnostic |
| `sweep_reddit.py` | Batch fetch top threads with comments for a query × subreddit grid | Edit `QUERIES`, `SUBREDDITS` |
| `merge_corpora.py` | Merge `reports/stories-*.json` + dedup by (name, pain-prefix) | Pain corpora |
| `merge_solutions.py` | Merge `reports/solutions-*.json` + dedup by (name, tool) | Solution corpora |
| `render_corpus.py` | Single-corpus HTML cards with score-color border | Adapt to schema |
| `render_solutions.py` | Solution-card variant with outcome pill | Adapt to schema |
| `build_dashboard.py` | Unified multi-tab dashboard with sidebar filters | Edit data paths + tab list |

All in `~/.claude/lobbly-research/`. Read first before adapting.

## Pivot recipes (things that bit us in this session)

- **Reddit API policy-gate** silently breaks "Create App". Skip — use the public `.json` endpoints with a polite User-Agent and 2s delay. No app, no cookies.
- **Windows cp1252 encoding error** on JSON with emoji/Unicode. Use `path.write_bytes(json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"))` instead of `write_text`.
- **Comet first-launch onboarding** breaks `launch_persistent_context` because Playwright can't attach to `chrome://perplexity-onboarding/`. Reuse the existing `~/.claude/travel-search/profile-comet/` profile — already past onboarding.
- **Reddit 429** on a second consecutive sweep. Exponential backoff (15s → 30s → 60s → 120s) inside the fetcher, base delay 2s.
- **Long-running subagents end turn early** when a system-reminder fires mid-task. Always dispatch with `run_in_background: true`; wait for completion notifications, never poll the output file directly.

## Subagent prompt anatomy

See `references/dispatch-template.md` for the full templates. Key invariants for any platform:

- Pre-load the founder/user context (1-2 sentences) so the subagent knows what counts as on-topic.
- Define the exact schema as JSON, including `score` formula and `tags` vocabulary.
- Specify the output file path (predictable, schema-named).
- Give a concrete search strategy with example queries for that platform.
- Include the fallback: `bash ~/.claude/scripts/gemini-fetch.sh "URL"` for blocked URLs.
- Ask for a brief report at the end (counts, top 3, outcome distribution if applicable).

## Memory hygiene

After a successful run, save:
- `project_<topic>.md` with corpus path + hypotheses + named prospect map
- Update `[[practitioner-research-methodology]]` if you discovered a new scoring rule or a platform that surprised you
- Save dispatch-template tweaks back to `references/dispatch-template.md` here

---
name: overnight
description: Run a long/unattended agent task reliably — per-item checkpointing, an independent verifier loop, a hard token cap with circuit breaker, auth-work quarantine, fail-loud gates, and an honest report. Use for overnight batches, enrichment, audits, sweeps, or any run that must survive failures without burning budget silently.

# /overnight — reliable long-running runs

Built after a fire-and-forget run burned ~1.3M tokens and wrote nothing. It encodes the 2026 consensus (see `~/multi-agent-review-2026.md`): never let an agent grade its own work, the verifier is the bottleneck, write the done-condition first, checkpoint every item, hard budget + circuit breaker, trace the full run, keep auth work for daytime.

Engine: `harness.js` in this folder (a reusable Workflow script). This skill assembles a **run-config** and launches it.

## The reliability contract (always on)
- **Fail-loud gate** — 0 items → halts with `_HALTED.md`, never "succeeds" empty.
- **Per-item checkpoint** — each item writes `items/<id>.json` the moment it's produced; a crash costs 1 item, not the run. Resumable: a re-run skips items already `status:pass`.
- **Independent verifier** — a separate read-only agent checks each result against the spec; one bounded refine on failure. The generator never self-grades.
- **Hard token cap + circuit breaker** — pass `tokenCap`; once `budget.spent()` hits it, no new items start, and the run reports what's done. Works even with no `+Nk` directive.
- **Auth quarantine** — anything needing an authenticated/OAuth tool (LinkedIn, etc.) is queued to `_NEEDS-AUTH.md` for a daytime pass, never attempted unattended.
- **Honest report** — `REPORT.md` states real coverage, cap-hit, and next actions; no overstatement.

## How to run it
1. **Define the done-condition FIRST** — write the `verifySpec`: what a correct item must contain (every field, every claim sourced). This is the most important input.
2. **Assemble the item list** — `items: [{ id, data }]`. Prefer passing data in directly over a parse sub-step (parse flakiness is a top failure mode). `id` must be a safe filename slug.
3. **Write the generatePrompt** — instructions to produce ONE item's result from its `data`.
4. **Set `tokenCap`** — ask the user for a number if unsure; default 500000. Convert "stop at ~X% of my weekly limit" to a token number with the user (no tool reads live usage).
5. **Pick `outDir`** — a fresh run folder.
6. **Launch** the harness Workflow with `scriptPath` = this folder's `harness.js` and `args` = the run-config:
   ```
   Workflow({ scriptPath: "C:\\Users\\Alessandro\\.claude\\skills\\overnight\\harness.js",
              args: { runName, outDir, tokenCap, items, generatePrompt, verifySpec, rules, maxRefine } })
   ```
7. **On completion / interruption** — read `REPORT.md`. To resume after any failure, relaunch the SAME command; passed items are skipped via their checkpoints.

> GOTCHA (verified 2026-07-03): this runtime delivers the Workflow `args` global to the script as a **JSON string**, not an object. `harness.js` already `JSON.parse`s it. If you write another workflow script, do `const A = (typeof args === 'string') ? JSON.parse(args) : (args||{})` — otherwise `args.foo` is silently `undefined` (this caused two "empty/0-row" run failures before it was found).

## Rules of thumb
- Web-only by default. Keep authenticated/2FA work for a live session (it quarantines automatically).
- One strong verifier by default; only escalate high-stakes items to a panel (evidence shows naive many-judge/debate stacks can amplify bias).
- Bigger `tokenCap` ≠ better. Right-size to the item count.
- Never claim done without reading `REPORT.md` and spot-checking a checkpoint file.

# Product / demo feedback to Caleb

His ruling, 2026-10-02: "In the future, we're making this a workflow: I tell you to send prod
feedback/demo feedback to Caleb and you do it in this format (make it a card first so I don't send
shit)."

## The format, exactly

```
WHAT Happened:

WHY wrong:

How to replicate:
```

Three headings, in that order, his capitalisation. The screenshot goes with it, attached, never
described instead of attached.

## What each heading is for, and the way each one gets written badly

**WHAT Happened** is what was observed, in the order it was observed, with no cause in it. "I
clicked X, the page showed Y." If you find yourself writing "because", you are in the wrong
heading.

**WHY wrong** is what should have happened instead, and what it costs. Not "this is a bug": say
the expectation it broke. The cost is what makes Caleb rank it, so give the real one ("he could
not approve two emails that were ready, for five hours") and never inflate it.

**How to replicate** is a sequence another person can follow on their own machine. Numbered steps,
the starting state named, the exact values typed. If you cannot write this heading, say so in it
rather than guessing: "seen once, not reproduced" is honest and useful; an invented recipe wastes
his afternoon.

## Rules

1. **Card first, always.** Build the message, post it as a hub card, and stop. He approves. His
   words: "so I don't send shit". A draft that goes to Caleb unapproved is the failure this
   workflow exists to prevent.
2. **One observation per card.** Two bugs in one message get one answer and one of them dies.
3. **The screenshot is evidence, not decoration.** Attach the file. If he sent it on Telegram it is
   already on disk under `~/.claude/channels/telegram/inbox/`; read it first, so what you write
   matches what is in the picture.
4. **Read the picture before writing the card.** Several times the screenshot has carried the
   answer (a permission prompt, a button label) that the text around it did not.
5. **Quote him verbatim where he described it.** His words are evidence of what a user expected;
   your paraphrase is not.
6. **Never state a cause you have not verified.** If you looked at the code and found it, say
   where. If you did not, the WHY heading stays about the expectation, not the mechanism.

## Channel

Caleb is `caleb@tundrahealth.ai`. Check the last thread with him before choosing: if the
conversation about this product area is already running somewhere, reply there rather than
starting a new one. The card names the channel it proposes, so he can redirect it in one word.

For an email, the card is a real Gmail draft through the normal lane
(`gmail.py draft` then `_system/drafts/register.py`), not a chat message pasted into a card.

## Both machines

This skill is installed on the box (`~/.claude/skills/prod-feedback/`) and on the laptop, and the
copy of record lives in `task-land/_system/skills/prod-feedback/` so neither machine can lose it.
His instruction, same minute: "Make this a thing on laptop too, not just vps."

# SDE Curriculum Source (`/sde`)

Ingest one of Alessandro's own projects into the curriculum app's backlog as
project-tagged example material. The curriculum app lives at
`C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\curriculum-server` (localhost:4117); see
[[feedback_curriculum_tracker]].

This is the "drop into a Raw folder" step for his OWN tools (distinct from the
daily CDTM-Eng chat crawl). Per his instruction (2026-06-15) it mines **both**
the code and the build session.

## Steps

1. **Resolve the project.** Determine the project directory:
   - If the user named a project/path, use it. Common roots: `C:\Users\Alessandro\Coding\<name>`,
     `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\skills\<name>`, or wherever the current session has been working.
   - If ambiguous, list the plausible candidates and ask which (one short question).
   - The project `--name` defaults to the directory's basename; keep it stable across re-ingests (dedup keys on it).

2. **Find the build session(s).** The build-session transcripts are JSONL files in
   `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\projects\C--Users-Alessandro\`. Find the ones that
   actually built this project by searching for the project path or name:
   - Use the Grep tool over that directory for the project folder name (and/or an
     absolute path fragment). Collect the matching `*.jsonl` files.
   - If the user is currently building it, the active session counts too — include
     the most recent matching transcript(s). Pass up to ~3; more just adds noise.
   - If none match, proceed code-only (omit `--session`) and say so.

3. **Make sure the server is up** (the script doesn't need it, but the review UI does):
   check `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 4117`; if nothing, run `start.ps1`.

4. **Run the ingest** from the curriculum-server dir:
   ```
   node ingest-project.mjs --path "<projectDir>" --name "<name>" --session "<jsonl1>" --session "<jsonl2>"
   ```
   (Drop `--session` entirely for code-only.) It walks handmade source (skipping
   node_modules/.next/dist/build/lockfiles/minified/generated), pulls substantive
   build-session excerpts, classifies both against all topics, and appends PENDING
   items tagged `kind:"project"`, `project:"<name>"` to `data/inbox.json`, and
   registers the project in `data/sources.json`.

5. **Report** the counts (files, session excerpts, matches, new items) and point him to:
   - **Backlog tab** → the `Project: <name>` section, where new/weak-topic items are
     flagged and sorted first ("suggested first"), and
   - **Projects tab** → the per-project view of topics he's enriched.
   Surface `localhost:4117` only here because this turn is about the tracker
   (NO standing footer — see [[feedback_curriculum_tracker]]).

## Notes
- Re-running is idempotent: dedup is on `(project, origin, topic)`, so re-ingesting
  after adding files only adds the new matches.
- Approving a project item attaches it to the topic as an example with
  `project:"<name>"`, which is what populates the Projects view.
- The review itself (deciding which items to approve, prioritizing new/weak topics)
  happens in the app; the app already pre-sorts and flags. When he asks you to do a
  "project review" / "backlog review", read `data/inbox.json` + `data/curriculum.json`,
  and recommend the project items whose target topic is `untouched` or low
  `strength` first.

# Slack Skill (cdtm + xplore)

## CRITICAL: Sending Rules

**NEVER send without explicit confirmation.** Wrong-channel or wrong-user
sends leak confidential info.

- Discussion / drafting / brainstorming is NOT a send instruction. Only
  "send", "send it", "go", "yes" counts.
- Always run `send` dry-run first, show the resolved channel/user, wait for
  confirmation, then re-run with `--confirmed`.
- Same content-aware confirmation rules as the `wa` and `triage` skills.

## Workspaces

| Slug     | Account                          |
|----------|----------------------------------|
| `cdtm`   | alessandro.sodano@cdtm.com       |
| `xplore` | alessandro.sodano@tum.de         |

Token files: `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\tokens\<workspace>.json`.

**Auth method in use: browser cookie.** Both CDTM and XPLORE block custom-app
installs without admin approval, so the helper authenticates via the
`xoxc-` user token + `d` cookie that the user's browser already holds.

If a token expires (`not_authed` / `invalid_auth` from any call):

1. User opens https://app.slack.com/client/ in their browser, logged in to
   both workspaces.
2. F12 -> Console -> paste:
   ```js
   copy(JSON.stringify(Object.values(JSON.parse(localStorage.localConfig_v2).teams).map(t => ({name: t.name, domain: t.domain, token: t.token})), null, 2))
   ```
   Paste clipboard back to Claude.
3. F12 -> Application -> Cookies -> https://app.slack.com -> copy the `d`
   cookie value (URL-encoded, ~300 chars, starts `xoxd-`).
4. Claude re-runs:
   ```powershell
   python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.py auth-cookie `
     --workspace <cdtm|xplore> --token 'xoxc-...' --cookie 'xoxd-...'
   ```

The OAuth-based `auth` subcommand exists too, but only works on workspaces
that allow self-installed Slack apps (neither cdtm nor xplore do).

## Reading

```powershell
# DM unreads + @-mentions (lookback in hours, default 24)
slack.cmd unread --workspace cdtm --hours 24
slack.cmd unread --workspace xplore --hours 24

# Read recent messages from a channel/DM
slack.cmd read --workspace cdtm --channel "#general" --limit 20
slack.cmd read --workspace cdtm --channel "@alice"  --limit 10
slack.cmd read --workspace cdtm --channel C0123ABC  --limit 50

# Full Slack search syntax (from:, in:, has:link, after:YYYY-MM-DD, etc.)
slack.cmd search --workspace cdtm --query "from:@boss after:2026-05-01"
```

`slack.cmd` lives at `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.cmd`. The full
path is required unless that folder is on PATH; default to using the
absolute python invocation in scripts:

```powershell
python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.py <subcmd> ...
```

## Sending

### Step 1 — dry run (always first)

```powershell
python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.py send `
  --workspace cdtm --to "@alice" --text "ping"
```

The script prints the resolved channel ID, a human-readable label
(`#general`, `DM with user U0123`, etc.), and the text. Nothing is sent.

`--to` accepts:
- a Slack channel ID (`C...`, `D...`, `G...`)
- `#channel-name` for public/private channels
- `@username` (resolves against `name`, `display_name`, `real_name`, or email)

### Step 2 — show user, wait for explicit confirmation

Echo the resolved label + text in a compact block. Wait for "yes", "send",
"go", or equivalent.

### Step 3 — send

```powershell
python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.py send `
  --workspace cdtm --to "@alice" --text "ping" --confirmed
```

Successful send prints the channel + ts.

### Threaded replies

When replying inside an existing thread, pass `--thread-ts <parent-ts>`.
Get `parent-ts` from the `ts` field of the parent message returned by
`read` or `search`. Same dry-run / confirm rule applies.

```powershell
python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.py send `
  --workspace cdtm --to "#general" --thread-ts "1715000000.123456" `
  --text "agreed" --confirmed
```

# /snm — Signal-to-Noise Maximizer quick access

User invocation: `/snm $ARGUMENTS`

Parse `$ARGUMENTS` and route per the table below. Everything else: read `~/.claude/snm-receiver/state.json` for current state.

## File and command refs

- State: `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\snm-receiver\state.json`
- Paused sentinel: `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\snm-receiver\paused`
- CLI: `snm <focus|resume|scan|status|show>` (global shim at `~/.local/bin/snm.cmd`)
- Gmail helper: `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\gmail.cmd`
- WA state file (for mark-done on WA items): `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\wa-state.json`
- Slack send (rare for /snm): `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.cmd`

## Routing

Match `$ARGUMENTS` semantically (loose, not regex-strict):

| User said | Action |
|---|---|
| (empty) or "list" or "show" or "what's there" | List current actionable items per Display rules below |
| "urgent" or "gimme urgent" or "just urgent" or "anything urgent" | List only items where `urgent === true`. If none, say "no urgent items" |
| "status" | Run `snm status` and print its output |
| "focus" or "pause" or "off" or "dnd" or "stop" | Run `snm focus`. Confirm with "Focus mode ON. Background scans paused." |
| "resume" or "on" or "start" or "back on" | Run `snm resume`. Confirm with "Resumed. Next scan in ≤20 min." |
| "scan" or "scan now" or "refresh" or "force scan" or "rescan" | Run `snm scan` (synchronous, ~3 min). Then print updated list. |
| "N done" / "consider N read" / "N not urgent" / "mark N done" / "done N" / "drop N" | Look up item N in state.json. Mark it done in its source system (see Mark-done section). Then update state.json to drop that item and decrement `actionable_count` (and `urgent_count` if applicable). |
| "<freeform query>" not matching above | Treat as a natural-language question about current state.json — answer directly. Never fabricate items not in state.json. |

## Display rules

Apply the rules from `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Alessandro/memory/feedback_triage_display.md`. Each item is a 3-line block. Use these emoji color codes for the Src:

| Source | Code | Emoji |
|---|---|---|
| WhatsApp | `W` | 🟢 |
| Gmail cdtm | `C` | 🔴 |
| ~~Gmail lobbly~~ | `L` | retired 4 Oct 2026 (old state.json items only) |
| Slack cdtm | `SC` | 🟣 |
| Slack xplore | `SX` | 🟣 |

Format per item:

```
#: N · <EMOJI> <SRC> · <from> · <time if available, else omit>
Snippet: **<full snippet text from state.json>**
Why: <urgency_reason if urgent, else short one-liner inferred from source/from>
```

For urgent items, **prefix the line with ⚠** so they pop:

```
⚠ #: 1 · 🔴 C · CDTM All Hands · 
Snippet: **One last reminder ...**
Why: Deadline May 15 EOD (2 days)
```

Header line above the list:
- If items > 0: `ACTIONABLE (N total, M urgent) — last scan <scanned_at>`
- If 0: `Inbox clear ✓ — last scan <scanned_at>`
- If `paused: true`: `Focus mode ON — scans paused. Run /snm resume to re-enable.`

If `scanned_at` is older than 45 min, append ` (stale)` to the header line.

## Mark-done section

When user says "N done" / "consider N read" / similar for item N:

1. Read state.json, find `actionable[N-1]` (0-indexed lookup).
2. Based on `source` field:
   - **`Gmail cdtm`**: search Gmail to resolve the thread ID — `gmail.cmd search --account cdtm --query "<distinctive snippet phrase>"`. Pick the first matching thread. Then `gmail.cmd label --account <X> --thread-id <id> --remove Label_1,UNREAD --add Label_3` (Label_1=triage/action, Label_3=triage/done — same in both accounts).
   - **`WhatsApp`**: parse the WA daemon JID — query `triage.js --hours 24 --json` once, find the chat where `label` matches state.json's `from` field, get its `jid` and `lastIn.timestamp`. Append to `wa-state.json#done` with that `lastInTimestamp` and a short note like "via /snm".
   - **`Slack cdtm DM`** or similar: no separate state file. Just removing from state.json is enough — the next scan won't see it again unless new activity arrives.
3. Rewrite state.json to remove the item from `actionable[]` and decrement counts.
4. Confirm to the user: e.g., "Marked #2 (group: MPD BMW - ALERTS) done. 5 left."

If user says "N not urgent": same as marking done — the item leaves the list.

If multiple items at once ("1 and 3 done"): apply both, then print one summary line.

## Edge cases

- State.json missing or unparseable: `No scan yet. Run /snm scan to populate.`
- `paused: true`: still allow `list` and `urgent` queries (show last-known state with `(paused)` note). Don't auto-resume on `scan` — make the user explicit.
- Stale (>45 min since `scanned_at`): show with `(stale)` warning, don't error out.
- User asks something like "/snm what about Caleb?": search `actionable` for items whose `from` contains the keyword, show those. If none, say "Caleb not currently in actionable list."
- **`errors[]` in state.json**: if non-empty, print errors as a banner ABOVE the actionable list. Example: `⚠ Last scan errors: <message>`. Most common case = WA daemon was flapping or stale during the scan window, meaning OUT msgs from your phone may not be captured (see `[[feedback_wa_daemon_flap_and_outgoing_gap]]`). When surfacing WA items in this state, hint: `for full thread context use /triage — /snm shows only the surfaced classifier output, /triage fetches live and shows last-4-msg arc per DM.`

## DO NOT

- Don't run a fresh scan unless user explicitly asks (it costs tokens + 3 min).
- Don't apply markdown pipe-tables — they render badly in this terminal (use the 3-line block format).
- Don't fabricate items not in state.json.

## DO (shared state)

- `~/triage/state.json` is the SHARED cutoff for /triage, /snm, SNM cron, AND informal "what's new" checks. SNM `scan.ps1` updates it automatically after each scan. When `/snm scan` is invoked, it goes through `scan.ps1` so the cutoff advances there too. See `[[feedback_shared_inbox_cutoff]]`.
- When user marks an item done via /snm: also apply per-channel "considered read" (Gmail label, wa-state.json#done) so `/triage` won't re-surface it.

# /telegram-switch <laptop|vps>

Moves the Telegram daemons between machines. Both sides hold the same tokens
(`~/.env/tg-bridge.env`, `~/.env/sodanotif.env`) and since 2026-09-01 run the
SAME source files (paths are env-overridable), so a switch is pure start/stop.
NEVER let both sides run the same bot: tg-bridge would hit a getUpdates
conflict; two sodanotif daemons would double every card.

Covered: **tg-bridge** (@Soda2402_bot phone→claude pipe), **sodanotif**
(WA tap → classify → TG cards) and, since 2026-09-05, the **channel plugin**
(@Claudio_al_TG_v3_bot, the savior's own chat). WhatsApp daemons are NOT
touched - each machine keeps its own linked device.

## The channel plugin (Claudio v3) - VPS owns it since 2026-09-05
The savior lane lives on the box (tmux `savior`, `claude --channels
plugin:telegram@claude-plugins-official`, state dir
`/home/da/.claude/channels/telegram`). The laptop copy of the same token is
`~/.claude/channels/telegram-v3` and must stay STOPPED: two pollers on one
token means 409, and the loser dies for good instead of retrying.
- Stop the laptop side: kill the `bun server.ts` child of the laptop savior
  (`~/.claude/channels/telegram-v3/bot.pid`).
- Start the box side: `ssh da-box /home/da/.local/bin/box-sessions.sh` (the
  sanctioned attach-or-create launcher, also on cron every 5 min; `restore-savior`
  calls it). Then verify `pgrep -a bun` shows `bun server.ts`, and that the newest
  `~/.cache/claude-cli-nodejs/-home-da/mcp-logs-plugin-telegram-telegram/*.jsonl`
  ends in `Channel notifications registered`.
- Do NOT pass a prompt argument to a `--channels` claude: it exits instantly.
  Same for any pipe/redirect (`> log 2>&1` makes it print-mode and die).

### Box-wide "inert by default" (set 2026-09-06, user-approved)
The box now defaults `TELEGRAM_STATE_DIR` to the TOKENLESS
`/home/da/.claude/channels/telegram-null`, so nothing on the box can steal the
savior's poller. Set in three places: `DefaultEnvironment=` in
`/etc/systemd/system.conf` (covers every unit, `.bak-20260906` alongside), a soft
default in `/home/da/.bashrc` + `.profile`, and a `TELEGRAM_STATE_DIR=` line at the
top of da's crontab. **The ONE override lives in `box-sessions.sh`** on the savior's
`tmux new-session` line (`.bak-20260906` alongside): that is the only process
allowed to poll. The `workbench` session inherits the null dir and is inert by
design. If you ever write a new savior launcher, it MUST carry that override or the
lane silently never starts - verify with
`tr '\0' '\n' < /proc/<savior-pid>/environ | grep TELEGRAM_STATE_DIR`.

SSH: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/da-vps_ed25519 root@100.85.52.84` (alias: box).

## → vps (the default home)

**State as of 2026-09-06: the box owns tg-bridge AND the channel plugin.** The
laptop's tg-bridge had been left running alongside the box's `da-tg-bridge` unit
since it was enabled, so @Soda2402_bot was 409-conflicting every 6s and the
phone→claude pipe was a coin flip between the two. Laptop side stopped 2026-09-06
via `stop.ps1` (task `TG-Bridge-Autostart` was already Disabled); the box journal
went quiet immediately. If the pipe ever feels flaky, check for exactly this: two
listeners, `journalctl -u da-tg-bridge | grep 409`.

1. Laptop OFF first:
   - `powershell -File ~/.claude/tg-bridge/stop.ps1`
   - `Disable-ScheduledTask TG-Bridge-Autostart`
   - Stop sodanotif: kill node processes matching `sodanotif`, then
     `Disable-ScheduledTask SODANOtif-Daemon-Watchdog` and (stop, then disable)
     `SODANOtif-Watch`. Leave `SODANOtif-Recap` alone (sender, no conflict).
2. Box ON: `systemctl enable --now da-tg-bridge.service da-sodanotif.service`
3. Verify: `journalctl -u da-tg-bridge -n 2` shows "tg-bridge up as @Soda2402_bot";
   `journalctl -u da-sodanotif -n 2` shows "daemon up". Laptop has zero
   node processes matching tg-bridge/sodanotif.

## → laptop

1. Box OFF first: `systemctl disable --now da-tg-bridge.service da-sodanotif.service`
2. Laptop ON:
   - `Enable-ScheduledTask TG-Bridge-Autostart` then
     `powershell -File ~/.claude/tg-bridge/start.ps1` (spawns keep-awake -
     expect the command to linger; verify via `tg-bridge/bot.pid` instead)
   - `Enable-ScheduledTask SODANOtif-Daemon-Watchdog, SODANOtif-Watch` then
     `powershell -File ~/.claude/sodanotif/start-daemon.ps1`
3. Verify: laptop `tg-bridge/bot.log` tail shows "tg-bridge up"; box units
   inactive.

## Gotchas
- A just-stopped listener's long-poll can hold the bot for up to ~50s; if the
  new side logs a 409 Conflict, wait a minute - do NOT bounce it repeatedly.
- **Any `claude -p` spawn without `--strict-mcp-config` kills the channel
  poller.** The plugin boots inside that short-lived run, takes the poller lock
  (`telegram channel: replacing stale poller pid=N`) and drops it seconds later
  when the run ends; the savior's lane is then dead until restarted, and
  messages sent meanwhile are swallowed. Found 2026-09-05: `sodanotif/daemon.js`
  classifier ran many times an hour like this (fixed there). STILL UNGUARDED:
  `sodanotif/bot.js:273` and `tg-bridge/bot.js:432` only add the flag when
  `mcp.load` is false. If the lane dies again, check these first.
- The laptop copies of bot.js/daemon.js/sources/wa.js are the canonical source;
  if they were edited since the last switch, re-copy them to the box
  (`scp -r ~/.claude/tg-bridge root@100.85.52.84:/home/da/` etc., re-chown da)
  before starting the box side.
- Always report which machine now owns which bot, with the verify evidence.

# /todo: the to-do pipeline

The contract is `C:\Users\Alessandro\task-land\_system\PIPELINE-WORKER.md` (box: `/home/da/task-land/_system/`).
Read it first, every time: it is short and it is the source of truth. This file only says how to start.

## Start

1. `python C:/Users/Alessandro/task-land/_system/pipeline.py list --bucket today` (add `--stage created` to see
   only what nobody picked up). With an argument (`/todo <words>`), work that one to-do; with none, go through
   Today top to bottom.
2. For each to-do decide, before touching it: what final state does he want, and can it be reached without him?
   Imagine what a very knowledgeable system would do in front of it. A to-do that needs him in person, his
   money, or a decision only he can take stays created: say so in one line and move on.
3. `pipeline.py pickup <slug> --note "<what I am doing>"`, do the work inside the system that owns it, then
   `pipeline.py ready <slug> --summary-file <file> --note "<what is ready>"`. One card per to-do.

## The rules that bite

- **Drafts: check they are not already there** before writing one (`gmail.cmd find-draft --account <a> --to <x>
  --subject "..."`); `gmail.cmd draft` refuses with `EXISTING DRAFT` when one exists. Load the `drafting` skill
  before writing to a person, register through the lane, never send.
- **A call is a CRM row, not a to-do line:** find the number, then `pipeline.py call <slug> --name "..."
  [--phone ...]`.
- **A yes/no list for him** is a keyboard review board (`~/gtm-eng/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`), never a wall of text.
- The summary card: decision in the first line, then what was done, where it lives (links), what is left for
  him. At most 58 lines. Read the `hub` skill before posting any other card.
- Could not finish: `pipeline.py release <slug> --why "..."`. Never leave a to-do in progress with nobody on it.
- Several to-dos that do not depend on each other: one background agent each, but the MAIN thread keeps working
  and never ends its turn waiting on them (memory `feedback_unattended_run_never_wait_on_agents`).

## Report

One line per to-do: picked up and ready (card #N), left created (why), released (why).

# /tovps — migrate the current session to the DA VPS

Moves the session you are IN right now onto the always-on box (`100.85.52.84`,
tailscale `da-box`/`100.85.52.84`), where it keeps running in tmux with remote
control active, so the user picks it up on their phone (Claude app → **Code**)
with the laptop fully off.

**Mechanics proven 2026-08-24:** transcripts are portable; `claude --resume <id>`
on the box rebuilds the session from the copied `.jsonl`. ALWAYS pass an initial
prompt to `--resume` — promptless resume can die on transcripts with a stale
deferred-tool marker ("No deferred tool marker found").

## Steps (do them in one flow, no questions)

1. **Session id** — take the UUID segment from your own scratchpad directory
   path (`...\claude\<project-slug>\<SESSION-UUID>\scratchpad`). Fallback: the
   most-recently-modified `.jsonl` in the current project dir under
   `~/.claude/projects/`.

2. **Pick the box cwd** — map the laptop cwd to what exists on the box:
   - under `task-land` → `/home/da/task-land`
   - under `vault_kb` → `/home/da/vault_kb`
   - under `medtech-brain` → `/home/da/medtech-brain`
   - anything else → `/home/da`
   Compute the box project slug from it: replace every `/` with `-`
   (`/home/da/task-land` → `-home-da-task-land`).

3. **Copy the transcript** (laptop project dir may differ from the box slug —
   that is fine, the file goes into the BOX slug dir):
   ```bash
   scp -i ~/.ssh/da-vps_ed25519 ~/.claude/projects/<LAPTOP-SLUG>/<ID>.jsonl \
       root@100.85.52.84:/tmp/<ID>.jsonl
   ssh -i ~/.ssh/da-vps_ed25519 root@100.85.52.84 \
     'mkdir -p /home/da/.claude/projects/<BOX-SLUG> && \
      mv /tmp/<ID>.jsonl /home/da/.claude/projects/<BOX-SLUG>/<ID>.jsonl && \
      chown -R da:da /home/da/.claude/projects'
   ```

4. **Resume in tmux** — session name `mig-<first 8 of ID>`; NO pipes/tee on the
   claude command (a pipe makes it print-and-exit instead of interactive):
   ```bash
   ssh -i ~/.ssh/da-vps_ed25519 root@100.85.52.84 \
     'sudo -u da tmux new-session -d -s mig-<FIRST8> \
      "cd <BOX-CWD> && exec claude --resume <ID> \"Migrated from the laptop via /tovps. You are now on the DA VPS (da-box). Laptop-only files are unavailable; task-land, vault_kb and medtech-brain are synced at /home/da/. Reply with one line on where the work stands, then continue or stand by.\""'
   ```

5. **Verify** — sleep ~20s, then
   `sudo -u da tmux capture-pane -t mig-<FIRST8> -p` and confirm the model
   actually answered (its one-line handoff reply is visible) and the status bar
   shows `/rc active`. If the pane is gone, read what it printed (relaunch with
   `2>&1 | tee /tmp/mig-<FIRST8>.log` ONCE to see the error, then fix — usually
   a wrong id or slug).

6. **Report** — tell the user: session is live on the box; on the phone open
   Claude app → **Code** → the session appears once it has the handoff reply;
   from a laptop later:
   `ssh -i ~/.ssh/da-vps_ed25519 da@100.85.52.84 -t "tmux attach -t mig-<FIRST8>"`.
   Then STOP WORKING in the laptop session — from that moment the two copies
   diverge and the box copy is the live one. Do not keep executing new work
   here after the handoff is confirmed.

## Caveats to state when relevant
- Only files that exist on the box are reachable there (the three synced repos
  + whatever the box owns). A session mid-way through laptop-only work (Outlook
  COM, browsers, AHK, local servers) migrates its CONTEXT but not those tools.
- MCP servers from the laptop are not on the box; the resumed session has the
  box's toolset.
- The laptop transcript stays behind unchanged; the migrated copy accumulates
  its own history on the box from the fork point.
