# Triage (multi-account, label-as-source-of-truth)

## Architecture

**Helpers** (each has its own SKILL.md or memory reference for full setup):

- **Gmail**: `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\gmail.cmd` (PowerShell shim → Python 3.12 + `gmail.py`). Custom OAuth for `cdtm` (lobbly retired 4 Oct 2026, his ruling: dead; gmail.py refuses it by name). The `.cmd` shim bypasses the Microsoft Store python alias. Full reference: [[reference_triage_gmail]]. Claude.ai's Gmail MCP is NOT used.
- **WhatsApp**: daemon at `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\` owns the Baileys connection; read via `node triage.js --hours N --json`, send via `node send.js`. Aliases auto-populated; group blocklist via `wa-mute.js`. Full setup: `~/.claude/skills/wa/SKILL.md`. Daemon liveness/flap handling covered in step 2 below.
- **Slack**: `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\slack\slack.cmd` (shim → `slack.py`). OAuth user tokens for `cdtm` + `xplore`. Three output sections: `dm_recent`, `mentions`, `channel_recent`. Channel blocklist in `slack/mute.json` — apply client-side in this skill (no upstream filter yet); DMs never filtered. Full setup: `~/.claude/skills/slack/SKILL.md`.

**State separation:**

- **Gmail**: labels are the truth (`triage/action` = fresh, `triage/todo` = postponed (carries forward), `triage/done` = handled, no label = auto-deprioritized / re-evaluated each round). No local `todos.md`.
- **WA**: `wa-daemon\wa-state.json` (no native WA label system). Schema in "WA state file" section below.
- **Slack**: piggybacks on the shared cutoff — no separate Slack state file yet.
- **Shared cutoff** (cross-channel, cross-tool): `~/triage/state.json` holds `last_check_at` / `last_check_unix` (legacy aliases: `last_triage_at` / `last_triage_unix`). Updated by `/triage` round end, SNM cron scans, `/snm scan`, and any informal "what's new" inbox check. Every check reads AND advances it. See [[feedback_shared_inbox_cutoff]].
- **SNM surfaced list** (cross-tool): `~/.claude/snm-receiver/state.json#actionable` holds items pre-classified by 20-min background scans. `/triage` merges these into its candidate set (dedupe by source-id). After round end, items the user handled (or auto-done) are cleared; carryover stays.
- **Accounts:** `cdtm` (tokens at `~/triage/tokens/<account>.json`; `lobbly` retired 4 Oct 2026, token renamed `lobbly.json.retired-20261004`)

## Hard rules

1. **ALWAYS end with AskUserQuestion (locked-in 2026-05-18, re-enforced 2026-05-21).** After printing the actionable list (step 5), you MUST immediately call AskUserQuestion with ONE question per item (N items → N questions; max 4 per call, batch + re-prompt if N>4). Never return control to the user without it. **If you find yourself about to type "Actions?" as a freeform prompt and stop, STOP — you missed this rule.** Full per-item option layout in step 6. The "Actions? e.g. ..." line in the displayed list is a reminder caption ABOVE the AskUserQuestion chips, not a substitute. *(The pre-2026-05-18 freeform-batch mode is gone — when N=0 ("Inbox clear ✓") just return, no AskUserQuestion needed; that's the only exception.)*
2. **Send is dry-run by default.** Always show dry-run, wait for explicit "go"/"send", THEN re-run with `--confirmed`.
3. **Total output before user input: ≤50 words plus the list itself.**
4. **Multi-account by default.** Default scope = every triage account (today only `cdtm`; lobbly retired 4 Oct 2026).
5. **Skip non-actionable items silently** — never print them, never label them.
6. **Use `--thread-id` on sends** so replies join the original thread, not a new conversation.
7. **Hard time window — 24h ceiling (PER-ITEM POST-FILTER, NOT JUST FETCH SCOPE).** Effective cutoff = `max(last_check_at, now - 24h)`. The fetch is just a coarse filter — you MUST re-apply the cutoff per item after fetching, checking the latest INCOMING timestamp (NOT internalDate, NOT thread bump time). Drop any item whose latest incoming is `< effective_cutoff`, regardless of `is:unread`, `repliedSinceLastIncoming`, Gmail's `after:` operator returning it, or any other signal. Gmail's `after:` filters by thread internalDate (latest msg, including auto-bumps and label changes) — it CAN return threads whose last incoming is older than 24h. Sole exception: explicit carryover (Gmail `triage/todo`, WA `wa-state.json#todo`). At round end, ALWAYS rewrite `~/triage/state.json#last_check_at` to the current run's timestamp. *(Bug 2026-05-21: a Prof Kolisch reply from 05-18 14:38 (>2 days old) was surfaced because Gmail `after:1779289165` returned the thread despite the incoming being older. Post-filter is mandatory.)*
8. **One-shot preservation window.** The user's IMMEDIATE next message after the actionable list is the only chance to flag items (`todo`, `defer`, `reply: ...`, etc.). Any other message (silence, unrelated request, meta-comment, "ok") implicit-dones EVERY shown item. No retroactive "todo" for items shown a round ago. Never re-show.

## Recipe

### 0. Read behavioural notes (prioritisation prior)

Before fetching, read `~/task-land/agent-notes.md` — the SAME behavioural log `/daily` maintains. It records how you actually handle things over time. Use it silently to inform classify + order (steps 3–6):
- Senders/groups you consistently skip or auto-done → deprioritise; suggest `mute` if a group keeps recurring as noise.
- People you reply to with care (you heavily edit the suggested draft, or always reply) → pre-compose more carefully, never auto-skip.
- Items you defer repeatedly → surface near the top with their prior context.
- Time-of-day / day-of-week handling patterns → tune how aggressively you trim.

Never echo these notes to the user; they are a silent prior, not output.

### 1+2. Fetch everything in one call

**ONE PowerShell call.** The wrapper reads all state files, resolves Gmail label IDs, dispatches all 7 fetches in parallel (Gmail Query A + B × cdtm + WA debrief + WA flap check + Slack cdtm + Slack xplore), THEN auto-enriches: pulls per-thread `From`/`Subject`/`latest_incoming_unix` for every fresh Gmail thread (so the 24h post-filter can apply) and per-chat bursts for every WA group with `count > 1`. It also applies the 24h post-filter (Rule 7) inline before returning. Output is one ~50–200 KB JSON blob:

```
node C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\fetch-all.js
```

**Invoke via the PowerShell tool, NOT the Bash tool.** Bash on Windows routes through Git Bash / MSYS, which mangles backslash Windows paths: `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\fetch-all.js` becomes `C:\Users\Alessandro\UsersAlessandrotriagefetch-all.js` and node fails with `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. This applies to every node/python/.cmd invocation in this skill that uses a `C:\...` path. PowerShell is the default Windows shell here; treat Bash as off-limits unless the command is shell-builtin only.

Or via the .cmd shim: `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\fetch-all.cmd`. Flags: `--no-enrich` (skip per-item context fetches; for fast first look), `--scope gmail|wa|slack` (narrow), `--hours N` (override window).

**Bundle schema** (relevant fields you'll actually use):

```jsonc
{
  "now": { "unix": ..., "iso": "..." },
  "cutoff": {
    "last_check_unix": ..., "last_check_at": "...",
    "effective_unix": ..., "effective_iso": "...",  // 24h post-filter pivot
    "hours_since_last": <1..24>
  },
  "snm_carryover": [...],   // SNM background-scan items pre-classified
  "snm_paused": <bool>,
  "gmail": {
    "cdtm": {
      "label_ids": { "action": "Label_1", "todo": "Label_2", "done": "Label_3" },
      "todo": [{ id, snippet, ... }],     // carryover
      "fresh": [{                           // Query B, post-24h-filtered & enriched
        id, snippet, subject, from,
        latest_incoming_unix, latest_incoming_snippet,
        within_24h
      }],
      "fresh_dropped_24h": [...]            // dropped by post-filter, kept for audit
    }
  },
  "wa": {
    "ok": <bool>,
    "daemon_pid_alive": <bool>,
    "daemon_heartbeat_age_sec": N,
    "flap": { "disc5m": N, "replaced60m": N, "lastReason": "..." },
    "state": { "todo": {...}, "done": {...} },   // wa-state.json contents
    "items": [{ jid, label, isGroup, lastIn, repliedSinceLastIncoming, count, burst: [...] }],
    "items_dropped_24h": [...]
  },
  "slack": {
    "cdtm": { "ok": <bool>, "dm_recent": [...], "mentions": [...], "channel_recent": [...] },
    "xplore": { ... }
  },
  "errors": [{ "source": "...", "err": "..." }]   // per-fetch failures, soft
}
```

**Handle the bundle:**

- `wa.daemon_pid_alive === false` OR `wa.daemon_heartbeat_age_sec > 300` (or null): WA daemon dead/disconnected — the `WA-Daemon-Watchdog` scheduled task will restart within 5 min. Warn `WA daemon dead/disconnected — proceeding without WA` and skip WA items.
- `wa.flap.disc5m >= 3` OR `wa.flap.replaced60m >= 1`: print the flap banner BEFORE the actionable list (Rule 7 — outgoing-msg gap means `repliedSinceLastIncoming` lies):
  ```
  ⚠ WA daemon flapping (X disconnects in last 5 min, last reason: <reason>). Outgoing msgs from your phone may not be captured — verify replies before marking done. Likely cause: duplicate daemon process. Run `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='node.exe'" | ? CommandLine -like '*wa-daemon*'` to find duplicates; keep PID listed in daemon.pid, ask user before killing the other.
  ```
  Trust `repliedSinceLastIncoming` less; per-chat burst becomes the primary signal.
- `errors[]` non-empty: degrade gracefully — surface the channels that succeeded, mention which failed (e.g., "Slack xplore auth expired — skipped").

**Do NOT re-issue any fetch the wrapper already did.** No more `gmail.cmd search`, `gmail.cmd list-labels`, `gmail.cmd get` (for the fresh threads), `node triage.js`, `node check-flap.js`, `node show-thread.js` (for groups with count>1), or `slack.cmd unread` in the same round — that's all already in the bundle. Per-item fetches still allowed for: deeper Gmail thread context (`gmail.cmd get` on a TODO carryover that wasn't enriched), Slack `read` on a specific channel, WA `show-thread` for items the wrapper didn't burst (e.g. DMs with `repliedSinceLastIncoming === true` — those are skip candidates anyway).

The wrapper's post-filter already drops `fresh_dropped_24h` items — they're stored separately for audit, do NOT classify them. Carryover (`gmail.cdtm.todo`, items in `wa.state.todo`) bypasses the cutoff per Rule 7.

**Manual fallback** (only if `fetch-all.js` fails or is missing — should never happen in normal flow): see `## Manual fallback (legacy)` at the bottom of this file for the per-helper commands.

### 3. Classify silently

#### 3.1 Gmail Query B results

For each Query B thread, decide actionable using snippet + subject only (no `get_thread` yet — saves tokens).

**Actionable** = ANY of:

1. Would affect what Alessandro does next (changes plans, requires action, info he'd want for a real upcoming commitment), even if not directly addressed to him.
2. Mentions a deadline that regards him.
3. Project update for a priority project (see "Priority projects" in [[context_user_life]]: BMW / MPD BMW, CDTM Onboarding TF, xplore, thesis). These always-flag, regardless of who sent it or how the latest msg looks.

**Time window:** any real commitment, however far out. A June booking decision, a 3-week deadline, a 2-month-out event all count if they require action. (The 24h ceiling in Rule 7 governs what we FETCH; this rule governs what we SURFACE within that fetch.)

`triage/todo` is reserved for items the *user* explicitly postponed via "N todo".

**Not actionable** (skip silently — DO NOT print, DO NOT label):

**FIRST read `~/triage/skip-rules.md`** — your declared type-skip policy, the single source of truth for "this TYPE never matters". Apply every rule there as authoritative. The categories below are the baseline rationale; when you state a NEW type to skip, it goes into skip-rules.md (step 7e proposes the line), not buried here.

- Newsletters, marketing, promotions, automated digests, calendar acks, receipt notifications
- **Appointment / meeting booking confirmations — NEVER actionable.** Subjects starting with `Appointment booked:`, `Booking confirmation:`, `Invitation:` (auto-generated by Calendly, Cal.com, Google Calendar, unternehmertum.de booking pages, Microsoft Bookings, etc.) OR bodies containing `Reserviert von`, `You have been invited`, `An invitation has been sent`. The booking IS the action — the email is just a receipt. Form-prompt phrases the booking page bakes into the confirmation body (e.g. `Please include 2-3 agenda points and a goal for the meeting.`) are NOT real asks; they're the page's instructions to the booker shown back as a reminder. The agenda goes inside the booking workflow (or is sent separately if needed), never triggered by the confirmation email. *(Rule added 2026-05-23 after Moritz Krüsselmann unternehmertum.de booking confirmation surfaced — the "Please include 2-3 agenda points" line was a form-prompt echo, not a request.)*
- **Automated-sender invitations** — `From:` matches `noreply@*`, `no-reply@*`, `donotreply@*`, `notifications@*`, `welcome@*`, `invite@*`, `invitations@*`, `hello@*`, `notify@*`, `team@<saas>`, etc. EXCEPT: surface ONLY if the body contains a deadline that affects an account/service Alessandro already actively uses (e.g., a Slack-Pro trial-ending notice for a workspace he runs). A 3rd-party platform invite (RealBee, Notion, Linear, etc.) "inviting him via X" is NOT actionable just because X is a priority topic — priority-topic flag only triggers when the sender/channel IS the project, not when the project name is mentioned in marketing body. *(Rule added 2026-05-18 after RealBee misfire: noreply@realbee.dk was flagged because xplore was in the body.)*
- FYI cc'd messages with no question for him
- Threads where Alessandro was the last sender
- Community-broad asks (job postings, surveys, event announcements not addressed to him personally)
- **Reactions / closures** — emojis, "ok", "thanks", "noted", "rimaniamo in contatto", single-word acks. EXCEPTION: before skipping a reaction/closure as the LATEST msg in a burst, check the earlier msgs (via `show-thread.js`) — if there's an earlier actionable msg the user hasn't acted on, surface THAT one. The classifier reads the burst, not just the tail.
- **Group banter** — sports talk, link shares, generic chitchat with no question for him.
- **Past-tense / moot questions** — "can someone do X today at <time>?" where the time has passed; "who's leading the Y call?" after the call. The question is moot. (LLM judgment from text; no calendar lookup needed.)
- **Per-contact noise rules** from [[context_user_life]] — e.g. Renata Arlaud's image-only messages.

#### 3.2 WA results

Existing WA classification logic — repliedSinceLastIncoming, group-vs-DM, MPD BMW always-flag, Renata image rule, etc. See "WA state file" section below.

**Group burst fetch (mandatory before classifying):** the daemon's `text` field only exposes the LATEST message in a chat, even when `count > 1`. Classifying a group based on a single line ("Ok", a reaction, etc.) misses the actual content above it. For every group item with `count > 1`, BEFORE classifying, call:
```
node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\show-thread.js --jid <chatJid> --limit <count>
```
Read the full burst, THEN apply closure/banter/actionability judgment. The same fetched burst is reused in step 5 for the display snippet (join with ` / `).

**Hard skip rules (apply BEFORE any LLM judgment):**

- `status@broadcast` — always skip.
- `Renata Arlaud` image-only messages — always skip (see `context_user_life.md`).
- **`group: Me ` (jid `120363023505333085@g.us`) — ALWAYS skip, regardless of content.** Never actionable. (User confirmed 2026-05-11. See `feedback_triage_group_me_skip.md`.)

**Mandatory pre-display filter:** every candidate WA item MUST be cross-checked against `wa-state.json#done`. Drop the item if EITHER:

1. **Exact-or-stale done:** `done[chatId]` exists AND `done[chatId].lastInTimestamp >= currentChat.lastIn.timestamp` (equality counts — same timestamp means it was processed last round). OR
2. **Closure-tail short-circuit (added 2026-05-22):** `done[chatId]` exists with `lastInTimestamp < currentChat.lastIn.timestamp` AND the LAST 1–3 IN messages in the burst are all closures (pure reactions like `[reaction: ...]`, stickers, voice-note acks under 2s, single-word/single-emoji replies — "ok", "thanks", "got it", "ahh good tip", "👍", "❤️"). A closure tail means the user already saw and informally acknowledged the burst on their phone, even when substantive content sits in the middle. Surface only when the tail itself is substantive (fresh question, ask, deadline, non-trivial statement). The cleanup rule in step 4 already preserves the done entry in this case; pre-display must mirror that or the item resurfaces inconsistently.

Missing this filter is the #1 cause of re-surfacing noise.

3. **Read-on-phone (added 2026-05-22):** Each item carries `unreadCount` from `chats-state.json`, populated by `daemon.js` from **two signals**:
   - **(a) Baileys `chats.update`** — `chat-utils.js:639` emits `unreadCount: 0` when WhatsApp's app-state sync delivers a `markChatAsReadAction` from your phone. Server-mediated and EVENTUAL: can lag minutes-to-hours when WhatsApp batches app-state pushes. Triggered by `case 'server_sync'` notifications (`messages-recv.js:314`).
   - **(b) Outgoing-message fallback** — `daemon.js#ingest()` forces `unreadCount: 0` whenever it captures a message with `fromMe: true` (recorded as `readVia: "outgoing-message-fallback"` in `chats-state.json`). Near-real-time: replying on phone clears unread within seconds. Stronger signal than (a) anyway — you can't reply without having read.

   Drop the item if `unreadCount === 0` regardless of which signal set it. If `unreadCount === null` (daemon hasn't seen either signal for this jid yet — typical right after a daemon restart or for quiet chats), treat as inconclusive: fall through to the other rules, do NOT use the closure-tail short-circuit alone unless `unreadCount` actively says `> 0`. The pure read-without-reply case relies on signal (a) alone, so expect it to lag — when in doubt, surface, the user can mark done.

**unreadCount confidence ladder:**
- `unreadCount === 0` AND `unreadStateUpdatedAt` is recent (≤ 24h): user read on phone — drop.
- `unreadCount > 0`: user has NOT cleared on phone — surface, BUT see rule 4 below before deciding what to display.
- `unreadCount === null`: no signal — fall through to rules 1 + 2 + classification.

**Note for older chats:** chats-state.json starts empty after the 2026-05-22 daemon deploy. For chats with no recent `chats.update`, `unreadCount` stays `null` until Baileys re-emits state. Expect some lingering false positives on quiet chats for the first few days post-deploy; mark `done` to suppress.

4. **Unread-tail slicing (added 2026-05-23 after Lara miss):** when `unreadCount > 0` AND `unreadStateUpdatedAt` is recent (≤ 24h) AND `unreadCount < count` (i.e. fewer unread than total IN messages in the burst), the FRESH portion is only the **last `unreadCount` IN messages**. The earlier IN messages in the burst are already read on phone — possibly already replied to from phone too (the daemon's outgoing-sync hole means `lastOut` can be days stale even when the user replied minutes ago; see [[feedback_wa_daemon_flap_and_outgoing_gap]]).
   - **Closure-tail short-circuit (extended):** if the last `unreadCount` IN messages are ALL closures (stickers, single emoji, reactions, single-word acks like "ok"/"thanks"/"got it"), DROP the item — the user already engaged with the substantive content on phone, the closure is decoration. This fires REGARDLESS of `done[chatId]` (rule 2 above was the original `done[]`-keyed version; this is the unreadCount-keyed generalization).
   - **Display rule (extended, supersedes step 5's "all-window" snippet rule for these items):** when surfacing, the snippet must show ONLY the last `unreadCount` IN messages (plus interleaved OUT messages from within that span if any). Do NOT include earlier already-read IN messages — they make the item look fresher and more substantive than it is. The earlier messages can go on a `Read on phone: <count>` caption line above the snippet if context helps, but never in the bolded body.

*(Concrete miss 2026-05-23 — "Lara" item: `count: 4, unreadCount: 1`, burst was 3 substantive 16:52 messages + 1 sticker at 18:15. User had already replied "Yess nw" at 14:26 from phone (daemon never captured the outgoing). The 1 unread = sticker = pure closure. Rule 4 would have dropped this silently. Instead I rendered all 4 lines with sticker bolded, which read like a fresh substantive thread.)*

#### 3.3 Slack results — three tiers

Apply `slack/mute.json` filter FIRST (drop `channel_recent` and `mentions` entries whose `(workspace, name)` matches a blocked entry, case-insensitive, `#` prefix optional). DMs always pass through.

Then classify by tier:

- **`dm_recent` (DMs and mpim group DMs):** **always include** as a candidate. The peer is messaging you directly. LLM judgment only to drop pure-emoji closures or "thanks!" wraps that don't need replies (same closure rule used for WA).
- **`mentions`:** **always include**. Someone explicitly tagged you. Even if it's in a noisy channel, the tag is a personal signal. LLM judgment can de-prioritize (sort lower) if the mention is a passing reference, but don't drop.
- **`channel_recent`:** include only if LLM judges actionable per message — most channel activity is ambient (announcements, group discussion not directed at you). Default: skip silently. Surface only if a message contains a direct question or request that plausibly needs your action.

### 4. Auto-label new actionables (Gmail) + state cleanup (WA)

**Gmail:** before labeling, for each Query B candidate that LOOKS actionable from the snippet, run `gmail.cmd get --account <acct> --thread-id <id>` to fetch headers. **Two reasons to fetch before labeling:**

1. **Confirm direction.** Snippets can begin mid-message ("Hi Roderick, We would love to..."). The actual `From:` header reveals whether it's incoming (someone wrote to Alessandro) or outgoing (Alessandro's own message landing in inbox via BCC/forwarding — e.g. mail from his `alessandro.sodano@hec.edu` account auto-delivered to lobbly, retired 4 Oct 2026). Outgoing-self threads are NOT actionable. Skip silently, do NOT label.
2. **Get FROM + SUBJECT for the display** (step 5). Always show real sender (e.g. `David M. Huber <david.m.huber@tum.de>`) and real subject (e.g. `Re: CDTM Lecture Visit`), never inferred from snippet body.

For each thread that's confirmed-incoming AND classified actionable, in parallel:
```
gmail.cmd label --account <acct> --thread-id <id> --add <action_label_id>
```

(Carryover threads from Query A already have action OR todo label — they were validated when first labeled. Re-fetch only if displaying. Auto-deprioritized Query B threads stay UNLABELED — do NOT label them anything.)

**WhatsApp state cleanup** (run BEFORE display, mutates `wa-state.json`):

Read `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\wa-state.json` (create if missing with `{"todo": {}, "done": {}}`). For each entry, cross-reference against the current `triage.js` JSON:

- For each `done[chatId]`: if `currentChat.lastIn.timestamp > entry.lastInTimestamp` → newer incoming arrived, **delete the entry** (item re-surfaces as NEW).
- For each `todo[chatId]`: if `currentChat.repliedSinceLastIncoming === true` → user replied outside the integration (e.g., from phone), **delete the entry** (item resolved itself).

Write `wa-state.json` back. Now the state file reflects current truth.

### 5. Print the combined list (Gmail + WA)

**Build candidate set:**

- **SNM carryover (step 2's second read):** items in `~/.claude/snm-receiver/state.json#actionable` already pre-classified by background scans since the last `/triage`. Include all of these — they passed SNM's classification rules (which mirror /triage's). They're typically the bulk of the list when running `/triage` after some background activity.
- Gmail items: Query A (carryover, action+todo labels) + Query B (newly classified actionable, just labeled action).
- WA items from `triage.js` JSON. Filter:
  - DMs (`isGroup === false`): include if `repliedSinceLastIncoming === false` AND chatId NOT in `wa-state.json#done` (after cleanup). DMs in `wa-state.json#todo` ARE included (they're carryover, displayed as TODO).
  - Groups (`isGroup === true`): include all (the group blocklist is already applied by `triage.js` server-side; trust it). Use **per-item LLM judgment** to drop reactions, banter, FYI noise — only keep items that look like they need your action. Same `wa-state.json` filtering applies.

**Dedupe:** an item can appear in both SNM's surfaced list AND a fresh fetch (e.g., a Gmail thread SNM saw 10 min ago, still unread+actionable now). Dedupe by source identity:
- Gmail: thread ID (SNM's state.json may not store thread ID; match by `from` header + first 60 chars of snippet as a fallback).
- WA: `chatJid`.
- Slack: `channel_id` + `ts` (or `peer` for DMs).
If SNM saw it AND a fresh fetch finds it, treat as one item — display once.

Sort: TODO items first (Gmail todo-labeled + WA todo entries — oldest postponement at top), then NEW items oldest-first across all sources.

**Format — 3-line block per item with emoji color codes. NON-NEGOTIABLE.** Full rule set in [[reference_triage_operations]] section 1 (read when display drift suspected — flagged 5+ times historically). Load-bearing essentials below.

Skeleton:
```
ACTIONABLE (N) — since <ISO>

#: 1 · <emoji> <Src> · <From> · <Time>
Snippet: **<full text verbatim, never truncated>**
Why: <short reason>

Actions? e.g. "1 done, 3 reply: text, 4 todo"
```

**Source codes + emojis:**

| Channel | Src | Emoji |
|---|---|---|
| WhatsApp | `W` | 🟢 |
| Gmail cdtm | `C` | 🔴 |
| ~~Gmail lobbly~~ | `L` | retired 4 Oct 2026 |
| Slack cdtm | `SC` | 🟣 |
| Slack xplore | `SX` | 🟣 |

**From column:** Gmail = real `From:` header (fetched via `get_thread`, never inferred from snippet); WA DM = contact name; WA group = `group: <name>`; Slack DM = peer name; Slack channel/mention = `<sender> / #<channel>`. Append `(N)` for chats with >4 messages (e.g., `Caleb (17)`).

**Time:** `HH:MM` if today, else `MM-DD HH:MM`. Use latest incoming timestamp.

**Snippet rules — NEVER truncate or summarize:**
- **WA DMs (`isGroup=false`):** show ALL messages within the effective cutoff window (`max(last_check_at, now - 24h)`) — incoming AND outgoing — oldest-first, each prefixed `← HH:MM` (in) or `→ HH:MM` (out). Newest IN bolded with `**...**`. Do NOT include older context messages from outside the window — they're noise; the user only cares about what arrived since last check. Always fetch via `show-thread.js` (NEVER trust the daemon's `text`/`lastIn.text` field for display — it collapses multi-part messages like `[document] + caption` into a single line, hiding text the user sent alongside an attachment). Use `--since-unix <effective_cutoff_unix>` to filter to the window:
  ```
  node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\show-thread.js --jid <chatJid> --since-unix <effective_cutoff_unix>
  ```
  (Fallback if `--since-unix` not supported: `--limit 30` and drop entries older than the cutoff client-side.) If zero `→` lines AND the daemon-flap banner fired in step 2, append `(⚠ no OUT msgs in store — daemon may have missed your replies)` to the snippet.
- **WA groups (`isGroup=true`) — MANDATORY sender prefix.** A group burst without sender names is useless; the reader can't tell who said what. ALWAYS re-fetch via `show-thread.js --with-sender` (added 2026-05-23), NEVER use the burst already embedded in the fetch-all bundle — that bundle's burst lines lack senders. Show all messages within the effective cutoff window (`max(last_check_at, now - 24h)`), oldest-first, each formatted `← HH:MM <pushName>: <text>` (or `→ HH:MM <text>` for outgoing). Newest IN bolded with `**...**`. Join multi-message bursts with ` / ` only when collapsing to a single snippet line; prefer the multi-line form for groups so each sender is clearly visible. Command:
  ```
  node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\show-thread.js --jid <chatJid> --since-unix <effective_cutoff_unix> --with-sender
  ```
- **Gmail, Slack:** latest incoming VERBATIM, bolded. Multi-message burst: all consecutive short messages joined by ` / `. **Document/media + caption rule (WA groups + DMs):** whenever the latest IN is `[document: ...]`, `[image]`, `[video]`, or `[voice note]`, the daemon may have a sibling text message in the same burst that's the actual content (e.g. "Pagina 32 e colonna viola a pagina 33" alongside the PDF). Always check the full burst from `show-thread.js`, not the collapsed `text` field.

**Urgent items:** prefix `#:` with `⚠ `.

**TODO carryover** (Gmail `triage/todo` OR WA `wa-state.json#todo`): prefix Src with `TODO ` (e.g., `TODO W`). Sort TODO items first.

Internally track `(channel, account-or-chatId, threadId-or-ts, current_state)` for each item so step 6 applies the right transition.

If N=0: `Inbox clear ✓`. Done.

**Concrete worked example:**

```
ACTIONABLE (3) — since 2026-05-13T15:52:24Z

#: 1 · 🟢 W · Caleb (17) · 17:48
Snippet:
  ← 16:05 3min
  ← 19:27 https://webticketing3.cinestar.de/
  ← 19:33 https://www.linkedin.com/safety/go/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fjobs%2Eashbyhq%2Ecom%2Fspherecast%2F...
  **(latest IN, link share)**
Why: co-founder, day-long DMs, latest IN is FYI link — likely residual

#: 2 · 🟣 SC · perrotc (6) · 05-12 17:34
Snippet: **Sure no problem :) / Looking forward to talk to you / It does not show a google meet link for me in the invite / Maybe it was filtered out / I just send you a teams link / The updated invite did not yet go through**
Why: meeting coordination, 6 unread msgs, NOT closure

⚠ #: 3 · 🔴 C · CDTM All Hands · today
Snippet: **One last reminder to submit your commitment for CDTM's future funding by May 15 EOD via allhands2026.cdtm.com**
Why: Deadline May 15 EOD (2 days)

Actions? e.g. "1 done, 2 reply: ..., 3 todo"
```

### 6. Parse one user reply

**Default UI is AskUserQuestion (locked in 2026-05-18).** AFTER printing the actionable list (step 5, unchanged — same colored 3-line blocks, same source codes), immediately call AskUserQuestion with ONE question per item (N items → N questions, max 4 per call; if N>4, batch the first 4 and re-prompt after). Each question is the item summary (truncate to fit). Options per item, fixed order:
1. **Done** — mark as read/handled this round
2. **Todo** — capture to vault inbox (`~/task-land/Tasks/inbox/`) AND mark source done so triage stops re-surfacing it. Item is now tracked by `/today` / `/inbox` / `/done`, not by /triage.
3. **<your suggested reply>** — label = "Send my suggested reply"; PRE-COMPOSE a 1-2 sentence reply per item and put it in the option's `description` field so the user sees it on focus. For non-reply items (FYI updates, info-only), this option is "Acknowledge with: ..."  
4. (auto "Other") — user types freeform: their own reply text, "suggest" to ask for a draft, "skip", etc.

The displayed actionable list stays ABOVE the AskUserQuestion — never replace it. Both formats together: classic colored list (for context) + CLI chips (for fast action).

When the response comes back, parse per item:
- "Done" → implicit-done semantics (step 7b)
- "Todo" → preserve, see Todo verb below
- "Send my suggested reply" → use the pre-composed text as `reply: <text>` (dry-run + content-aware confirmation, see step 7)
- Freeform text → if it reads as message body, treat as `reply: <text>`; if it reads as a verb (`todo`, `done`, `mute`, `suggest`, `skip`), apply the verb table below.

Verb table (applies to freeform replies and to fallback batch-reply mode if AskUserQuestion isn't appropriate). Each token is `<N> <verb>[: <text>]`.

**Vault integration for `todo` (added 2026-05-20):** The `todo` verb no longer carries items inside the source channel. Instead it captures the item into the vault inbox AND marks the source done, so the item lives in `~/task-land/Tasks/inbox/` (visible to `/today` and `/inbox`) and /triage stops nagging. Capture helper:
```
C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe ^
    C:\Users\Alessandro\task-land\_system\capture.py ^
    --title "<inferred summary>" --source <see table> --project "" --dest inbox
```
Title inference: Gmail = `<Subject> — <From>`; WA DM = `<contact>: <snippet first 60 chars>`; WA group = `<group name>: <snippet first 60 chars>`; Slack DM = `<peer>: <snippet>`; Slack channel/mention = `<sender> in #<channel>: <snippet>`. Project left blank — user re-tags via `/inbox`.

**Gmail items** (`c` or `l` channel):

| Verb | Action |
|---|---|
| `todo` / `defer` | Capture to vault: `capture.py --title "<Subject> — <From>" --source gmail-<acct>`. THEN remove `triage/action`/`triage/todo`, add `triage/done`, remove UNREAD (same as `done`). Item now lives in `~/task-land/Tasks/inbox/`; will NOT re-surface in /triage. |
| `done` / `read` / `dismiss` | Remove `triage/action` AND/OR `triage/todo`, add `triage/done`, remove UNREAD. Gone from future debriefs. |
| `suggest` | `get_thread` → compose polished body in chat → wait for user (`approve`/`edit: ...`/`cancel`) → on approve, run `gmail.cmd draft` with `--thread-id` → print draft summary → wait for explicit `send the draft` → `send-draft --confirmed` → remove action, add done, remove UNREAD |
| `reply: <text>` | `gmail.cmd draft` with `--thread-id` and user's text (auto-fetches parent, auto-quotes, sets In-Reply-To/References) → print draft summary + rendered body → wait for explicit `send the draft` (or `edit: ...`/`cancel`) → `send-draft --confirmed` → remove action, add done, remove UNREAD |
| `skip` | No action |

**WhatsApp items** (`w` channel):

| Verb | Action |
|---|---|
| `todo` / `defer` | Capture to vault: `capture.py --title "<contact-or-group>: <snippet 60ch>" --source wa`. THEN add entry to `wa-state.json#done` (same as `done`). Item now lives in `~/task-land/Tasks/inbox/`; will NOT re-surface in /triage even if newer incoming arrives (user is consciously tracking it in vault). |
| `done` / `read` / `dismiss` | Add entry to `wa-state.json#done`: `{chatId, lastInTimestamp: currentLastIn.timestamp, doneAt: <ISO>}`. Remove from todo if present. Item re-surfaces only when a NEWER incoming arrives. |
| `suggest` | Read recent thread messages from `triage.js` JSON (already includes recent messages context per chat) → compose polished reply body in chat → wait for user (`approve`/`edit: ...`/`cancel`) → on approve, dry-run via `wa-daemon\send.js --jid "<chatJid>" --text <body>` (always `--jid`, never `--to "<name>"` — see reply verb) → wait for explicit `send it` → `--confirmed` → daemon will capture outgoing within seconds → next debrief naturally drops it. |
| `reply: <text>` | **ALWAYS send via `--jid` from the bundle, NEVER `--to "<name>"`.** The chat's `jid` field is the unique identifier already in hand; name lookup re-introduces an ambiguity error class that doesn't need to exist (e.g. "Lara" matches both Klara UR and Lara CDTM, fails before send). Dry-run `wa-daemon\send.js --jid "<chatJid>" --text "<text>"` → show JID + body → wait for explicit `send it` (content-aware confirmation, NOT just "go") → `--confirmed`. Daemon picks up outgoing; item drops naturally next run. |
| `mute` (groups only, optional `: reason`) | `node wa-daemon\wa-mute.js add <chat-jid>` (or with `--reason "..."`). Removes from current debrief AND adds to blocklist so the group's items never resurface. Use when a group's noise consistently outweighs its signal. |
| `skip` | No action. Item reappears next debrief if conditions still met. |

Unknown verb: ask once, terse. Example: `Item 3 verb 'foo' unknown — what action?`

### 7. Execute in batch

Where independent, parallelize tool calls. For `suggest` and `reply: ...`, batch all proposed drafts in one message and wait for batch approval like `send the draft 3, edit 5: make shorter, cancel 7`.

**Sending:** for every `reply: ...` and `suggest` action: `gmail.cmd draft` → print summary → user confirms with content-aware phrase (`send the draft`) → `gmail.cmd send-draft --confirmed`. The two-step shape is required because (1) plain `send` doesn't reliably thread on recipient's side, (2) the permission hook blocks `send-draft` without content-aware confirmation (bare "go"/"ok" rejected).

After SENT, do the label transitions (remove action label if any, add done, remove UNREAD).

### 7b. Implicit done for everything else shown this round

**Default semantic:** any item shown to the user this round and NOT explicitly preserved in the user's IMMEDIATE next message is treated as read/handled. Apply done at end of round. Items dropped this way are NEVER re-shown — no retroactive recovery.

**Preserve verbs** (keep an item alive for next round): `todo`, `defer`, `preserve`, `keep for later`, `keep this for later`, `save for to-do`, `save this for my to-do`, or any phrasing that semantically reads as "hold for next round". **Everything else — including silence, "ok", a meta-comment about workflow, an unrelated request, `done`, `read`, `reply: ...`, `suggest`, `skip` — drops the item.**

**One-shot rule:** the user gets exactly ONE reply slot — the message right after the list. If they reply about something else (e.g., "fix the WA bug first"), every item in that list still implicit-dones. They cannot come back two messages later and say "1 todo."

- **Gmail items:** for each shown item not in user's `todo` list, apply `triage/done` label and remove `UNREAD`. (No need to ever apply `triage/action` — that label is now obsolete in the round-based model.)
- **WA items:** for each shown item not in user's `todo` list, append to `wa-state.json#done` with `lastInTimestamp = currentChat.lastIn.timestamp`. Cleanup logic (newer-incoming → drop) still applies on next round.

This is the "auto-done" behavior the user asked for: by default, what's shown is treated as seen. Only `todo`/`preserve` keeps an item alive for next round.

For WA replies (which the user explicitly handled with `reply: <text>`), still apply done to the chat (the conversation is resolved on user's side; the daemon will see `repliedSinceLastIncoming = true` shortly anyway, but the done entry guards against any sync lag).

### 7c. Auto-add unresolved WA names to `aliases.json`

For every WA item shown this round whose chat label is a name (not a `group:` label) AND NOT already a key in `wa-daemon/aliases.json` AND NOT in `contacts.json`: append `<chat label>: <chatJid>` to `aliases.json`. This makes future `wa <Name> ...` requests succeed without `--jid` lookup.

Skip this for chats whose label is `unknown (lid <last6>)` — those have no useful name yet.

### 7d. Update the shared cutoff and clear SNM's surfaced list

**a) Advance the cutoff in `~/triage/state.json`.** Write `last_check_at` (ISO), `last_check_unix` (seconds), and `last_check_kind: "triage"`. ALSO write the legacy aliases `last_triage_at` + `last_triage_unix` so older code paths still work. Use the timestamp of step 2's fetch (WA `generated_at` if present, else `Date.now()`).

**b) Clear `~/.claude/snm-receiver/state.json#actionable`.** Every item from the SNM surfaced list was either:
- explicitly handled this round (`done`, `reply: ...`, `suggest` followed by send), or
- preserved by user (`todo` / `defer` → moved to per-channel todo state), or
- silently auto-done (per rule B in `[[feedback_triage_workflow_v3]]`).

Either way, the items shouldn't sit in SNM's list anymore. Rewrite `~/.claude/snm-receiver/state.json` with `actionable: []`, `actionable_count: 0`, `urgent_count: 0`, `scanned_at: <round end ISO>`, preserve `paused` and `errors` fields. Next 20-min SNM scan starts from the freshly-advanced cutoff.

This is the critical step that prevents items from re-appearing across `/triage` rounds when SNM has been running in between.

### 7e. Record behavioural observations

Append any NEW pattern worth remembering to `~/task-land/agent-notes.md` under `## Patterns observed`, dated and tagged `(triage)`. Record only SIGNAL that should change future prioritisation:
- A sender/group skipped across multiple rounds (candidate for mute).
- A contact whose suggested replies you always rewrite (recalibrate draft tone for them).
- An item deferred repeatedly (recurring obligation).
- Notably careful or slow handling of a particular person/thread.

One line per observation. Skip entirely if nothing notable happened this round. Do NOT compact here — `/daily` owns compaction of this file (collapses `## Patterns observed` past ~30 lines); triage only appends.

**Promotion to a permanent rule.** If `~/task-land/agent-notes.md` shows a STABLE skip pattern — the same message TYPE skipped across ≥3 rounds (e.g. "always skips booking-confirmation emails") — propose promoting it: ask the user once, "Make 'skip <type>' a permanent rule?" On yes, append a one-line rule to `~/triage/skip-rules.md` under `## Always skip (message types)` and drop the now-committed observation from agent-notes. This is the hand-off: agent-notes = hypotheses, skip-rules.md = committed policy. Never auto-promote without the user's ok.

### 8. Summary

One line at the end:
```
✓ N done · M deferred · K drafted-and-sent · J suggested · L implicit-done
```

(`L implicit-done` = items in the displayed list that the user didn't mention; counted separately from explicit `done` so the user can spot if too much is being auto-cleared.)

## Common command examples

**Search carryover — both action AND todo (cdtm):**
```
gmail.cmd search --account cdtm --query "(label:triage/action OR label:triage/todo) -label:triage/done"
```

**Get a thread for context:**
```
gmail.cmd get --account cdtm --thread-id 19df...
```

**Mark a thread done (remove action, add done, remove UNREAD):**
```
gmail.cmd label --account cdtm --thread-id 19df... --remove Label_1,UNREAD --add Label_3
```

**Defer (action → todo):**
```
gmail.cmd label --account cdtm --thread-id 19df... --remove Label_1 --add Label_2
```

**Threaded reply — single-line body** (`draft` auto-fetches parent, builds multipart MIME with auto-quote, sets In-Reply-To + References):
```
gmail.cmd draft --account cdtm --to person@x.com --subject "Re: ..." --body "single line ok" --thread-id 19df...
gmail.cmd send-draft --account cdtm --draft-id r-12345... --confirmed
```

For **multi-line bodies**, do NOT use the .cmd shim with `--body` (cmd.exe truncates at first newline). Use `--body-file <path>` or call `python.exe` directly with `gmail.py`. Pattern + root cause: [[reference_triage_operations]] section 4.

**WA debrief read:**
```
node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\triage.js --hours 24 --json
# Inspect generated_at for staleness, then iterate chats[]
```

**WA reply (dry-run + content-aware confirm + send) — ALWAYS use `--jid` from the bundle, NEVER `--to "<name>"`:**
```
node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\send.js --jid "393331234567@s.whatsapp.net" --text "ready when you are"
# Shows JID + body. User says "send it".
node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\send.js --jid "393331234567@s.whatsapp.net" --text "ready when you are" --confirmed
```
`--to "<name>"` is reserved for ad-hoc sends OUTSIDE /triage where there's no JID context (e.g. `/wa send Marco "..."`). Inside /triage, the bundle hands you the JID — skip the name-resolution detour.

**WA mute a group** (only `add` is used inside triage; `list`/`remove` admin via `~/.claude/skills/wa/SKILL.md`):
```
node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\wa-mute.js add <chat-jid> --reason "too noisy"
```

## WA state file (`wa-daemon\wa-state.json`)

Schema:
```json
{
  "todo": {
    "393331234567@s.whatsapp.net": {
      "lastInTimestamp": 1778165511000,
      "addedAt": "2026-05-08T14:23:00Z",
      "summary": "got the report ready, send when?"
    }
  },
  "done": {
    "120363042xxxxxx@g.us": {
      "lastInTimestamp": 1778165505000,
      "doneAt": "2026-05-08T14:25:00Z"
    }
  }
}
```

Auto-cleanup runs at the start of every triage (step 4):
- Drop `done[chatId]` if `currentChat.lastIn.timestamp > entry.lastInTimestamp` (newer incoming → item re-surfaces).
- Drop `todo[chatId]` if `currentChat.repliedSinceLastIncoming === true` (user replied via phone → resolved itself).

This keeps the file small and reflective of current state.

## Edge cases

- **Gmail auth expired:** error from gmail.py mentions token. Tell user to re-run `gmail.cmd auth --account <acct>`. Exit.
- **WA daemon stale (>30 min):** warn `WA daemon may be disconnected — last update <X min ago` and proceed Gmail-only for this run. Don't fail the whole debrief.
- **WA daemon never started in this session:** same handling — proceed Gmail-only, mention staleness in output.
- **Zero items in either query for both accounts AND zero WA actionable:** `Inbox clear ✓`.
- **User says "I already handled X" in freeform:** treat as `done` for that item.
- **User scopes to one channel** ("triage gmail" / "triage wa") or one account ("triage cdtm"): only run those.
- **WA group with no resolvable name:** `triage.js` falls back to a placeholder like `group <last6>`. Display as-is; user can `mute` it if it stays noisy.
- **WA item where contact name is unresolved (`unknown (lid <last6>)`)**: display as-is. If user wants to reply, they'll need to either tag the LID first via `node wa-daemon\lid-tag.js <lid> "Name"`, add a manual entry to `wa-daemon\aliases.json`, or pass a phone number directly to `wa-daemon\send.js`.

## Manual fallback (legacy — only if `fetch-all.js` is broken)

The wrapper at `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\fetch-all.js` replaces all of step 1+2. If it fails or is missing, run these by hand IN PARALLEL (all 7 + state reads), then apply the 24h post-filter yourself per Rule 7:

1. State reads (Read tool, parallel):
   - `C:\Users\Alessandro\triage\state.json` → `last_check_unix`
   - `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\snm-receiver\state.json` → SNM carryover
   - `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\wa-state.json` → WA state
2. Compute `effective_cutoff_unix = max(last_check_unix, now_unix - 86400)` and `hours_since_last = min(24, ceil((now - last_check) / 3600))`.
3. Resolve labels: `gmail.cmd list-labels --account cdtm` (find `triage/action|todo|done` IDs).
4. Gmail × the triage accounts (cdtm; lobbly retired 4 Oct 2026):
   - `gmail.cmd search --account <acct> --query "label:triage/todo -label:triage/done"` (carryover)
   - `gmail.cmd search --account <acct> --query "is:unread after:<effective_cutoff_unix> -label:triage/todo -label:triage/done"` (fresh)
5. WA: `node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\triage.js --hours <hours_since_last> --json` AND `node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\check-flap.js`. Inspect `daemon_pid_alive` + `daemon_heartbeat_age_sec`. Do NOT use `generated_at` for staleness — it always reads fresh and lied during the 2026-05-11 34-hour outage.
6. Slack × 2: `slack.cmd unread --workspace cdtm --hours <hours_since_last>` and same for `xplore`.
7. **24h post-filter (mandatory — Rule 7):**
   - WA: drop `items` where `lastIn.timestamp < effective_cutoff_unix`.
   - Gmail: for each fresh thread, run `gmail.cmd get --thread-id <id>` to fetch headers, find the latest non-SENT message's `internalDate / 1000`, drop if `< effective_cutoff_unix`. Gmail's `after:N` filters by thread internalDate (latest msg including SENT/bumps) — it WILL return threads whose latest incoming is older.
   - Slack: drop messages where `ts < effective_cutoff_unix`.
8. Per-group WA bursts: for each group item with `count > 1`, `node C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\wa-daemon\show-thread.js --jid <jid> --limit <count>` to read the full burst before classifying.

This is ~15+ shell calls vs. the wrapper's 1. Use only as a fallback.

# trippy-old — multi-modal travel search (SUPERSEDED)

> **Naming (locked 2026-08-03).** This skill was called `trippy` until 2026-08-03. It is now
> `/trippy-old`. The current, up-to-date travel skill is `/trippy` (formerly `trippy-v2`) —
> persistent trip boards, context duels, preference wiki. **Route new travel work there.**
> This file remains because the shared helper at `travel-search\` (adapters, `search.py`,
> `render_html.py`, the momondo/skiplagged anti-bot map) is still used, and because the
> `cooked trips\` HTML artifacts it produced are still on disk.

Wraps the helper at `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\` so the user can describe trips in plain language. You parse the request, do the geography reasoning, run `search.py` across all relevant modes, and present a tight ranked summary that accounts for the user's discount cards / subscriptions.

## Philosophy — search EVERYTHING, not the obvious

The user invokes trippy precisely because they don't want to think narrowly. If they wanted the cheapest direct flight to the closest airport, they'd use Google Flights themselves in 30 seconds. Trippy's value is **breadth + last-mile honesty**:

1. **Multi-airport on both ends.** Termoli has no airport, so search PSR (closest, 80 km) AND NAP / BRI / FCO (250 km) AND even MIL / ROM major hubs — sometimes a cheap MUC→MIL flight + 8 h Frecciarossa beats a direct PSR flight. List every reasonable option, then rank by *total* cost+time.
2. **Multi-modal.** Always consider, when applicable: flight, ICE/Intercity day train, night train (EN/NJ/ICN), Flixbus, BlaBlaBus, BlaBlaCar rideshare, ferry (for islands), and combinations. Stub adapters are silently dropped — say so out loud rather than pretending coverage is complete.
3. **Multi-leg auto-assembly.** When the literal origin or destination is a small town (Termoli, Asti, Santa Lucia di Nuoro), break the journey into legs: ground transport to the nearest transit hub → main carrier → ground transport from arrival hub to the final point. Compute the *door-to-door* total, not just the carrier price.
4. **Apply the user's discount stack silently** (see `user_profile.json`): age 24 + Deutschland-Ticket + Carte Avantage Jeune SNCF + MegaVolotea. Don't ask, just use.
5. **Take time.** The user expects trippy to be slow and thorough. A 5-minute search that surfaces 30 well-evaluated options across modes is the point. Don't shortcut to one site, one airport, one mode.

The skill *will* fall short of the philosophy until every adapter and a multi-leg assembler are implemented — but the gap should be visible to the user, not hidden.

## Workflow: two-pass depth (broad sweep → deep dive on chosen config)

**Pass 1 — broad sweep**: parse + run all relevant site adapters, aggregate, present picks per origin/destination/date. For *ancillary* transit < €20 (airport bus, S-Bahn, Alibus etc.), do NOT drive booking forms — quick-estimate from knowledge with `(est)` label. The user picks one or two configurations from the picks-table.

**Pass 2 — deep dive on selected config(s)**: once user names a specific itinerary, drive booking forms / website search to surface *exact* values:
- Train segments: open Trenitalia / Italo / SBB / DB website, fill the form for the specific date+time window, surface exact departure time + price (NOT just an estimate).
- City-airport transit: same — actual Alibus / SADEM / Orio Shuttle schedule + price.
- Validate the constraint "arrive ≥ 1h30 before flight" (or whatever buffer the user specified).

## Workflow: aggregator vs airline-direct

**Rule:** aggregators (momondo, skiplagged, kayak) are **discovery tools** — they're great for finding *which* airline flies the route and the broad price band. They are **not** the source of truth for the final price.

Once we already know we want a specific airline's specific flight (e.g. "Volotea OLB→TRN 18:30 on Aug 21"), **always re-verify on the airline's own website**:
- Aggregators add booking-fee markups and lag the real fare by a few hours.
- The airline's site applies loyalty discounts the aggregator can't see (MegaVolotea, ITA Volare, Lufthansa Miles & More, Ryanair myRyanair).
- The airline's site is where the user will eventually pay anyway — checking there avoids a price-jump surprise at the booking step.

**How to apply:**
- In Pass 1 (discovery): aggregator is fine.
- In Pass 2 (chosen flight): immediately switch to airline-direct. Drive the airline's search form for the exact route+date, click the same flight, capture the *real* fare + the *real* bag adder, then update the JSON.
- The airline-direct number is what goes into the cooked-trips HTML — not the aggregator number.
- Exception: if the airline-direct site is broken / blocking / requires login the user doesn't have, fall back to the aggregator and explicitly flag "fare from momondo, airline-direct unverified".

## Workflow: carry-on / checked luggage deep-dive

**Trigger:** user mentions wanting to bring a bag.

The headline flight price almost never includes a cabin bag larger than a personal item. So **never** trust the published price for bag-carrying users. Drive the airline's booking funnel through to the "add extras / select bag" step (typically: search → pick flight → fare-tier or extras page → passenger data → payment). Screenshot that page. From there report:

1. The **cheapest single-bag add-on** for what the user actually needs (cabin 55×40×20 vs checked). Don't pad with bundles.
2. Any **fare-tier bundles** that include the bag *plus* stuff the user doesn't want (priority boarding, allocated seat, refund flexibility). Note them but flag the surplus.
3. The **standalone "checked bag"** price if it ends up cheaper than the "cabin bag" upgrade.

Cross-airline cheat sheet (Aug 2026, can drift):
- **Ryanair**: small under-seat free. "Priority & 2 Cabin Bags" ~€10-35 (cabin). Checked 10 kg ~€20-30, 20 kg ~€30-50.
- **easyJet**: small under-seat free. "Hands On 15 kg" cabin upgrade ~€8-25. Checked from €10 (varies).
- **Wizz Air**: small under-seat free. Cabin 10 kg ~€8-30. Checked 20 kg ~€15-50.
- **Volotea (with MegaVolotea sub)**: cabin bag usually included; deep-dive confirms.
- **ITA Airways / Lufthansa / KLM**: legacy carriers — cabin bag included on most fares; deep-dive only for the cheapest Economy Light tariffs.

Present the result as the *real* total: `headline flight + cheapest bag add-on = €X`. That's what goes into the cross-leg cost summary.

**CRITICAL: ignore the fare-tier upsell modal.** Ryanair, Wizz, easyJet show a "REGULAR includes bag + priority + seat" modal right after flight selection. That modal price (~€20-25 typical) is a BUNDLE that includes stuff the user doesn't want. The real cabin-bag-only price is on the Extras page, AFTER passenger data entry, where it's sold as standalone "Priority & 2 Cabin Bags" for ~€10-15 (usually €5-15 cheaper than the bundle). Always click "Continua con Basic" through the upsell modal, drive past passenger entry, capture the standalone bag price on Extras. Don't stop early.

## Workflow: final deep dive — never estimate, leave checkout pages open

Once the user picks a specific configuration, the deep-dive output must be:

1. **All prices real, no estimates.** Iterate Playwright scrapes until you have actual numbers from each provider. If something blocks, fix the script — don't punt with "typically €X".
2. **One open Comet tab per purchasable leg, parked at the checkout step.** Drive the booking flow (flight, train, ferry) past fare-selection, past passenger-data (filled with real identity Alessandro Sodano / `alesoda2002@gmail.com`), into the "select payment method / review order" screen. Then STOP — do not submit payment. The user opens the tab and finalizes.
3. **A live HTML artifact in OneDrive `cooked trips\`** that summarizes the deep-dive with deep-links to each booking step plus the real totals. Update this file at every iteration that changes the trip data.

The HTML serves as the human-readable index; the Comet tabs are the live payment-ready surface. Together they let the user finish the trip in ~5 clicks per leg.

## Workflow: script discipline

One driver script per provider with subcommands (e.g. `bahn_drive.py {search|select|extras|checkout}`). Before writing a new script, `Glob **/*<provider>*.py` and extend an existing one. Never create sibling files like `_v2`, `_fix`, `_step2`.

Validate before run: `python -m py_compile <file>` OR Read it back, before invoking.

## Workflow: date verification

Every schedule/price URL must carry the requested date as a parameter (`rideDate=DD.MM.YYYY`, `?date=DD-MM-YYYY`, `dateOut=YYYY-MM-DD`, etc.). After fetch, confirm the response is for the asked date; if it falls back to "today" or a different date, that is a NEGATIVE result.

If the asked date is outside the operator's booking window (FlixBus ~90d, airlines 11-12mo, trains 2-4mo), say so and offer the nearest-available structural read labeled as a proxy. Never quote a different date's prices as if they applied to the asked one.

## Workflow: cooked-trips HTML artifact

Every trippy trip lives as a JSON spec at `~/.claude/travel-search/cooked/<slug>.json` and a rendered HTML at `<OneDrive>\cooked trips\<slug>.html` (path: `C:\Users\Alessandro\OneDrive - HEC Paris\cooked trips\`). The renderer is `render_html.py`. Slug = `<origin>-<dest>-<YYYY-MM>` lowercased with hyphens.

At every relevant iteration (broad-sweep result, config picked, deep-dive bag price found, exact train times found), update the JSON and re-render. The HTML must contain per-leg booking deep-links and a clear "last updated" timestamp.

### CRITICAL: booking links MUST be DURABLE (no session tokens) — locked 2026-06-09

The HTML is reopened days later, so a link is worthless unless it still resolves with **zero session state**. NEVER store an aggregator's per-result redirect URL: momondo `/book/flight?code=…&cookieOverrides=…&viewId=…`, skiplagged redirect URLs, kayak `/book` tokens. These embed a live-search `viewId`/`code` that dies in minutes-to-hours → every link is dead on the next open (the recurring "links never work afterwards" bug). The "airline link inside the momondo result" is ALSO a momondo redirect token — scraping it does NOT make it durable.

A durable link carries the flight's own identity (route + date), not a session, and regenerates a live search every open. Use, in order of preference:
1. **Airline-direct search deep-link** for a flight on a single operating carrier. Verified durable for United: `https://www.united.com/en/us/fsr/choose-flights?f=<ORIG>&t=<DEST>&d=<YYYY-MM-DD>&tt=1&sc=7&px=1&taxng=1&newHP=True&clm=7&st=bestmatches&tqp=R` (opened cold in a fresh no-cookie profile → lands on the right results). Build the equivalent per airline as templates are confirmed.
2. **Google Flights route+date deep-link** for multi-stop / multi-carrier connections (no single airline can reconstruct them): `https://www.google.com/travel/flights?q=Flights%20one%20way%20from%20<ORIG>%20to%20<DEST>%20on%20<YYYY-MM-DD>&curr=EUR&hl=en`. Durable, doesn't bot-block normal clicks.

Always cold-test a new link template once in a throwaway profile before trusting it. Repair tool for an existing HTML full of dead momondo links: `fix_html_links.py` (swaps momondo `/book` → Google Flights route+date, keeps airline-direct links, writes a `.bak`).

JSON schema is documented at the top of `render_html.py`. Re-render: `python render_html.py --json cooked/<slug>.json`.

## Tool location

- Helper dir: `C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\`
- Orchestrator: `search.py`
- Prime: `prime.py <site>` (one-off cookie/captcha clear per adapter; user logs into the site here if needed)
- **First-run setup**: `setup_comet.py` (dismiss Comet onboarding once)
- State: `state/primed.json` (which adapters are usable)
- **User profile**: `user_profile.json` — discounts, age, subscriptions — adapters apply these automatically
- Reports: `reports/trip-<timestamp>.{json,md}`
- **Reaper**: `reap.py` — kills stale automation browsers (see preflight below)

## Browser

### MANDATORY preflight + teardown — reap stale browsers (locked 2026-08-02)

Site drivers launch Chrome/Comet **detached** on a fixed CDP port and deliberately never
close them, so login state survives between invocations. That is correct and must stay.
What it lacks is any reaping once a run is over: on 2026-08-02 three orphaned trees
(`profile-comet`, `profile-rail-chrome`, `profile-wA` — 37 processes, 1.1 GB) had been up
**30 hours** with every parent process already dead.

**Run this FIRST, before any sweep:**

```
C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\reap.py --kill
```

Age-based (default 6h), so it never kills a browser a live run is using. Drop `--kill` to
dry-run. `--all` ignores the age threshold; `--profile profile-wA` restricts to one.

**At the end of a trip, tear down explicitly** — do not leave browsers for the reaper:
- `paris_rail.py close`
- `trn_termoli_check.py close`
- or `reap.py --kill --all` for everything.

Safety: `reap.py` only matches real browser executables whose `--user-data-dir` resolves
under `travel-search\`. The user's daily-driver Comet runs on the default profile and can
never match. Do not loosen either guard — an earlier version matched on command-line text
alone and killed the launching shell.

### STANDING RULE — Pass-1 sweeps run HEADLESS + PARALLEL (never headed + sequential)

A multi-route / multi-date discovery sweep MUST fan out across parallel workers and stay
invisible. Headed + sequential is a defect, not a safe default — it is slow and pops Chrome
windows on the user's desktop. Locked 2026-06-04 after exactly that mistake.

How (verified 2026-06-04, every claim below tested on real ORD/NYC -> Italy routes):

1. **Parallelism** — `search.py` already supports it: pass a distinct `--profile-dir` per
   worker, and `write_report` stamps a `pid+ms` suffix so concurrent workers never clobber
   reports. A single `profile-chrome/` CANNOT be opened by parallel Chrome instances (profile
   lock), so seed one dir per worker first:
   `python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,r'C:/Users/Alessandro/.claude/travel-search'); from lib import seed_worker_profiles; seed_worker_profiles(4)"`
   -> clones `profile-chrome/` (minus cache) into `profile-w1..wN` (~13 MB each, <1 s for 4).
   Each worker carries the primed Cloudflare/Akamai clearance cookie. Then launch N background
   processes, each its own `--profile-dir profile-wK`, each handling a slice of the dates/origins.
   A FRESH (un-seeded) profile gets bot-challenged -> 0 results. Always seed.

2. **Headless mode is per-SITE, because anti-bot differs:**
   - **momondo** (Akamai): `--headless` works -> Chrome **new-headless**, fully invisible, returns
     results AS LONG AS the profile carries the Akamai cookie (seeded). Verified 38 results headless.
     - **UPDATE 2026-07-27: headless is no longer reliable.** All 3 seeded workers hit the Italian
       anti-bot interstitial on every route headless (0 results), while the SAME seeded profiles
       returned 50 results/route with `--offscreen`. Akamai now appears to fingerprint new-headless
       regardless of cookie age. **Try `--offscreen` first for momondo**; it is just as non-intrusive.
       Do not burn a whole sweep re-confirming headless.
   - **skiplagged** (Cloudflare "Just a moment"): blocks BOTH old and new headless even with a
     valid clearance cookie (verified 0 results twice). It only renders for a real **headed**
     browser. **DECISION (user, 2026-06-04): running skiplagged non-headless is ACCEPTED — it
     is the only way, so do not keep fighting to make it headless.** Default to `--offscreen`
     (headed but window shoved to `-32000,-32000`, off the visible desktop) so a parallel fan-out
     stays non-intrusive; plain on-screen headed is also fine if offscreen ever misbehaves.
     Verified 22 results offscreen.
   - Net: momondo -> `--headless`; skiplagged -> `--offscreen` (or plain headed — both non-headless, accepted).

2a-bis. **`sites/skiplagged.py` has two bugs — use `skiplagged_drive.py` instead** (added 2026-07-27):
   (1) `goto(wait_until="domcontentloaded", 45s)` HANGS indefinitely, not for 45s, when Cloudflare
   holds the socket — that is the real cause of the "crash after 2-3 routes" symptom below. Fix:
   `goto(wait_until="commit")` then POLL for `div.trip` against a wall-clock deadline.
   (2) The time regex `\b\d{1,2}:\d{2}\b` matches NOTHING on skiplagged, which renders `6:00am` —
   `0` followed by `a` is not a word boundary. Every card was silently dropped, so the adapter
   returned 0 while the page was full of results. Use `(\d{1,2}):(\d{2})\s*([ap]m)`.
   `skiplagged_drive.py {probe|scrape|hidden}` implements both fixes; `hidden` searches cities
   BEYOND Chicago and keeps itineraries stopping at the target — the real hidden-city test, which
   a plain from->to search never surfaces.

2a. **skiplagged offscreen workers CRASH after ~2-3 routes in one process** (Chrome dies, then
   `page.goto` hangs forever — observed 3× on 2026-06-05; logs stop mid-route, no chrome orphan
   left). Do NOT batch many routes through one skiplagged process. Run **one search.py process
   per (origin,date) call doing <=2 routes** (`--to "MXP,TRN"`), looped, fresh browser each time.
   Slower per-route (launch overhead) but reliable. momondo does NOT have this problem.

2b. **Sweep speed** — set env `TRIPPY_NO_DEBUG=1` for the whole fan-out. It makes the
   momondo + skiplagged adapters skip the per-route full-page screenshot + HTML dump, which
   otherwise dominate wall-time (~15-20 s/route -> a few seconds). Those artifacts are
   debug-only; parsed results are unaffected. Keep them ON (unset) only when debugging a
   broken adapter. Also: more workers = less wall-time — a sweep of ~44 routes on 2 workers
   is slow; split it across 4-6 seeded workers.

3. **Mechanism** (in `lib.py launch_profile`): `--headless` injects `--headless=new` (NOT
   Playwright's detectable old `--headless`); `--offscreen` adds `--window-position=-32000,-32000`.
   `--profile-dir` workers now launch with system Chrome (not vanilla Chromium, which Cloudflare
   flags harder). Pass-2 checkout (where the user pays) stays HEADED and on-screen — that is the
   ONLY time a visible window is correct.

**Default: system-installed Google Chrome** at `C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe`, with a dedicated automation profile at `profile-chrome/`. ALL adapters use Chrome unless they explicitly opt into Comet. Per global rule [[feedback_browser_chrome_default]]: Chrome is default; Comet only when the task needs the user's daily-driver session.

**Adapters that may opt into Comet (the exception list):**
- `sncf` — login lives in the user's Comet daily-driver session.
- (any future adapter whose authenticated state is only present in Comet)

**Adapters that MUST stay on Chrome** (no exceptions, no "but it would be convenient"):
- `momondo`, `skiplagged`, `flixbus`, `omio` — pure aggregators, no daily-driver login involved.
- `db`, `italo`, `trenitalia`, `blablabus`, `blablacar`, `volotea` — their logins, when needed, are seeded into `profile-chrome/` (or no login required for the search step).

The reason Comet is reserved for SNCF: opening Comet on the user's machine is intrusive (it's their daily browser). Defaulting to Chrome keeps automation invisible to the user's normal workflow. See the prior failure: a momondo search opened a Comet window unexpectedly, contradicting the global rule.

### When Comet IS used — CDP-attach pattern

If an adapter has `use_comet=True`, lib.py uses CDP-attach (subprocess.Popen launches Comet with `--remote-debugging-port=9222` and `--user-data-dir=profile-comet`, then Playwright connects over CDP). Never spawn a fresh Comet via `launch_persistent_context` — it trips on `chrome://perplexity-onboarding/`. See `sncf_check_cdp.py` as the reference implementation.

### When Chrome is used — the default path

lib.py calls `p.chromium.launch_persistent_context(user_data_dir=PROFILE_CHROME_DIR, executable_path=CHROME_EXE, ...)`. Standard pattern, no CDP gymnastics needed. Chrome doesn't have the onboarding-page issue Comet has.

The `profile-chrome/` directory is dedicated to this skill. NEVER point at the user's real Chrome profile (`~/AppData/Local/Google/Chrome/User Data`). Adapters log into airline / aggregator sites against `profile-chrome/`; those cookies persist there only.

### Copy-paste snippets for sub-agents

Every sub-agent dispatched for a flight/train/ferry check or booking funnel must use ONE of these two prologues. Default is the Chrome one. Use the Comet one ONLY for SNCF (or another adapter explicitly opting in).

#### CHROME (default, for momondo/skiplagged/flixbus/omio/easyJet/Volotea/Ryanair/bahn/italo/trenitalia/etc.)

```python
# === Chrome — DEFAULT PROLOGUE FOR EVERY TRIPPY BROWSER SCRIPT ===
from pathlib import Path
from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright

CHROME_EXE = Path(r"C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe")
PROFILE_DIR = Path(r"C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\profile-chrome")

with sync_playwright() as p:
    ctx = p.chromium.launch_persistent_context(
        user_data_dir=str(PROFILE_DIR),
        executable_path=str(CHROME_EXE),
        headless=False,
        viewport={"width": 1366, "height": 850},
        args=[
            "--disable-blink-features=AutomationControlled",
            "--no-default-browser-check",
            "--no-first-run",
        ],
    )
    page = ctx.new_page()
    page.goto("https://www.momondo.it/")
    # ... drive the search / booking ...
```

#### COMET (SNCF only — opt-in, CDP attach)

Comet's `chrome://perplexity-onboarding/` page trips `launch_persistent_context`. Launching outside Playwright + attaching via CDP avoids it.

```python
# === Comet CDP attach — SNCF/DAILY-DRIVER-LOGIN ADAPTERS ONLY ===
import json, subprocess, time, urllib.request
from pathlib import Path
from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright

CDP_PORT = 9222
COMET_EXE = Path(r"C:\Users\Alessandro\AppData\Local\Perplexity\Comet\Application\comet.exe")
PROFILE_DIR = Path(r"C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\profile-comet")

def _comet_alive(port=CDP_PORT):
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}/json/version", timeout=2) as r:
            return json.loads(r.read().decode())
    except Exception:
        return None

def attach_or_launch_comet(start_url: str | None = None):
    if _comet_alive():
        return  # already up; attach
    args = [str(COMET_EXE), f"--remote-debugging-port={CDP_PORT}",
            f"--user-data-dir={PROFILE_DIR}", "--no-first-run", "--no-default-browser-check"]
    if start_url:
        args.append(start_url)
    subprocess.Popen(args)
    for _ in range(120):
        if _comet_alive(): return
        time.sleep(0.5)
    raise RuntimeError(f"Comet did not come up on port {CDP_PORT}")

attach_or_launch_comet("https://www.sncf-connect.com/")
with sync_playwright() as p:
    browser = p.chromium.connect_over_cdp(f"http://127.0.0.1:{CDP_PORT}")
    ctx = browser.contexts[0]
    page = ctx.new_page()
    page.goto("https://www.sncf-connect.com/")
    # ... drive the search / booking ...
```

**Universal don'ts:**
- ❌ `playwright.chromium.launch(headless=True)` — no persistent state, no logins.
- ❌ Opening Comet for non-SNCF adapters. Default is Chrome. The user's daily browser is not a sandbox; opening it during automation is intrusive and was an explicit prior correction.
- ❌ Using `launch_persistent_context` on Comet — trips on perplexity-onboarding (use CDP attach for Comet).
- ❌ Sub-agents picking their own ports (9333, 9444, etc.) — fixed port 9222 for Comet, no port for Chrome.
- ❌ Pointing `--user-data-dir` at the user's real Chrome profile (`~/AppData/Local/Google/Chrome/User Data`). Always `profile-chrome/` (or `profile-comet/` for Comet adapters).
- ❌ Closing the browser window at script end — leave it open so the next agent attaches / so the user can see / pay.

**To log into a site (legacy)**: `python prime.py <site>` — kept for compatibility but prefer the appropriate inline prologue above.

## User profile

`user_profile.json` is the source of truth for personal context. Currently:
- **Age 24** → triggers youth fare on every adapter that supports it (DB 15-26, SNCF, Italo Giovane, etc.)
- **Deutschland-Ticket** (active) → DB can filter to D-Ticket-covered legs
- **Carte Avantage Jeune SNCF** (active) → SNCF caps TGV/IC at €49
- **MegaVolotea** (active) → Volotea direct booking applies member fare

Don't ask the user about discounts — read the profile and apply silently.

## Step 1 — Parse the user's request

Extract these fields. Don't ask for them if you can infer.

| Field | Examples |
|---|---|
| **origin** | "from Munich", "MUC", "I'm in Milan" |
| **destinations** | "to Termoli", "for Asti", "Bologna or Florence" |
| **date** | "May 29", "next Friday", "tomorrow" → ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` |
| **arrive_before** | "arrive before 9pm", "by 21:00" → `HH:MM` |
| **depart_after** | "after 6am", "morning departure", "evening" |
| **max_price_eur** | "under 200€", "max 150" |
| **modes** | "flights only", "no buses", "include night train", default = all working |

Relative dates → resolve using env date. Confirm only if highly ambiguous.

**Lock the objective.** Write the parsed request to `state/objective.txt` before any tool call. Re-read at the start of every later turn. If a later message seems to deviate, STOP and confirm before pivoting.

## Step 2 — Map geography (your job)

For each destination decide:
- Direct airport? → use that IATA (or metro code for MIL/ROM/LON/PAR).
- Small / no airport (Asti, Termoli, Matera) → pick 3-5 nearest commercial airports within ~250km AND the nearest mainline rail station.
- Origin → main airport + main station (e.g. Munich = MUC airport, München Hbf station).
- Cap destinations at 6 IATA codes per flight search to keep runtime sane.

## Step 3 — Decide which sites to run

Check `state/primed.json` to confirm what's primed. Default routing per mode:

| Mode | Default sites |
|---|---|
| Flights | `momondo,skiplagged` |
| Trains | `db,sncf,italo,trenitalia` (whichever are no-longer-stub) |
| Buses | `flixbus,blablabus` |
| Rideshare | `blablacar` |

When the user says "find me everything", use all working adapters. Otherwise infer mode from speech: "drive me" → blablacar; "by train" → trains only.

## Step 4 — Run the search

Single route (one worker):

```powershell
python C:\Users\Alessandro\.claude\travel-search\search.py `
  --from <ORIGIN> --to "<DEST1,DEST2,...>" --date <YYYY-MM-DD> `
  --arrive-before <HH:MM> --depart-after <HH:MM> `
  --sites momondo --headless --profile-dir <...\profile-wK>
```

Multi-route / multi-date sweep — ALWAYS headless + parallel (see the STANDING RULE in the
Browser section). Seed worker profiles once, then launch one background process per worker:
- momondo workers: `--headless --profile-dir profile-wK`  (invisible new-headless)
- skiplagged workers: `--offscreen --profile-dir profile-wK`  (headed off-desktop; Cloudflare blocks headless)
- distinct date/origin slice per worker; they run concurrently and write non-colliding reports.

`--headless`/`--offscreen` flags live on `search.py`; `seed_worker_profiles(n)` lives in `lib.py`.

Default browser: Chrome at `profile-chrome/` (workers are seeded clones of it). Add `--comet` ONLY when running `--sites sncf`. Never default to Comet for momondo/skiplagged/flixbus/omio/etc.

Run via `Bash` with `run_in_background: true`. Tee to `state/sweep_wK_<slug>.log`.

**Wait via Monitor or background notification; never ScheduleWakeup polling loops.** Tail `state/search_<slug>.log` with Monitor, or let the harness notify on completion. One ScheduleWakeup at expected-ETA+30s as a safety net is fine; progressive short-interval polls are not.

Runtime grows linearly per (site x destination).

## Step 5 — Read the report and present

After completion, list `reports/` and read newest `trip-*.json`. Filter + sort:

- Drop entries where `price_eur` is null OR `arrive_local[-5:] >= arrive_before`
- Group by `mode` first (flight, train, bus, rideshare), then by destination, then sorted by price asc
- Top 3-5 per group

Present in chat:

1. **Top picks** — markdown table per mode. Columns: EUR | Operator | Route | Depart→Arrive | Duration | Notes
2. **Recommended** — pick the best balance of (total cost) + (door-to-door time). Factor airport access / station last-mile.
3. **Caveats** — overnight arrivals, parser noise, missing data from stubbed adapters
4. **Files** — paths to JSON + MD report

Single-screen output. Don't dump 50 results.

## Step 6 — Empty results = re-prime

If a primed adapter returns 0 across ALL destinations, cookies are likely expired. Offer to re-prime that one adapter. Don't auto-prime; ask first.

## Step 7 — One-way only (for now)

Round-trip is not implemented. For return, run twice with reversed args. Tell user upfront.

## Working with stubs

Most train/bus adapters return [] today. When asking "find me trains from X to Y", a stub adapter will say `STUB` in the log and return nothing. Mention this to the user as "trains via DB/SNCF aren't wired in yet — flights+bus only for now" rather than silently dropping them.

## Aggregation across engines — normalize codes, trust the query (locked 2026-06-05)

momondo searches by METRO code (NYC->ROM, ->MIL) and labels results `ROM`/`MIL`; skiplagged
searches SPECIFIC airports (JFK->FCO) and labels `FCO`/`MXP`. When you aggregate both:

- **Normalize destinations to a CITY bucket** (ROM/FCO/CIA->Rome; MIL/MXP/LIN/BGY->Milan;
  TRN->Turin) and rank by city. An Italy filter that lists only specific airports silently
  DROPS every momondo metro result — that miss hid a EUR 204 Norse JFK->FCO nonstop while the
  report claimed EUR 343 was cheapest (skiplagged-only).
- **Destination = the QUERIED `to` code**, never guessed from card text. momondo's old parser
  picked 3-letter tokens out of the card and grabbed connection airports (STN/LTN/ATH) and
  airline codes (ITA/KLM), scattering real Rome flights under bogus dests. Fixed in momondo.py.
- **Sanity check:** if one engine contributes ~0 rows to a destination it clearly serves, that's
  a code-mismatch filter bug, not a real absence. The cross-engine "cheapest" must include BOTH.
- **momondo can't do small US origins.** Its deep-link returns the "non abbiamo trovato voli /
  seleziona le date" empty-state for non-hub origins (MKE Milwaukee, MSN Madison, MDW Midway) on
  BOTH metro and specific-airport destinations (verified 0 every time 2026-06-05). skiplagged
  handles them fine. So: small/regional US origin -> use skiplagged. Never report "no flights"
  from a momondo-only result for a small origin; cross-check skiplagged first.

## Notes for the agent

- **Don't expose `CITY_TO_AIRPORTS` to the user.** The skill always passes IATA directly so adapters don't have to guess.
- **Don't dump full JSON in chat.** Top 5-7 picks max + file paths.
- **Don't run primes the user didn't ask for.** Only on empty results, and confirm first.
- **Don't claim a site is "down" without evidence.** 0 results almost always = cookies expired.
- **For UX consistency:** parse → run → present → optional follow-up. No multi-step interrogation.
- **Apply user_profile silently.** Don't ask about discounts; just use them.
